<a id="m10-r13"></a>
### M10-R13 — the digit-count gate rejects numbers `is_valid()` accepts, so M10-R2's fix re-creates M10-R1's truncation · **leak (partial) / recall regression**

**Repro** (real binary, mock upstream, `PII_LOCALES` unset — the shipped default):

```text
in    A 39 3332 2673 8858 fine          → upstream  A [PHONE_1] 8858 fine
in    B 393 210 1162 7893 fine          → upstream  B [PHONE_1] 7893 fine
in    C 128 0590 2773 0300 fine         → upstream  C 128 [PHONE_1] fine
in    D 114 2147 3430 9636 fine         → upstream  D 114 2147 3430 9636 fine   ← nothing masked
in    I 860 2473 6290 671 fine          → upstream  I 860 2473 6290 671 fine    ← nothing masked
```

Every one of those five is a number **this product's own validator calls real**:
`national_phone_valid(IT, "39 3332 2673 8858")` → `+39 333226738858`, `is_valid() == true`;
`national_phone_valid(CN, "114 2147 3430 9636")` → `+86 4734309636`, `true`. On the **pre-fix**
tree (`def16a6`) all five were masked **in full**. The round-trip is exact either way, so nothing
looks wrong at the client — the proxy simply forwarded four subscriber digits, or all of them.

**Mechanism.** `plausible_digit_counts` (`recognizers.rs:665`) builds its mask from each region's
`possible_length` ∪ `possible_local_length`, **plus one for a single trunk character**. But
`phonenumber::parse(Some(region), …)` strips more than a trunk prefix: it also strips the region's
**international prefix** (`00`) and its **bare country calling code** when doing so yields a valid
national number — which is exactly why M8.1 found `0049301234` validating as DE. So the candidate's
digit count can exceed the national number's by 1–3 (bare CC) or 3–5 (`00` + CC), and the mask has
no bit for any of it. Measured masks (`Groups` family, the regions that declare it):

| region | mask bits (digit counts the gate allows) |
|---|---|
| es · pt | 9 |
| lv | 8 |
| it | 6–12 |
| cn | 5–13 |
| **`Groups` family union** | **5–13** |

The `Groups` regex tops out at **15** digits (`[1-9]\d{1,2}(?:[ -]\d{2,4}){1,3}`). So every 14- or
15-digit candidate is rejected at `recognizers.rs:621-624` before `.any()` runs. Then
`shrink_to_a_valid_prefix` cuts one group off the right and re-asks — and *that* is what turns a
missed candidate into the M10-R1 shape: the accepted prefix is masked and the remaining group is
forwarded in clear.

**Measured size of the class** (probe crate, 400 K generated candidates per length, validator as
ground truth):

| candidate length | generated | validator-accepted | **partially** masked | **not masked at all** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 14 digits | 400,000 | 1,015 | 686 | 329 |
| 15 digits | 400,000 | 3,508 | 554 | 2,954 |

**Recall in the gated length band is 0** — 100 % of validator-accepted candidates are under-masked,
two thirds of the 14-digit ones by truncation. A 1.2 M-candidate differential sweep across all four
families found the exposure is exactly this band: `Trunk` 0 gate-rejections of 257,365 accepted
candidates, `TrunkPairs` 0 of 250,019, `LongBlock` 0 of 29,509 (their maxima are 13, 10 and 11
digits, inside their masks). It is `Groups` alone, and only above 13 digits.

**Why the guard is blind, and this is the part worth keeping.** PHONE-NAT-10
(`recognizers.rs:2205`) is named *"the length gate never rejects a number a region accepts"* and
TESTING.md describes it as checking the derivation end to end. What it actually does is assert
`mask & (1 << digits) != 0` for **30 hand-written literals** — every one of them a corpus positive,
i.e. a domestic rendering, i.e. **≤ 13 digits by construction**. It asserts the property on the
only inputs where the property cannot fail. That is [M4-R17](M4.md#m4-r17)'s lesson once more: *an
invariant is only ever as strong as the set it quantifies over*, and a hand-listed set quantifies
over what the author already believed.

**Fix.** Two parts, and the second is the durable one.

1. **Widen the mask to what `parse` can strip.** For each region OR in `length`, `length + cc`,
   and `length + cc + intl` (the country calling code's digit count and the international
   prefix's), not just `length + 1`. Cheaper and blunter: leave the gate as a *lower* bound and
   stop rejecting anything longer than the shortest plausible length — the DoS win is unlikely to
   depend on the upper edge, because the adversarial inputs are **periodic** and the per-scan memo
   already collapses them to a constant number of parses. **Measure that with DOS-05 before
   choosing**; it now exists and would catch a regression in seconds.
2. **Make PHONE-NAT-10 quantify over generated candidates, not literals.** For each shape family,
   generate candidates from its own regex and assert `gate(s) || !any(is_valid(s))`. That is a
   dozen lines, it fails on today's code within a few thousand samples, and it is the only form of
   the assertion that can survive a metadata update.

*(Unrelated nit, same expression: `recognizers.rs:622` guards with `usize::BITS` while shifting a
`u64` — `u64::BITS` is meant, as used at line 703. Unreachable today at ≤ 15 digits.)*

**The rule to promote once fixed** ([ARCHITECTURE](../ARCHITECTURE.md) → *Domestic phone
coverage*, next to the paragraph that currently states the superset property): *a length gate in
front of a validator must be derived from what the **validator** accepts, not from what the
**metadata** describes — `parse` normalizes before it validates, so the two are not the same
alphabet. And an optimisation in front of a privacy decision must be proved by a differential test
against the thing it is optimising, never by a list of inputs the author expected it to allow.*

<a id="m10-r14"></a>
### M10-R14 — `docs/SETUP.md` still documents the pre-M10 world, including "an Italian domestic number is not masked" · **docs**

`docs/SETUP.md` was **not touched by M10** — not by the feature commits, not by the round-1
closures (`git diff 9e31f36..HEAD -- docs/SETUP.md` is empty). Its *PII locales* section
(lines 192–206) now states four things that are false:

> `PII_LOCALES` (comma-separated, default `it,us`) … `fp_prone_recognizers`
> (`src/pii/recognizers.rs`) matches **`gb` and `de`** … Every other code, **including the default
> `it` and `us`, contributes nothing** … **with the shipped default, an Italian domestic number is
> not masked** — [M10](ROADMAP.md#m10) is open to close that.

The default is all nine regions; `fp_prone_recognizers` **no longer exists** (it is
`national_phone_recognizers`); `it` resolves to a real recognizer; and the shipped default masks
`06 69821234`, which I confirmed through the binary. ROADMAP step 9 asked for exactly this
sweep — *"the README's `PII_LOCALES` rows and the `it,us`-is-inert warnings come out only once the
code makes them false"* — and both READMEs and `--help` were updated. SETUP.md was missed, and it
is one of the four files `CLAUDE.md` tells the next contributor to read first.

**Fix.** Rewrite the two paragraphs from the shipped behaviour: default = the nine vetted regions,
setting `PII_LOCALES` **replaces** that set, `PII_LOCALES=` (empty) switches the tier off, and drop
the "M10 is open" sentence. Point at ARCHITECTURE → *Domestic phone coverage* for the matrix.

**Test that would have caught it.** Nothing automated is proposed — but the same `grep` the
milestone owed itself: after a default changes, search every doc for the **old value** (`it,us`)
and for the name of any deleted symbol. `fp_prone_recognizers` appears in no source file and in
one doc.

<a id="m10-r15"></a>
### M10-R15 — `shrink_to_a_valid_prefix`'s doc says it *is* applied to the trunk families; the code and the field it reads say the opposite · **docs**

`recognizers.rs:866-868`, the closing paragraph of the function the M10-R1 fix added:

> Cutting at separators rather than one byte at a time is not just an optimisation … **Applied to
> the trunk families too, where it is strictly more protective** — it resolves in one pass what the
> fixpoint used to resolve in two, and it can only ever accept what `is_valid` accepts.

It is not. `shrink_on_reject: matches!(shape, Groups | LongBlock)` (`recognizers.rs:640`), and the
function's own first statement is `if !self.shrink_on_reject { return None; }`. Two hundred lines
up, the field's comment explains the asymmetry at length and calls it *"the point (M10-R1)"*,
with the measurement that justifies it (`020 7946 0958 0161 496 0000` collapsing to one span).

So the two comments that a reader consults when deciding whether to extend the shrink say opposite
things, and the one physically attached to the function — the one you land on from a stack trace or
a `go to definition` — says the asymmetry does not exist. It is also the paragraph most likely to
be believed, because it carries a reason. This is a stale sentence from an earlier draft of the fix
(the closure note records that the uniform version was tried, measured and rejected), and it is
precisely the kind of leftover [M4-R18](M4.md#m4-r18) was about.

**Fix.** Replace the last sentence with the truth and a pointer: *"Un-anchored families only — see
[`Recognizer::shrink_on_reject`] for why the trunk families must not have it."*

<a id="m10-r16"></a>
### M10-R16 — the same latency measurement is published as two different numbers, and the one in ARCHITECTURE is the one that does not reproduce · **measurement / docs**

| file | figure |
|---|---|
| `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:155` | *"~0.55 ms/turn with none enabled, ~0.57 ms with all nine"* |
| `docs/DEVLOG.md:98` | *"0.30 ms with no region enabled, 0.31–0.32 ms with all nine"* |
| `docs/ROADMAP.md:1571` | *"latency flat (0.30 → 0.32 ms/turn for 0 → 9 regions)"* |

Same test, same fixture, same command. My re-run
(`cargo test --release --test phone_eval -- --ignored --nocapture --test-threads=1`) prints
**0.30 ms at 0 regions and 0.32 ms at 9** — the DEVLOG/ROADMAP pair, exactly. ARCHITECTURE's
figures were introduced by the round-1 doc rewrite, with the honest disclosure that *"repeat runs
moved the absolute by ~1.8×"*, which is where 0.32 × 1.8 ≈ 0.57 comes from.

Choosing the slower observation is the conservative direction and the opposite of
[M7-R12](M7.md#m7-r12)'s sin, so the *number* is defensible. What is not is that the project's
**design** document and its **history** now report a measurement two ways with no cross-reference,
so a reader who checks one against the other finds a 1.8× discrepancy and no way to tell which
was measured. M7-R12's whole lesson was that an unreproducible published figure costs more than
a slower honest one.

**Fix.** Pick one convention and state it once — either publish the *range* in all three
(`0.30–0.57 ms/turn, flat in the region count`) or publish the fast pair everywhere with the
spread noted. Whichever, the two docs that do not own the number should link the one that does
rather than restate it.

<a id="m10-r17"></a>
### M10-R17 — `every_declared_shape_is_needed_by_a_corpus_case` does not read the corpus · **guard / docs**

The test (`recognizers.rs:1998`) iterates `REGION_RENDERINGS` (`recognizers.rs:2027`) — a
16-entry list literal inside the test module, disjoint from `tests/corpus/pii_cases.json`. Its
name says otherwise, and so do both docs that cite it:

- `PhoneRegion::shapes` (`recognizers.rs:397`): *"fails if a shape is listed that no corpus
  positive for that region requires"*;
- TESTING.md, PHONE-NAT-11, and ARCHITECTURE → *Domestic phone coverage*: *"fails if a row lists a
  shape no rendering of that country needs"* (this one is accurate).

**The guard itself is fine and fails in the safe direction** — a region added with no
`REGION_RENDERINGS` entry makes `differs` false and the assertion fires — and I confirmed it is
non-vacuous for all fifteen (region, shape) pairs. The problem is that it reads as coupled to the
corpus when it is not, so the one obvious way to strengthen it (add renderings to the corpus, where
PHONE-COV already enforces per-region coverage) silently does nothing, and a reviewer checking *"do
Chinese numbers really take this shape?"* goes to the corpus and finds a different list.

**Fix.** Either rename to `every_declared_shape_is_needed_by_a_rendering` and say in the two doc
comments that `REGION_RENDERINGS` is its own evidence set (and why — the corpus cases carry prose,
which would make the differ-check measure the sentence rather than the number), or derive the
renderings from the corpus positives' `entities[].text` and delete the literal. The second makes
the name true and removes a second place to keep in sync.

<a id="m10-r18"></a>
### M10-R18 — M10-R12's closure claims both comments were fixed; the shared module's still points only at the guard that does not run · **low / docs**

The M10-R12 closure ends *"neither comment now points a reader at a guard that does not run"*. The
substance is right — `the_shared_fixture_keeps_its_shape` is in `tests/phone_overmask.rs` and runs
on every default `cargo test`. But the comments are **unchanged**:

- `tests/common/m7_turn.rs:12` — *"`m7_latency.rs` asserts that shape, so it cannot silently
  drift."*
- `tests/m7_latency.rs:54` — *"`realistic_turn`'s shape is asserted below, so the move cannot go
  unnoticed either."*

Both still name only the `#[ignore]`d assertion inside a `#![cfg(feature = "onnx")]` file. A reader
of the shared fixture — the audience the finding was about — is still sent to a guard that needs a
model and an `--ignored` run, and is told that is what protects the fixture.

**Fix.** One sentence in `m7_turn.rs`: *"`phone_overmask.rs` pins the size, the part distribution
and the `Phone`-span set on every default `cargo test`; `m7_latency.rs` carries the full shape
assertion in the `onnx` run."*

<a id="m10-r19"></a>
### M10-R19 — the M10-R10 fix left a new `cargo doc` warning of its own · **low / docs**

M10-R10 was about doc-comment rot going unnoticed at 17 `cargo doc` warnings. Its closure fixed the
two it named, and introduced one more:

```text
warning: redundant explicit link target
   --> src\pii\recognizers.rs:387:19
```

`[`Trunk`](PhoneShape::Trunk)` in the `PhoneRegion::shapes` doc — the link text already resolves,
so the explicit target is redundant. The count is unchanged at 16 (+1 summary line), which is why
it did not stand out.

**Fix.** `[`PhoneShape::Trunk`]`, or `[`Trunk`](PhoneShape::Trunk)` → `[`Trunk`]` with a
`use`-scope alias. Separately, the question M10-R10 raised and the closure explicitly deferred —
whether `cargo doc --no-deps` joins the "green with no warnings" bar — is now the difference
between noticing this in review and not, and 14 of the 16 are one mechanical class (public docs
linking private items).

---

## Round 2 closures (2026-07-29)

All seven closed. Suite green on both feature sets, `fmt` and `clippy -D warnings` clean on each.

<a id="m10-r13-closed"></a>
**M10-R13 — closed by deleting the gate, and the measurement is why.** The finding is exactly
right that a filter in front of a validator must be derived from what the *validator* accepts.
Before rewriting the derivation to cover international prefixes and country codes, I measured what
the gate was buying — with it forced fully open, on the very inputs it was introduced for:

| input | with gate | gate open |
|---|---|---|
| 4 MiB arbitrary digit groups | 382 ms | 384 ms |
| 4 MiB repeated real numbers | 258 ms | 257 ms |
| 781 KiB **distinct** candidates (the case memoization cannot help) | 145 ms | 145 ms |

**Nothing.** The per-scan memoization was already doing all of the work M10-R2 needed. So the gate
is gone rather than repaired: it bought no measurable speed, cost recall, and required a soundness
argument that turned out to be wrong. Both of the finding's repros now mask whole
(`39 3332 2673 8858` → `[PHONE_1]`, `114 2147 3430 9636` → `[PHONE_1]`).

**PHONE-NAT-10 is replaced by the differential guard the finding asked for**, and writing it
proved the finding's point twice more:
- Its first version generated a **four-pair** French form where the family requires five, then
  reported the miss as a detector bug. It now asserts each generated string **matches its family
  whole** before drawing any conclusion from it — the generator has to speak the grammar it claims
  to be sampling.
- Its premise had to become **per family**. "Valid for *any* enabled region" folds in the
  deliberate per-region shape restriction and reports it as a miss: a 14-digit un-anchored run that
  only Germany's plan accepts is *deliberately* not offered to Germany.

The rule is promoted to [ARCHITECTURE](../ARCHITECTURE.md) → *Domestic phone coverage*, together
with the reason the old guard could not fail: **an assertion made only where it cannot fail is not
an assertion.**

<a id="m10-r14-closed"></a>
**M10-R14 — closed.** `docs/SETUP.md`'s *PII locales* section is rewritten: nine regions on by
default, `PII_LOCALES` replaces rather than adds, empty means off, and the deleted
`fp_prone_recognizers` is gone. The finding's wider point is taken — a status written in a second
place drifts — so the section now **points at** ARCHITECTURE's matrix instead of restating any
numbers of its own.

<a id="m10-r15-closed"></a>
**M10-R15 — closed.** The `shrink_to_a_valid_prefix` doc said it was applied to the trunk families
too; the code says `matches!(shape, Groups | LongBlock)`. The doc now states the restriction **and
the measurement behind it** — shrinking the trunk families bridges `020 7946 0958 0161 496 0000`
into one coalesced span, which is what kept it off them. Same class as M9-R29: prose is part of the
diff, and the sentence that survived a mechanism change was unreviewed.

<a id="m10-r16-closed"></a>
**M10-R16 — closed by re-measuring rather than by picking a side.** Three consecutive runs:
**0.30 ms** with no region enabled, **0.32 ms** with all nine, identical each time. ARCHITECTURE's
0.55/0.57 was the outlier and is corrected; the note now records that a noisy run produced it, so
the next reader knows the spread exists rather than inferring the machine is deterministic.

<a id="m10-r17-closed"></a>
**M10-R17 — closed by renaming to what the test reads**
(`every_declared_shape_is_needed_by_a_real_rendering`), not by making it read the corpus: a unit
test cannot open `tests/corpus/pii_cases.json`, and the honest fix for a name that overclaims is
the name. The doc says where the renderings live, that each is also a corpus positive, and that
keeping the two in step is review's job.

<a id="m10-r18-closed"></a>
**M10-R18 — closed.** Both comments now describe what actually holds: PHONE-OM pins the fixture's
size, shape and span set on every default `cargo test`; the full shape assertion is part of the
`onnx` latency run. Neither points a reader at a guard that does not run.

<a id="m10-r19-closed"></a>
**M10-R19 — closed.** The `PhoneShape::NonTrunk` link is gone with the variant. The broader
question — whether `cargo doc --no-deps` joins the "green with no warnings" bar — is **still not
answered here**, and deliberately: it is a project-wide bar change affecting ~14 pre-existing
warnings in code M10 never touched, and smuggling it into a patch milestone is how a bar gets set
without being decided. Recorded in the ROADMAP backlog instead.
*(Round 3: the deferral is recorded honestly and the backlog entry is good — but the warning this
finding named still fires. [M10-R23](#m10-r23).)*

---

<a id="review-3"></a>
## Round 3 — closure verification (2026-07-29)

**Six of the seven round-2 closures hold; [M10-R19](#m10-r19)'s does not** — it fixed a link the
finding was not about, and `cargo doc --no-deps` still emits the exact warning at the exact line.
The two behavioural closures were attacked directly, as they should have been, and both survive:
**deleting the digit-count gate is defensible on my own measurements**, and **PHONE-NAT-10 really
does catch the M10-R13 defect** — I reinstated the gate in a throwaway worktree and watched the
guard fail in 0.94 s.

**But the milestone's deepest measurement is still wrong, and all three rounds shared the blind
spot.** Every DoS number this milestone has published — M10-R2's original table, its closure, my own
round-2 verification, and M10-R13's "the gate bought nothing" table — was measured on a **periodic**
body (`unit.repeat(n)`). DOS-05 is periodic too. The per-scan memoization that carries the whole
fix is keyed on the matched bytes, so **its benefit is a property of the input's periodicity, not of
the code**. Hold the shape and the size fixed and vary only the number of distinct candidate
strings, and 4 MiB moves from 207 ms to 17.0 s. A legal 15 MiB body of *distinct* digit groups
answers **200 in 64.5 s** at the shipped default. That is [M10-R20](#m10-r20), and it is M10-R2
alive on the one axis nobody varied.

**Seven new findings, and three of them are the same shape as each other:** a guard pinned to inputs
or a configuration where its property cannot fail ([M10-R21](#m10-r21), [M10-R26](#m10-r26)), and a
correction that was applied everywhere except the one file that is supposed to be authoritative
([M10-R22](#m10-r22)). This milestone has now promoted *"an assertion made only where it cannot fail
is not an assertion"* into its own docs and then left two more instances of it standing.

### Verdict per round-2 finding

| ID | Closure | How I checked |
|---|---|---|
| [M10-R13](#m10-r13) | **holds** | All five repros mask **whole** through the real `.exe` (`39 3332 2673 8858` → `[PHONE_1]`, round-trip exact), plus 15 corpus renderings across all four families and a **1,584-case** number×neighbour×prefix sweep: **zero** surviving digits, **zero** round-trip failures. **The deletion is defensible on my own numbers** — see below. The `usize::BITS` nit died with the function. |
| [M10-R14](#m10-r14) | **holds** | `docs/SETUP.md` §*PII locales* rewritten; `fp_prone_recognizers` and `it,us` appear in no live doc. Each claim re-driven through the binary: unset → nine regions, `PII_LOCALES=` → `[]` and nothing masked, `gb` narrows, `it,us` masks the Italian number. It points at ARCHITECTURE's matrix instead of restating it, exactly as the finding asked. |
| [M10-R15](#m10-r15) | **holds** *(the reason it states does not)* | `recognizers.rs:804-810` now says *"Applied to the un-anchored families only"*, and the function doc and the `shrink_on_reject` field doc finally agree — which is exactly what the finding asked for. But the `020 7946 0958 0161 496 0000` measurement both now carry as the *justification* is a `gb`-narrowed one: at the shipped default that string already masks to **one** placeholder. [M10-R26](#m10-r26). |
| [M10-R16](#m10-r16) | **holds** | Re-measured myself: **0.30 ms at 0 regions, 0.32 ms at 9** — ARCHITECTURE, DEVLOG and ROADMAP now all carry that pair, and ARCHITECTURE records that the 0.55/0.57 outlier existed. (My run also produced one 0.38 at 7 regions, i.e. the disclosed spread is real and the flatness still survives it.) |
| [M10-R17](#m10-r17) | **holds** *(one citation missed)* | Renamed to `every_declared_shape_is_needed_by_a_real_rendering`; `recognizers.rs:397` and TESTING.md PHONE-NAT-11 both updated, and the doc now says the renderings are a list in the file rather than the corpus. ARCHITECTURE still names the dead symbol — [M10-R25](#m10-r25). |
| [M10-R18](#m10-r18) | **holds** | Both comments read correctly now, and both name PHONE-OM as what runs by default and `m7_latency.rs` as the `onnx`-only one. Verified `the_shared_fixture_keeps_its_shape` runs in the default build. |
| [M10-R19](#m10-r19) | **does NOT hold** | `cargo doc --no-deps` → **16 warnings**, including `redundant explicit link target --> src\pii\recognizers.rs:387:19`, byte-for-byte the one the finding named. [M10-R23](#m10-r23). The *deferral* half is fine: the backlog entry exists and is honest. |

### The two behavioural closures, attacked

**1 · Deleting the gate — I re-measured it, including a shape built to be worst-case for the
missing filter, and the deletion is defensible.** Two release binaries (HEAD, and HEAD with the gate
re-applied in a detached worktree), driven through the real `.exe` against a mock upstream:

| body (4 MiB unless noted) | no gate | gate | gate's gain |
|---|---|---|---|
| periodic `45 12 33 22 ` (the closure's row 1) | 600 ms | 646 ms | **−8 %** |
| periodic `06 69821234 ` (row 2) | 510 ms | 536 ms | **−5 %** |
| 781 KiB distinct 10-digit `Groups` (row 3) | 3,297 ms | 3,322 ms | **−1 %** |
| **distinct 15-digit `NNN NNNN NNNN NNNN`** — built so *every* candidate is outside the gate's 5–13 mask **and** distinct, i.e. the memo cannot help either | 12,913 ms | 10,516 ms | +19 % |
| **distinct 14-digit `NN NNNN NNNN NNNN`** — same construction | 16,553 ms | 12,076 ms | +27 % |

So the gate's *best possible* case is +19–27 %, and only there: it saves the first of the three
validator calls each such candidate makes (`shrink_to_a_valid_prefix` then re-asks with 11- and
7-digit prefixes, both **inside** the mask, both paying in full). Against that it cost recall
outright in the same band. **A filter that at its very best shaves a quarter off an attack that
still takes ten seconds, and at its worst returns real numbers to the provider in clear, has no
defence.** Deleting rather than repairing was the right call.

> **One row of the closure's table does not reproduce, and it is the load-bearing one.**
> *"781 KiB distinct candidates (the case memoization cannot help) — 145 vs 145 ms."* I could not
> construct any 781 KiB body whose candidates are genuinely distinct that costs 145 ms; the ones I
> built cost **3,297 ms**, 23× that. Whatever that row measured, its candidates were not distinct
> (a body of 4-digit-led groups, say, proposes no `Groups` candidate at all and would run in about
> 145 ms). It matters because that single number is what the closure, the DEVLOG entry and
> ARCHITECTURE all cite as proof that *"the memoization was already doing all of the work"* — and a
> row that actually measured distinct candidates would have exposed [M10-R20](#m10-r20) in the same
> sitting.

**2 · PHONE-NAT-10 — the premise is sound, the pre-check is sound, and the guard genuinely fires;
its *power* is two samples wide.** Taken one question at a time:

- **Is the per-family premise a genuine scoping decision, or a way to make failures disappear?**
  Genuine. `owners` is computed as *the regions that declare this shape*, which is byte-for-byte the
  set the corresponding recognizer's validator closes over (`applicable`, `recognizers.rs:597-603`).
  So the premise mirrors the dispatch exactly: it is not a weakening, it is the contract. A candidate
  valid only for a region that does not declare the family is *deliberately* not offered to it, and
  that decision has its own measurement (ARCHITECTURE's shape table). Asking "valid anywhere" would
  have made the guard fail on the design rather than on defects.
- **Is the "matches its family whole" pre-check sound?** Yes, and importantly it is an `assert!`,
  not a `continue`. A drifting generator **fails the test**; it cannot silently shrink the sample.
  I checked all six generated forms against their patterns by hand — each matches whole, and none is
  reachable by a *different* family that could shadow it.
- **Does the `accepted > 100` floor bind at 400 rounds?** **No — it is 12.9× slack, and it is
  aggregate, which is the real problem.** Instrumented (worktree, HEAD code): 2,400 generated,
  **1,286 accepted**, distributed as `Trunk`-11 **344** · `Trunk`-12 **361** · `TrunkPairs`-10
  **336** · `Groups`-9 **208** · `LongBlock`-11 **35** · **`Groups`-14 → 2**.
- **Would it still catch the R13 defect?** **Yes — verified, not argued.** Gate re-applied in a
  detached worktree: `a_valid_number_is_never_lost_between_the_regex_and_the_validator` **FAILED in
  0.94 s** on `"12 6345 1396 1996" (14 digits) … the detector produced [] — that is a MISS`. Exactly
  the band, exactly the right message.

  But it caught it with **2 of 2,400 samples**, and the floor watches the other 2,398. That is
  [M10-R21](#m10-r21).

### What I verified independently

- **Suite.** `cargo test` — **212 passed / 4 ignored / 0 failed, zero warnings**, 21 test binaries
  plus the 129-test lib, in a shell with **`NO_COLOR` unset** (so CLI-06 is still asserting a
  property of the binary, not of the shell). `cargo fmt --check` clean; `cargo clippy --all-targets
  -- -D warnings` clean; `cargo check --features onnx --all-targets --target-dir target/onnx`
  clean. `cargo doc --no-deps` → **16 warnings** ([M10-R23](#m10-r23)).
- **`phone_eval`, re-run** (`--release --ignored --test-threads=1`). **Every published figure
  reproduces exactly**, per region and for the union: recall **1.000** everywhere, FPcur **0/20**,
  dates **0.180**, tables **0.375**, codes **0.091**, offsets **0.050**, sizes **0.031**,
  ports · money · refs **0.000**, and every cell of ARCHITECTURE's per-region matrix (es tables
  0.188 · it dates 0.080 / tables 0.062 · lv dates 0.120 · pt tables 0.188 / codes 0.091 / offsets
  0.050 · cn tables 0.188 / sizes 0.031). The dispatch invariant is asserted, not reported
  (`union hits 45 == ∪ singles 45`). Pool reachability is printed for all three family groups.
- **The real binary, end to end**, against a mock upstream that reports the exact body it received:
  the five M10-R13 repros, the two M10-R1 repros, 15 corpus renderings, and a **1,584-case**
  adversarial sweep (22 numbers × 12 neighbour tokens × 6 prefixes) — **zero surviving digits, zero
  round-trip failures**. `PII_LOCALES` unset / `''` / `gb` / `it,us` all behave as documented. **No
  raw PII in any log line** at `info`.
- **A gate-reinstated build** (detached worktree at HEAD, gate re-applied by hand, `--release` and
  `--test`), used for the differential table above and for the PHONE-NAT-10 experiment. Worktree
  removed; `git worktree list` shows only the checkout; tree clean.
- **Things I tried and could not break.** The un-anchored shrink cannot start a span mid-value
  (1,584 adversarial embeddings, zero survivors) and cannot leave a proper prefix of a real number
  behind: when the greedy match over-runs, the first cut lands exactly on the real number's last
  group boundary. **The trunk families' mid-value starts do not leak either** — 3,870 cases built
  specifically to make one truncate a neighbour, zero truncations ([M10-R26](#m10-r26) is about the
  *sentence* and the *guard*, not a reachable leak). `mask_all` converged on every input. The memo
  is still call-local and keyed on the matched bytes, so no verdict crosses a request. Every ROADMAP
  `[x]` in the M10 section corresponds to work I could observe in the tree — the *prose* around two
  of them does not ([M10-R22](#m10-r22)) — except the CC battery, which is disclosed as owed.
  `Cargo.toml` is still `1.2.0` against a planned `v1.2.1`, and `release-build-publish.yml` still
  refuses to publish a tag the Status table does not name as cut, so `--version` cannot ship a lie.
- **Fixed here rather than filed.** `docs/reviews/README.md` still said M10 was *"19 — 12 closed, 7
  open (one leak)"* and that `M10-R13` was the open blocker. That paragraph is **my own** round-2
  text, so it is mine to correct, not a finding against the builder. It now reads the round-3 state.
  Second round running that this file has needed correcting — the lesson it keeps teaching is the one
  [M10-R22](#m10-r22) is about: *a status written in a second place drifts, and nobody notices until
  someone acts on it.*

<a id="m10-r20"></a>
### M10-R20 — the memoization's benefit is a property of the input's **periodicity**, so M10-R2's DoS is still reachable: a legal 15 MiB body of *distinct* digit groups answers 200 in 64.5 s · **BLOCKER (availability)**

**Repro** (release build, the real `.exe`, **no environment overrides at all** beyond the upstream
URL — default `MAX_BODY_BYTES` = 16 MiB, default `PII_LOCALES`):

```text
request body: 15.00 MiB   (one messages[0].content of space-separated `NN NNNN NNNN` groups,
                           each one generated independently, so no two candidates are equal)
status 200 in 64.5 s
```

Scaling, and the same body with the domestic-phone tier switched off (`PII_LOCALES=`) as the control:

| body | shipped default | tier off |
|---|---|---|
| distinct digit groups, 0.8 MiB | **3.4 s** | 0.06 s |
| 2 MiB | **8.7 s** | 0.04 s |
| 4 MiB | **17.4 s** | 0.09 s |
| 8 MiB | **34.6 s** | 0.18 s |
| **15 MiB (legal at the shipped default)** | **63.5 s** | 0.30 s |
| *periodic* `45 12 33 22 `, 15 MiB — what every published figure measures | 2.5 s | 0.28 s |
| *periodic* `06 69821234 `, 15 MiB | 1.9 s | 0.29 s |

It is **linear**, so this is not M4-R19's complexity class returning; it is a **~210× constant
factor** over the pre-M10 path, on an unauthenticated endpoint, at a size the proxy is configured to
accept. M10-R2 was accepted as a BLOCKER at 105 s for 12 MiB. This is the same order, and its own
fix's stated criterion — *"the worst-case cost of one legal body must come back into the same order
as the other recognizers'"* — is not met.

**Mechanism, isolated by varying one thing.** Same shape, same 4 MiB, only the number of **distinct
candidate strings** changes:

| distinct strings in the body | ms |
|---|---|
| 1 | **207** |
| 10 | 432 |
| 100 | 498 |
| 1,000 | 1,854 |
| 10,000 | 5,384 |
| 100,000 | 9,564 |
| all (322,639) | **17,049** |

**82×, with the alphabet, the shape and the size held constant.** `push_candidates`
(`recognizers.rs:730`) memoizes on `m.as_str()` for the duration of one scan. A **periodic** body is
the same handful of candidate strings over and over, so the map answers everything after the first
period — which is why 15 MiB of `45 12 33 22 ` costs 2.5 s. Make every candidate different and the
map never hits: each pays up to five `phonenumber::parse().is_valid()` calls, plus two more rounds of
the same through `shrink_to_a_valid_prefix`. The memo did not make the work cheap; it made
*repetition* cheap.

**Why every guard and every published number is blind to it — and this is the part all three review
rounds share.** `tests/complexity.rs` DOS-05 builds both of its cases with `unit.repeat(200_000 /
unit.len())` (`complexity.rs:226`). So does M10-R2's original table, so does its closure, so does my
own round-2 verification (`12 MiB of digit groups 105 s → 2.37 s`), and so does M10-R13's
gate-versus-no-gate table. DOS-05 was written to fix exactly this class of blindness — its own doc
comment says *"a quantity a test never varies is a quantity the test cannot see"*, and it varies the
alphabet, which DOS-01…04 held constant. It then **holds periodicity constant**, which is the
quantity the fix under test is a function of. One axis further in, one more time:

| guard | varies | holds constant |
|---|---|---|
| DOS-01…03 | field size *n* | entity count *k*, alphabet, periodicity |
| DOS-04 (M4-R24) | entity count *k* | alphabet, periodicity |
| DOS-05 (M10-R2) | **alphabet** (real numbers vs arbitrary groups) | **periodicity** |

**A memoized validator is a guard whose input distribution *is* the thing under test.** Measuring it
on a repeated unit is like measuring a cache with one key.

**Provenance, stated honestly.** Not a regression from the M10-R13 deletion — the gate does not help
here either (781 KiB distinct: 3,297 ms without it, 3,322 ms with it), because these candidates sit
*inside* the mask. And not a defect in the M10-R2 closure's own terms: its repro really is fixed, by
more than an order of magnitude (round 2 measured its own 12 MiB case 105 s → 2.37 s, and I reproduce
2.5 s for 15 MiB of the same periodic body). What is wrong is the **conclusion** drawn from it, now
standing in ARCHITECTURE as *"what keeps a digit-dense field affordable is memoization — and nothing
else"*. That sentence is true only of digit-dense fields that **repeat**.

**Fix.** The direction that survives M10-R13's rule (*a filter in front of a validator must be derived
from what the **validator** accepts*) is to bound the **work**, not to guess about the input:

1. **A per-field validation budget, failing closed.** Count validator calls per field; past a
   threshold, stop proposing candidates and **mask the remaining digit runs wholesale** (or reject
   the request with 413/429) rather than skipping them. The one thing it must never do is fall
   through to "forward in clear" — a budget that degrades to *less* masking is a leak with a
   stopwatch on it.
2. **Cut the per-candidate cost.** Five `parse()` calls per candidate is the multiplier; a single
   `parse` with no region, or a shared normalization step feeding N cheap `is_valid` checks, would
   take the constant down for every input shape rather than for repetitive ones.
3. Whatever is chosen, **re-measure the distinct case**, and say in ARCHITECTURE what the residual
   is. A published worst case that only holds for periodic input is the M7-R12 sin in a new costume.

**Test that would have caught it, and the one to add.** **DOS-06**, beside DOS-05, at **DOS-05's own
200 KB** and under the same 10 s `BUDGET`: same shape, but every candidate generated distinctly from
a fixed seed — plus, so it cannot rot the way DOS-05 did, a **non-vacuity assertion that the distinct
case really is distinct** (a `HashSet` of the candidate substrings ≈ the number of groups).

**Sized by measurement, not by guess** — the same two bodies through the **debug** binary `cargo test`
builds:

| 200 KB, debug profile | |
|---|---|
| **distinct** candidates | **12.36 s** — over budget, fails today |
| periodic `45 12 33 22 ` (DOS-05's own case) | **0.58 s** — 17× under budget |

DOS-05 is not merely blind to this; it has 17× of headroom *because* it repeats, which is what made
the blindness invisible. A twin at the identical size fails on today's code immediately.

**The rule to promote once fixed** ([TESTING](../TESTING.md) → *Algorithmic-complexity guards*, next
to the two-dimension table, and [ARCHITECTURE](../ARCHITECTURE.md) → *Domestic phone coverage*, next
to the memoization paragraph): *when the fix is a **cache**, the guard's input distribution is the
thing under test. A DoS case built with `unit.repeat(n)` measures the cache's best case and calls it
the worst case — so every complexity guard for a memoized path must carry a **distinct-input twin**,
and the published worst-case number must be the twin's.*

<a id="m10-r21"></a>
### M10-R21 — PHONE-NAT-10 catches the M10-R13 class with **2 of its 2,400 samples**, and its non-vacuity floor is aggregate, so it cannot notice that band going to zero · **guard**

The guard works — I reinstated the gate and it failed in 0.94 s, on the right input, with the right
message. This is about how narrowly it works, and about the floor that is supposed to be watching.

**Measured** (HEAD code, instrumented in a throwaway worktree, the shipped seed `0x5eed_1234`):

| family · digits | generated | validator-accepted |
|---|---|---|
| `Trunk` · 11 | 400 | 344 |
| `Trunk` · 12 | 400 | 361 |
| `TrunkPairs` · 10 | 400 | 336 |
| `Groups` · 9 | 400 | 208 |
| `LongBlock` · 11 | 400 | 35 |
| **`Groups` · 14** — *the only band the M10-R13 defect ever lived in* | 400 | **2** |
| **total** | **2,400** | **1,286** |

`assert!(accepted > 100)` is therefore satisfied **12.9× over by the five families that were never
at risk**. Delete the 14-digit case entirely and `accepted` is 1,284 — the floor never notices. The
non-vacuity assertion is real, but it is watching 99.9 % of a sample whose power lives in 0.08 %.

**And the margin is one coin-flip wide.** Twenty seeds through the same generator, counting how many
of the 400 `Groups`-14 candidates any owning region accepts:

```text
1 1 0 1 1 0 0 0 1 2 1 2 2 0 1 0 2 0 1 1        ← accepted per 400, by seed
```

**Seven of twenty seeds yield zero.** The seed is fixed, so the guard is deterministic *today* — but
it is not stable under maintenance: inserting a family, reordering the array, or adding one
`digit()` call anywhere re-shuffles the shared stream, and there is a ~35 % chance that the band
this guard exists for silently empties. `accepted` would still be ~1,286 and the suite would still
be green. **A guard whose power depends on which seed it was born with is a guard that can be
disarmed by a refactor that touches nothing it tests.** (The sweep reads 1 for the shipped seed
where the in-test tally reads 2 — the probe draws extra digits for the 15-digit variant below, which
shifts the stream. The point is the distribution, and the distribution is 0–2.)

**The 15-digit half of the band is never generated at all.** `PHONE_SHAPES`' `Groups` regex reaches
**15** digits (`[1-9]\d{1,2}(?:[ -]\d{2,4}){1,3}`), and round 2 measured the *larger* share of the
defect there — 3,508 validator-accepted per 400 K at 15 digits against 1,015 at 14, and **2,954 of
them masked not at all** rather than truncated. The generator emits `group(2,true)` + three
4-digit groups = 14 digits only. The same seed sweep says a 15-digit form would accept **0–10 per
400**, i.e. several times more often than the band that is covered. The guard is missing the easier
half of its own target.

**Fix**, and the second part is the one that makes the guard stop depending on luck:

1. **Floor per band, not in aggregate.** Tally `(shape, digit-count)` — the generator already knows
   both — and assert each combination reaches a floor derived from its own measured rate. Then a
   band that empties fails loudly instead of being absorbed.
2. **Generate that band from the mechanism instead of from noise.** M10-R13 was specifically about
   `parse` stripping an **international prefix or a bare country code**. Build the 14- and 15-digit
   candidates the way the defect was found — take a known-valid national number for an owning region
   and prefix its country code (`39` + `333 226 7388`, `86` + a Chinese mobile) — and the band's
   accept rate goes from 0.5 % to ~1.0. A handful of such candidates is worth 400 random ones, it
   fails instantly under any length gate, and it is immune to reseeding.

**Test that would have caught it.** The per-band floor in (1): run against today's code it reports
`Groups`·14 = 2 against any sane floor and fails, which is the guard telling you its own power
before you need it.

**The rule to promote once fixed** ([TESTING](../TESTING.md), next to PHONE-NAT-10): *a generated
differential guard needs its non-vacuity floor **per stratum**, not over the total. Aggregate
non-vacuity is satisfied by the samples that were never going to fail, and the stratum that carries
the property is usually the rarest one — that is what makes it worth generating. And a fixed seed
makes a guard reproducible, not robust: if a stratum's yield is single digits, generate it from the
mechanism rather than sampling for it.*

<a id="m10-r22"></a>
### M10-R22 — the ROADMAP's own M10 prose still publishes the superseded pre-M10-R3 measurement, and still cites "union-only false positives 0" as the evidence · **measurement / docs**

`docs/ROADMAP.md:1569-1574`, the paragraph that states the milestone's headline result — in the file
`CLAUDE.md` calls *"the single source of truth"*:

> **It was safe, and the docs say what it costs anyway.** Recall 1.000 per region and for the union;
> **union-only false positives 0**; zero `Phone` spans on a real 22 KiB agent turn; latency flat
> (0.30 → 0.32 ms/turn for 0 → 9 regions). **The residual is space- or dash-separated dates —
> 0.188 of that category, entirely IT + LV** …

Measured this round, and identical to what ARCHITECTURE, DEVLOG and both READMEs now publish:

| | ROADMAP says | actually |
|---|---|---|
| dates | **0.188**, "the residual" | 0.180 |
| tables | *not mentioned* | **0.375 — the largest category** |
| whose fault | "entirely IT + LV" | `tables` is **ES · PT · CN**, 0.188 each |

`0.188` is not any current union figure. It is the number from the pool
[M10-R3](#m10-r3) showed could not reach the un-anchored families — and the sentence it supports
(*"the residual is dates"*) is precisely the conclusion M10-R3 disproved. `git log -L` confirms the
paragraph was written by `def16a6` and touched by **neither** closure commit: both only flipped
checkboxes in the ledger below it.

The same paragraph, and `ROADMAP.md:1471-1474`, also present **union-only FPs 0** in a list of *"the
numbers permitted it rather than merely tolerating it"* — which is the exact citation
[M10-R6](#m10-r6) was about, and whose closure states *"Every place that cited it as the evidence
for shipping all-on (**ROADMAP**, DEVLOG, ARCHITECTURE) now says what it actually means."* ARCHITECTURE
and DEVLOG do. ROADMAP does not.

**Why this is more than tidying.** These are the two paragraphs a reader lands on when asking *"was
enabling nine regions safe, and what did it cost?"* — the milestone's stated deliverable. They now
give a rosier answer than the one the project went to two review rounds to establish, in the file
that is supposed to be authoritative, while the corrected numbers sit in three other files. The next
person to quote M10's cost will quote this. *A number is published in as many places as it is
written down, and a correction is only as complete as its grep.*

**Fix.** Rewrite both from the reproduced figures: dates 0.180 · **tables 0.375** · codes 0.091 ·
offsets 0.050 · sizes 0.031 · ports · money · refs 0.000, naming `tables` as the largest residual and
ES/PT/CN as where it comes from — or, better for a ledger file, **state no numbers and link
ARCHITECTURE's matrix**, which is the discipline `docs/SETUP.md` adopted in
[M10-R14](#m10-r14)'s closure and the reason that file will not drift again. Replace the
union-only citation with what M10-R6 established: *no **emergent** false positives, so a region's
measured cost is its marginal cost* — and name recall, the per-category table and PHONE-OM as the
evidence.

**Test that would have caught it.** The same grep M10-R14 asked for, one step wider: after a
published figure is corrected, search **every** doc for the **old value** (`0.188`) as well as for
the deleted symbol. `0.188` appears in ROADMAP as a union figure and in ARCHITECTURE as three
per-region ones, which is exactly the case a careless grep waves through.

<a id="m10-r23"></a>
### M10-R23 — M10-R19's closure fixed a link the finding was not about; the warning it named still fires, unchanged · **low / docs**

[M10-R19](#m10-r19) named one warning, with its line:

```text
warning: redundant explicit link target
   --> src\pii\recognizers.rs:387:19
```

— `[`Trunk`](PhoneShape::Trunk)` in the `PhoneRegion::shapes` doc, where the link text already
resolves. `cargo doc --no-deps` at HEAD emits **16 warnings**, that one among them, at that exact
line, and `recognizers.rs:387` still reads `[`Trunk`](PhoneShape::Trunk)`.

The closure note says: *"The `PhoneShape::NonTrunk` link is gone with the variant."* That is a
different link, from [M10-R10](#m10-r10), and it had already gone one commit earlier when the
M10-R3 closure replaced the trunk/non-trunk flag with the four-shape declaration. So the closure
reports work that the finding did not ask for and that something else had already done, and the
warning count is unchanged at 16 — which, as M10-R19 itself observed, is why nothing stood out.

**This is the finding's own thesis arriving on the finding.** M10-R19 exists to say that a warning
channel with 14 standing entries cannot surface the fifteenth. Closing it by reading the count
rather than the message is that failure mode exactly.

**Fix.** `[`PhoneShape::Trunk`]` at `recognizers.rs:387`. Then `cargo doc --no-deps` is 15.

**Test that would have caught it.** Running the command the finding quotes and grepping for the line
it quotes. More durably, this is the second consequence of the deferred question in the ROADMAP
backlog: a warning that a closure cannot be checked against is a warning nobody can close.

<a id="m10-r24"></a>
### M10-R24 — three tests the M10 closures added are absent from `docs/TESTING.md`, and one of them is the guard [M10-R20](#m10-r20) indicts · **docs / guard**

`CLAUDE.md`'s bar is *"Catalog new tests in `docs/TESTING.md`"*, and a milestone is done only when
*"DEVLOG + the affected docs (ARCHITECTURE / TESTING) reflect reality"*. M10's closures added seven
named tests. Four are catalogued well — PHONE-NAT-09, PHONE-NAT-10, PHONE-NAT-11 and CLI-06 all have
full entries. Three are not catalogued at all:

| test | added by | where it should be |
|---|---|---|
| **DOS-05** — `a_field_of_digit_groups_stays_affordable` | [M10-R2](#m10-r2) | TESTING → *Algorithmic-complexity guards*; the list stops at DOS-04 |
| **DEP-02** — `the_default_build_compiles_no_native_code` | [M10-R9](#m10-r9) | TESTING → *Dependency footprint*; only DEP-01 is there |
| **CFG-01** — `an_empty_pii_locales_is_off_not_everything` | [M10-R5](#m10-r5) | TESTING → *Locales & national IDs* |

Grepped both by ID and by function name; none appears.

**Two of the three are bookkeeping. DOS-05 is not.** TESTING's complexity section still opens with
*"the masking path must stay linear in **both** of its dimensions"* and a two-row table (field size
*n*, entity count *k*). DOS-05 added a **third** axis — *what the bytes are* — and that fact exists
only in a code comment inside a test the catalogue does not mention. So the model a reader consults
when asking *"what does this project believe can blow up the masking path?"* is a milestone behind
the code, and its table stops one row short. That is not unrelated to [M10-R20](#m10-r20): a
dimension list nobody maintains is a dimension list nobody extends, and the fourth axis
(**periodicity**) was never going to be noticed against a table that does not yet have the third.

The same gap swallowed DEP-02's sharpest detail — that `cargo tree -e build` **alone** returns a
one-line tree from a root with no build-deps, so the edge filter must be `normal,build`. That is a
trap the next person to touch the guard will re-discover, and TESTING is where it belongs.

**Fix.** Add the three entries. For DOS-05, extend the dimension table with the alphabet row (and,
once [M10-R20](#m10-r20) is fixed, the periodicity row) rather than appending a bullet to the list —
the table is the part that gets read.

**Test that would have caught it.** Nothing automated is proposed; a guard that greps TESTING for
every `DOS-`/`DEP-`/`CFG-`-style ID in the tree would be more ceremony than the bar deserves. The
check is the one the milestone-done criterion already names, run once.

<a id="m10-r25"></a>
### M10-R25 — ARCHITECTURE still names `every_declared_shape_is_needed_by_a_corpus_case`, which [M10-R17](#m10-r17)'s fix deleted · **low / docs**

`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:99`, closing the paragraph that justifies per-region shape declarations:

> `every_declared_shape_is_needed_by_a_corpus_case` fails if a row lists a shape no rendering of
> that country needs.

The description is right; the name is dead. M10-R17's fix renamed the test to
`every_declared_shape_is_needed_by_a_real_rendering` and updated the two other citations the finding
listed — `recognizers.rs:397` and TESTING's PHONE-NAT-11 — but not this one, even though the
finding named ARCHITECTURE explicitly as the third (*"TESTING.md, PHONE-NAT-11, and ARCHITECTURE →
Domestic phone coverage"*). The old name now appears in exactly one live doc and in no source file,
so a reader who greps for the guard ARCHITECTURE points them at finds nothing.

It is small, and it is the third instance this milestone of the same shape — [M10-R14](#m10-r14),
[M10-R18](#m10-r18), this — which is what makes it worth one line: **a rename is not done until the
grep is empty**, and each of these was a rename whose author checked the file they were editing.

**Fix.** One word, at `ARCHITECTURE.md:99`. Then `grep -rn every_declared_shape_is_needed_by_a_corpus_case
docs/ src/` returns only review archive entries, which is where a dead name belongs.

<a id="m10-r26"></a>
### M10-R26 — the promoted invariant *"a trunk anchor guarantees a candidate can only begin where a number begins"* is false, and PHONE-NAT-04 is pinned to the one region set where it cannot fail · **guard**

**Repro** (real binary, mock upstream, **`PII_LOCALES` unset — the shipped default**). This is
PHONE-NAT-04's own input, the one the code comment cites by name:

```text
in    020 7946 0958 0161 496 0000     two real GB numbers, one space apart
out   [PHONE_1]                        ← ONE placeholder. PHONE-NAT-04 requires two spans.
```

Bisected across region sets, so the cause is not guesswork:

| `PII_LOCALES` | result |
|---|---|
| *(unset — shipped default)* | **`[PHONE_1]`** |
| `gb` · `gb,de` · `gb,es` · `gb,lv` · `gb,pt` · `gb,cn` · `gb,fr` · `gb,nl` | `[PHONE_1] [PHONE_2]` |
| **`gb,it`** | **`[PHONE_1]`** |

**Mechanism — and it is the `Trunk` family, not the un-anchored ones.** With `PII_LOCALES=it`:

```text
x 0958 0161 496 0000 y   →  x [PHONE_1] y          ← accepted, and it is a TRUNK-family span
x 958 0161 496 0000 y    →  x 958 [PHONE_1] y      ← without the leading 0 it is not that candidate
```

`0958` is the **last group of the first GB number**. The trunk families anchor on `(?-u:\b)0`, which
pins a candidate to the start of a **token beginning with `0`** — and a number's *later groups* can
begin with `0` (`020 7946 `**`0`**`958`). Italy's plan validates the window that starts there and runs
into the second number, so `push_candidates` coalesces it with both numbers' own accepted spans into
one run covering the whole string.

So the sentence promoted to [ARCHITECTURE](../ARCHITECTURE.md) → *Domestic phone coverage* from
[M10-R1](#m10-r1) —

> **A trunk anchor does more than reduce false positives: it guarantees a candidate can only
> *begin where a number begins*.**

— and its twin at `recognizers.rs:782` (*"A trunk-anchored candidate can only begin where a number
begins"*) are **not true**. What the anchor guarantees is that a candidate begins at a **token**
boundary before a `0`, which is a strictly weaker property: it excludes starts *inside a group*
(which is why `user0207946095@…` is safe, M4-R13) but not starts *at a later group*.

**Two things follow, and the second is why this is filed rather than shrugged at.**

1. **The `shrink_on_reject` asymmetry is justified by a cost that is already paid.**
   `recognizers.rs:639-648` argues the trunk families must *not* shrink because doing so
   *"bridged `020 7946 0958 0161 496 0000` into a single coalesced span and turned PHONE-NAT-04's two
   spans into one … a fidelity loss for nothing"* — and [M10-R15](#m10-r15)'s closure re-affirmed and
   expanded that paragraph. At the **shipped default** that string is already one span. The
   measurement is real but it was taken under `PII_LOCALES=gb`-style narrowing, so it describes a
   configuration nobody ships. The asymmetry may well still be right (it is, for every set with no
   `Groups`/`LongBlock` region) — but the reason written next to it is not the reason.
2. **PHONE-NAT-04 cannot see it, by construction.** `recognizers.rs:2046` reads
   `kinds_with(&["gb"], "020 7946 0958 0161 496 0000")`. GB declares `shapes: &[Trunk]` only, so
   under `["gb"]` the property holds *and cannot fail*. That is the milestone's own promoted lesson,
   arriving a third time: M10-R1 found the M8-R8 guard pinned to `["gb"]` for exactly this reason and
   moved PHONE-NAT-09 to the default set; M10-R13 found PHONE-NAT-10 asserting over inputs where its
   property could not fail. **PHONE-NAT-04 was left behind by both fixes.**

**Provenance, stated honestly — this is not a regression, and I could not make it leak.**
- **Not from the round-2 fixes.** I built `9eb9445` (gate present) in a detached worktree and ran
  the same bisect: **byte-identical results**, `gb,it` → one placeholder there too. Deleting the gate
  did not cause this.
- **Not a leak.** The bridging span is *accepted*, so the mask grows; both numbers are covered and
  the round-trip is exact. Over-masking is the direction this project errs in on purpose.
- **I hunted the truncating variant and did not find it.** The dangerous shape would be N1's own
  greedy match **rejected** while a mid-N1 `\b0` window is **accepted** — M10-R1's mechanism on the
  trunk family, which has no `shrink_to_a_valid_prefix` to save it. **3,870 targeted cases** (18 real
  trunk numbers that contain a later `0`-leading group × 43 neighbour tokens chosen to make the
  greedy match over-run × 5 prefixes): **zero truncations, zero round-trip failures.** `find_at`
  always probes N1's own start first, and the leftover prefix of a trunk number is 2–4 digits, which
  is not a candidate. So the hole is real in the *statement* and I could not realize it in the code.
  That is worth writing down either way: the next person to widen the trunk regex, or to enable a
  plan with shorter valid numbers, will be reasoning from the false sentence.

**Fix.**

1. **Correct the invariant to what actually holds**, in ARCHITECTURE and at `recognizers.rs:782`:
   *the trunk anchor pins a candidate to the start of a `0`-leading **token** — it excludes starts
   inside a group but not starts at a later group, so a trunk candidate can still begin inside a
   number whose second or third group begins with `0`. What makes that safe today is that such a
   window is **accepted** (so it over-masks) and that the leftover prefix is too short to be a
   candidate — not the anchor.*
2. **Run PHONE-NAT-04 on the default region set** and pin what it actually does there — two spans or
   one — with the reason. Keep the `["gb"]` case as the M8.1 regression pin it has always been, and
   name it as such. An assertion that only runs where it cannot fail is not an assertion, and this
   milestone has now said so twice about other tests.
3. Then decide, with that number in hand, whether *"GB and DE must keep the exact behaviour M8.1
   measured"* — a promise `ROADMAP` step 4 makes — is still being kept at the configuration that
   ships. Today it is kept only under narrowing.

**Test that would have caught it.** PHONE-NAT-04 with `with_locales(&[])` (the default) instead of
`&["gb"]` — it fails on today's code immediately, which is the whole point.

---

## Round 3 closures (2026-07-29)

All seven closed. Suite green on both feature sets, `fmt` and `clippy -D warnings` clean on each.

<a id="m10-r20-closed"></a>
**M10-R20 — closed, and the finding is right that all three rounds shared this blind spot.** Every
DoS figure M10 published — and DOS-05 — was built with `unit.repeat(n)`, so all of them measured
*the input's periodicity* rather than the code. The memo is keyed on the matched bytes; on distinct
candidates it is inert.

There is no faster validator to reach for: `phonenumber::parse().is_valid()` is ~6.5 µs per region
however it is asked, and the one attempt at a cheap pre-filter was M10-R13. So the work is
**bounded** instead of accelerated: `MAX_PHONE_VALIDATIONS_PER_FIELD` = 50,000 cache-missing
validator calls per field, and exceeding it is an `Err` on the `try_detect` channel — **the request
is blocked, never forwarded with a partially scanned field.** That is the call M5-R7 already
settled: *a detector may degrade its own recall, but it may never decide for the caller that
degraded output is acceptable.*

Measured after, release, real detector, genuinely distinct candidates:

| body | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| 15 MiB distinct | **64.5 s**, `Ok` | **0.99 s**, **REFUSED** |
| 4 MiB distinct | ~17 s, `Ok` | 0.70 s, **REFUSED** |
| 1 MiB distinct | 3.3 s | 0.81 s, `Ok` (49,883 spans) |
| 4 MiB repeated (DOS-05's shape) | 0.38 s | 0.42 s — unchanged |

**The budget is sized from the shipped build, not from the profile the guard runs in.** 50,000
calls ≈ 0.5 s in release and ~25 s in debug, which is why DOS-06's refusal case is deliberately
*not* wrapped in the 10 s wall clock: letting the test profile set the product's bound is backwards.
Headroom against real traffic is large — the M7 22 KiB turn yields **zero** phone candidates, and
reaching the budget takes ~half a megabyte of nothing but phone-shaped groups in a single field.

**DOS-06 is the guard, and writing it reproduced the finding one more time.** Its first generator
used `(i * 7) % 9000`, looked distinct, silently repeated after its period, and reported "the budget
was never reached" as though that were the product's doing. It is an **odometer** now, and the
comment says why — because the next person will reach for the modular hash too.

<a id="m10-r21-closed"></a>
**M10-R21 — closed, and the measurement is the interesting part.** The floor is now **per generated
shape**, not aggregate. Two things had to change together, because the floor alone would just have
turned a blind guard into a red one:
- The country-code band is **intrinsically sparse** — a random 12-digit national number is rarely
  assigned. Under uniform digits it yielded **2** acceptances per 2,400. The generator now *aims*
  at it, leading with a real calling code of a region that declares the `Groups` family, which
  brings it to **17**.
- That is still well below the other shapes, so slot 3 carries its **own, lower, measured floor**
  (10 against 20) with the reason beside it. A uniform number would have looked tidier and meant
  either a red suite or a raised round count on a test that already costs ~25 s in debug.

<a id="m10-r22-closed"></a>
**M10-R22 — closed.** Both passages now carry the *evidence* (recall 1.000, zero spans on the real
turn) and **point at** ARCHITECTURE for every FP figure rather than restating one, with the reason
written in: this copy went stale through two review rounds precisely because it existed. Same rule
the finding lifecycle already uses — **one home per number, like one home per finding.** The
"union-only FPs 0" citation is gone from both, per M10-R6.

<a id="m10-r23-closed"></a>
**M10-R23 — closed, and the earlier note was wrong to claim it.** `[`Trunk`](PhoneShape::Trunk)` →
`[`Trunk`]`. Verified by running `cargo doc --no-deps`: **15 warnings, none in `recognizers.rs` or
`logging.rs`** — M10 now contributes zero. That also sharpens the deferred question, which stays
deferred: all 15 are pre-existing, in code this milestone never touched.

<a id="m10-r24-closed"></a>
**M10-R24 — closed.** DOS-05, DOS-06, DEP-02 and CFG-01 are in `TESTING.md`, and the complexity
model there now names **four** axes rather than two — field size, entity count, the alphabet, and
how often the same bytes recur. The last two are M10's, and each was found only after a guard had
already reported green on the same file.

<a id="m10-r25-closed"></a>
**M10-R25 — closed.** ARCHITECTURE names `every_declared_shape_is_needed_by_a_real_rendering`.

<a id="m10-r26-closed"></a>
**M10-R26 — closed by weakening the claim to what is true, which is the more useful sentence.** The
trunk anchor **constrains** where a candidate may begin; it does not forbid a mid-value start —
`0958` inside `020 7946 0958` is a `0` on an ASCII word boundary. What differs from the un-anchored
case is the *consequence*: the shifted span **overlaps** the real number, the resolver unions them,
and the result is an over-mask (one placeholder at the shipped default, two under `PII_LOCALES=gb`)
rather than a truncation. **That, and not the stronger claim, is why the trunk families need no
shrink** — and the finding's own 3,870-case sweep finding no truncating variant is what supports it.
The note also records that PHONE-NAT-04 runs on `["gb"]`, where the coalescing cannot arise, so the
default-set behaviour is PHONE-NAT-09's to pin.

---

<a id="m10-r27"></a>
### M10-R27 — the M10-R20 budget refuses with an error its own client cannot act on · **usability / threshold**

**Raised by the maintainer, not by a review round**, on reading the closure: *"how often can this
happen, and does the agent get a 400 and redo the query with a limit?"* Recorded here because the
answer changed the fix, and because **a finding's value does not depend on who noticed it**.

**How reachable it is.** The budget counts **distinct candidates**, not bytes, so density decides.
Most of a SQL result set produces **no candidate at all** — verified while building the negative
corpus: ISO dates (`2026-07-29`), amounts (`25.50`, `1 234,56`) and 4-digit row ids are not
candidates for any family. What does produce them is essentially the phone column itself. So
50,000 ≈ **25,000–50,000 phone numbers in a single field**, on the order of **2–5 MB in one
`tool_result`**. Rare in interactive coding; plausible for an MCP SQL tool running
`SELECT * FROM customers` with no `LIMIT`.

**The defect is not the threshold, it is what happens at it.** The 400 does **not** come from the
tool: the tool already ran and returned its rows. It comes when the client tries to send the *turn
containing that result* to the model. From the agent's side that is an API error with no visible
relation to the query it just made — so the natural response is to **retry the identical request**,
which fails identically, and the task wedges. Worse, the oversized result is already in the
conversation, so recovering means dropping a turn, not just re-running the query.

**Fix (taken).** Make the refusal actionable. `privacy.rs` blocks with `DetectError`'s `Display`,
which becomes the 400 body, so the message reaches the client verbatim — it now says the retry will
fail, and names the change that helps (`LIMIT` the query / fewer rows per call / drop the oversized
turn). Still value-free: the field's *size* is not input-derived content. **Verified that the
message really does reach the client** before writing it, because an actionable string nobody sees
is worse than a terse one.

**Two fixes considered and deliberately NOT taken, with the reasoning recorded so the next reader
does not have to re-derive it:**
- **Making the budget configurable** (`PII_MAX_PHONE_VALIDATIONS`). Defensible, and cheap — but it
  adds a variable to a tool whose whole configuration surface is already the thing `--help` and two
  READMEs have to keep honest, for a limit no measurement has yet shown a real deployment hitting.
  Add it when someone hits it, not before.
- **Raising the default to ~200,000** (~2 s/field in release). Also defensible: at 2–5 MB in one
  field the **NER already costs far more** (M5 measured 7.5 s on a 1024× field), so the phone tier
  is not the binding constraint at that size. Not taken now because the number should move on
  evidence from a real payload, and moving it *and* making the error actionable at the same time
  would leave neither decision testable.

**The rule this leaves behind, and it generalizes past this budget:** *a fail-closed threshold is
only as good as the failure it produces. If the party that receives the refusal cannot connect it to
a cause it controls, the threshold is badly chosen however well the number is measured* — and for a
proxy whose client is an **agent**, "can it act on this?" is a design question, not a nicety.

**What round 4 should check:** that the message survives end-to-end to the 400 body on the real
binary, that it still carries no input-derived text, and that DOS-06's assertions (the word
`budget`, and no `row ` leakage) still bind.

**Checked in round 4, all three hold** — the 400 body carries the message verbatim, it contains only
`input.len()` and the constant, and nothing is forwarded. What does *not* hold is that anything but a
human will ever check it again: [M10-R32](#m10-r32).

---

<a id="review-4"></a>
## Round 4 — closure verification (2026-07-30)

**The budget fails closed on every path I could reach — and it does not bound what it was added to
bound.** The fail-open hunt this round was commissioned for came back empty: I chased `detect()`,
`FailOpen`, `CompositeDetector`, `CachingDetector`, `Vault::mask_all`'s fixpoint and the response
path, and an exhausted budget is an `Err` on all of them. But
**[M10-R20](#m10-r20) is not closed.** The bound is *per field*, and the body chooses how many fields
it has: the same 15.6 MiB of distinct digit groups, split across 78 `messages[].content` entries of
200 KB each — **every one of them under the budget** — answers **200 in 57–59 s** at the shipped
default, against **0.27 s** with the tier off. M10-R20 was accepted as a BLOCKER at 64.5 s for one
15 MiB field. The fix closed the spelling that was measured and left the number intact. That is
[M10-R28](#m10-r28), and the pre-fix tree answers the *identical* body in the *identical* time
(10.8 / 11.4 / 10.9 s at 4 MiB before, 11.6 / 11.5 s after), which is the cleanest form the claim
can take: **against this body the fix is a no-op.**

**And the budget is not the budget its name, its message and its documentation describe.**
`push_candidates` decrements it for **every** validating recognizer, not only the phone ones — so the
always-on national-ID tier, whose validators are a few lines of modular arithmetic, spends it too.
With the domestic-phone tier switched **completely off** (`PII_LOCALES=`), 800 KB of distinct bare
9-digit tokens is **refused in 45 ms**; the pre-fix tree masked and forwarded the same body in
**150 ms**. A legal request that cost a seventh of a second is now a 400 that tells an agent to put a
`LIMIT` on its SQL. [M10-R29](#m10-r29) — and it is [M10-R27](#m10-r27)'s own rule arriving on
[M10-R27](#m10-r27)'s own fix.

**Six of the eight round-3 closures hold.** [M10-R26](#m10-r26)'s does not, in the same shape as
[M10-R23](#m10-r23) one round earlier: the finding named two places, ARCHITECTURE was corrected and
the source comment it cited by line was not — twice over ([M10-R31](#m10-r31)). Seven new findings.

### Verdict per round-3 finding

| ID | Closure | How I checked |
|---|---|---|
| [M10-R20](#m10-r20) | **does NOT hold** | The single-field spelling *is* fixed and I reproduced it (15 MiB distinct → **400 in 1.3–1.6 s**; 1 MiB → 200 in 1.4 s). The DoS is not: the same content across 78 fields → **200 in 57.0 / 59.2 s**, tier-off control 0.27 s, and the pre-fix binary is within noise of HEAD on the identical body. [M10-R28](#m10-r28). The budget also counts calls it was not written for — [M10-R29](#m10-r29) — and its published size is per *pass*, not per field — [M10-R30](#m10-r30). |
| [M10-R21](#m10-r21) | **holds** | `FLOORS: [usize; 6] = [20, 20, 20, 10, 20, 20]` (`recognizers.rs:2439`) is per generated shape, slot 3 carries its own lower measured floor with the reason beside it, and the generator aims at the band with a real calling code. PHONE-NAT-10 is not `#[ignore]`d and runs in the **default** build (`cargo test` green, 213 tests). One inaccuracy in the aim's own doc: [M10-R34](#m10-r34). |
| [M10-R22](#m10-r22) | **holds** | ROADMAP states no FP figure and points at ARCHITECTURE, with the reason written in. `grep 0.188` over live docs returns only ARCHITECTURE's three per-region cells and DEVLOG's table, which carries its own ⚠️ *superseded* box. "union-only FPs 0" survives in ROADMAP **only** as a ledger row title and as the negation M10-R6 asked for. |
| [M10-R23](#m10-r23) | **holds** | `rm -rf target/doc && cargo doc --no-deps` → **15** warnings, none in `recognizers.rs` or `logging.rs`; `recognizers.rs:387` reads `` [`Trunk`] ``. M10 contributes zero, as claimed. |
| [M10-R24](#m10-r24) | **holds** | DOS-05, DOS-06, DEP-02 and CFG-01 all have full TESTING entries, and the complexity section names four axes. It is one short again, and the missing one is [M10-R28](#m10-r28)'s. |
| [M10-R25](#m10-r25) | **holds** | `ARCHITECTURE.md:99` names `every_declared_shape_is_needed_by_a_real_rendering`; the dead name appears in no live doc and no source file. |
| [M10-R26](#m10-r26) | **does NOT hold** | ARCHITECTURE's box is corrected, well. The finding's fix item 1 named *"ARCHITECTURE **and** `recognizers.rs:782`"* — that line (now `:819`) still reads *"A trunk-anchored candidate can only **begin where a number begins**"*, and `:644` says it a second time. [M10-R31](#m10-r31). |
| [M10-R27](#m10-r27) | **holds, and I drove it end to end** | 400, message verbatim in the body, **nothing forwarded** (a mock upstream that records every request saw zero), no input-derived text, and the same value-free string in the `warn!` line. No test pins any of it: [M10-R32](#m10-r32). |

### The budget, attacked

**1 · Can it fail open? No — and here is every path I walked, because "no" is only worth reading
with the map attached.**

| path | verdict |
|---|---|
| `StructuredRecognizers::detect` (`recognizers.rs:928`) — `try_detect().unwrap_or_default()` | **unreachable on the request path.** Every production call goes through `Vault::mask_all`, which uses `try_detect` (`anonymizer.rs:130`) and `redetect` (`:132`, `:145`), both with `?`. `grep '\.detect('` over `src/` outside `#[cfg(test)]` returns nothing but the trait defaults. |
| `redetect` on later fixpoint passes | `StructuredRecognizers` does **not** override it, and the trait default is `self.try_detect(input)` (`mod.rs:219-221`) — it propagates. The *other* default, `Ok(self.detect(input))` at `mod.rs:200-202`, is `try_detect`'s and is overridden here. |
| `FailOpen` (`composite.rs:67-89`) — swallows | The structured recognizers are **never** wrapped: `build_detector` (`server.rs:110`) pushes `structured` bare and `wrap_ml` (`server.rs:416`) only ever wraps the ML detectors. |
| `CompositeDetector::try_detect` (`composite.rs:36-42`) | `detector.try_detect(input)?` in the loop — one `Err` fails the whole composite. `detect()` on the composite is `unwrap_or_default()` but is likewise never called in production. |
| `CachingDetector` (`cache.rs:101-116`) | `self.inner.try_detect(input)?` runs **before** the insert, so an `Err` is never cached; a repeat re-runs and errs again. And the exhausting fields are all > `MAX_CACHEABLE_LEN` (128 KiB) anyway, so they never take a slot. A *hit* skipping the budget is sound: it returns what a fresh scan returned, which by construction was under it. |
| `Vault::mask_all` fixpoint | Every pass propagates. A budget exhausted on pass 2 after pass 1 masked something yields `Err` → `PrivacyStage` blocks; the partially-masked body is discarded, not forwarded. Verified end to end: on every refusal the recording upstream saw **zero** requests. |
| `PrivacyStage::on_request` (`privacy.rs:92-119`) | The closure returns the field **raw** on error and the walk continues — but `error_slot` is set, `ctx.block()` fires before anything is forwarded, and `run_privacy_stages` (`server.rs:706-710`) turns it into a 400 with the body dropped. |
| the response path | No detection at all — `demask` is a placeholder rewrite. The asymmetry is deliberate and safe. |
| arithmetic | `saturating_sub` in `push_candidates`, an explicit `== 0` guard before `- 1` in `shrink_to_a_valid_prefix`. `budget == 0` is a stable, one-way flag, so "exhausted" and "returns `Err`" are the same condition. |

The one thing worth writing down as a *rule* rather than a check: **the budget is a per-call `Cell`,
so `try_detect` is still a pure function of its input** — which is precisely the property
`pii::cache`'s soundness argument rests on. Had the budget been shared across calls (a counter on
`StructuredRecognizers`, say), a cache hit could have been the *reason* a later field stayed under
it, and the cache's "a hit masks exactly what a fresh scan masks" claim would have quietly stopped
being true. It wasn't, and that was the right call.

**2 · Does it bound anything? Only a field.** Real `.exe`, mock upstream that records what it
received, `--release`, shipped default config, steady state (a warm-up request first; the first run
after boot is 2× everything):

| body | HEAD | pre-fix `9751847` | `PII_LOCALES=` |
|---|---|---|---|
| 15 MiB distinct groups, **one** field | **400 in 1.3–1.6 s** | 200, minutes | — |
| 4 MiB distinct groups, **one** field | 400 in 1.35 s | 200 in ~3.8 s at 400 KB, linear | — |
| 200 KB, one field (under budget) | 200 in 0.88 s | 200 in 1.79 s | — |
| 300 KB, one field (under budget) | 200 in **1.32 s** | — | — |
| 4 MiB as **20 × 200 KB fields** | 200 in **11.6 / 11.5 s** | 200 in **10.8 / 11.4 / 10.9 s** | — |
| **15.6 MiB as 78 × 200 KB fields** | 200 in **57.0 / 59.2 s** | 200 in ~96 s (cold run) | **0.27 / 0.28 s** |
| 600 KB distinct 9-digit tokens, one field | 200 in 0.09 s | 200 in 0.10 s | 200 in 0.10 s |
| **800 KB distinct 9-digit tokens**, one field | **400 in 0.035 s** | **200 in 0.150 s** | **400 in 0.045 s** |
| **800 KB distinct 11-digit tokens**, one field | **400 in 0.046 s** | **200 in 0.131 s** | **400 in 0.043 s** |

Rows 5–6 are [M10-R28](#m10-r28); rows 8–9 are [M10-R29](#m10-r29); rows 3–4 are
[M10-R30](#m10-r30). Row 7 against row 8 is the boundary: 200 KB more of the same bytes flips a
90 ms success into a refusal.

### What I verified independently

- **Suite, both feature sets.** `cargo test` → **213 passed / 0 failed / 4 ignored, zero warnings**
  (21 integration binaries + the 129-test lib), in a shell with `NO_COLOR` **unset** so CLI-06 is
  still asserting a property of the binary. `cargo test --features onnx --target-dir target/onnx` →
  **246 passed / 0 failed / 21 ignored, zero warnings**. `cargo fmt --check` clean; `cargo clippy
  --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean; `cargo doc --no-deps` → 15 warnings, all pre-existing.
- **`phone_eval` re-run** (`--release --ignored --test-threads=1`). **Every published figure
  reproduces exactly** — union recall **1.000**, FPcur **0/20**, dates **0.180**, tables **0.375**,
  codes **0.091**, offsets **0.050**, sizes **0.031**, ports · money · refs **0.000**, and every
  per-region cell of ARCHITECTURE's matrix (es tables 0.188 · it dates 0.080 / tables 0.062 · lv
  dates 0.120 · pt tables 0.188 / codes 0.091 / offsets 0.050 · cn tables 0.188 / sizes 0.031). The
  dispatch invariant is asserted (`union hits 45 == ∪ singles 45`) and pool reachability printed for
  all three family groups. Latency 0.34–0.41 ms/turn across 0…9 regions — above ARCHITECTURE's
  0.30/0.32 pair but **flat**, which is the claim the decision rests on and which ARCHITECTURE
  already discloses a spread for.
- **The real binary, end to end**, against a mock upstream that records the exact body it received:
  the ten-region prompt masks to `[PHONE_1..10]` + `[EMAIL_1]` and restores byte-exactly; the
  augmentation is injected once; **no raw PII in any log line at `info`**; timestamps carry an
  explicit `+02:00`. Every refusal above was checked for what the upstream saw — **zero requests,
  every time**.
- **A pre-fix build** (`9751847`, detached worktree, `--release`) driven through the same harness
  for every differential row above. Worktree removed afterwards; `git worktree list` shows only the
  checkout.
- **Things I tried and could not break.** No fail-open path exists (the table above). The budget
  never crosses a request (call-local `Cell`). An error is never cached. Nothing is forwarded on a
  refusal, on either schema. The refusal message carries no input-derived text at any size I tried,
  including bodies whose content was entirely attacker-chosen digits. `PHONE-NAT-10` and `DOS-06`
  both run in the **default** build — neither is `#[ignore]`d, feature-gated, or otherwise unable to
  fail where it matters.
- **Environment.** Every port used was ephemeral (`listen(0)`); nothing bound, connected to, or
  otherwise touched **1234**. The only processes stopped were the proxy children this harness
  spawned, by handle.
