<a id="m10-r28"></a>
### M10-R28 — the validation budget bounds a **field**, and the body chooses how many fields it has: M10-R20's 15 MiB DoS answers 200 in 57 s, unchanged by its own fix · **BLOCKER (availability)**

**Repro** (release build, the real `.exe`, **no environment overrides beyond the upstream URL** —
default `MAX_BODY_BYTES` = 16 MiB, default `PII_LOCALES`):

```text
request body: 15.60 MiB  — 78 messages[].content fields, each 200 KB of space-separated
                           `NN NNNN NNNN` groups generated independently (no two candidates equal).
                           EVERY field is under MAX_PHONE_VALIDATIONS_PER_FIELD.
status 200 in 57.0 s   (second run 59.2 s)
same body, PII_LOCALES=   →  status 200 in 0.27 s          ← ~210×
same body, pre-fix 9751847 →  status 200, same order       ← the fix changed nothing here
```

Scaling, with the field size held at 200 KB and only the field **count** varying:

| fields × 200 KB | body | HEAD | pre-fix `9751847` |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 0.2 MiB | 0.9–1.8 s | 1.8 s |
| 5 | 1.0 MiB | 2.0 s | — |
| 20 | 4.0 MiB | **11.6 / 11.5 s** | **10.8 / 11.4 / 10.9 s** |
| 40 | 8.0 MiB | 49.9 s (cold) | — |
| **78** | **15.6 MiB** | **57.0 / 59.2 s** | ~96 s (cold) |

**The 20-field row is the load-bearing one**: three runs on each binary, warmed, and they are
indistinguishable. Whatever the budget did, it did not do it here.

**Mechanism, and it is one line of scope.** `try_detect` (`recognizers.rs:936`) allocates
`Cell::new(MAX_PHONE_VALIDATIONS_PER_FIELD)` **per call**, and `PrivacyStage`'s walk calls it once
**per text field** (`privacy.rs:145-178` for OpenAI, `:303-339` for Anthropic — `messages[].content`,
each content part, each `tool_use.input` leaf, each `tool_result` block, every `description`). So the
bound on a *request* is `50,000 × (number of text fields) × (fixpoint passes)`, and every one of
those multipliers is chosen by the client. `MAX_BODY_BYTES` is the only thing that limits the
product, and at 16 MiB it permits ~78 fields of the densest shape.

**Why nothing saw it, and it is the same shape as M10-R20 itself.** DOS-06
(`tests/complexity.rs:272`) does exactly what M10-R20 asked: it varies **periodicity**, which DOS-05
held constant. It then holds the **field count** at 1 — `detector.try_detect(&huge)` on a single
`String` (`complexity.rs:319`), never through `mask_request`. TESTING's complexity model now names
four axes (size, entity count, alphabet, periodicity); this is the fifth, and it is the first one
that is not a property of the *text* at all but of the *body*:

| guard | varies | holds constant |
|---|---|---|
| DOS-01…03 | field size *n* | entity count, alphabet, periodicity, **field count** |
| DOS-04 (M4-R24) | entity count *k* | alphabet, periodicity, **field count** |
| DOS-05 (M10-R2) | alphabet | periodicity, **field count** |
| DOS-06 (M10-R20) | **periodicity** | **field count** |

Every complexity guard this project has ever written measures **one string**. The masking path takes
a **body**.

This is [M4's retrospective lesson 6](M4.md#retrospective) — *ask what a guard holds constant, not
only what it varies* — arriving for the third time, and the third time it has been the **quantity
one level above the one the guard thinks in**. DOS-01…03 varied field size and pinned entity count
at one; M4-R24 lived underneath them for four milestones. DOS-05 varied the alphabet and pinned
periodicity; M10-R20 lived underneath it. DOS-06 varies periodicity and pins the field count at one.
Each guard was written *by* the finding that the previous blind spot produced, and each inherited the
next blind spot from the shape of the thing it was handed to test: **a `&str`.**

**Provenance, stated honestly.** Not a regression: the pre-fix tree is the same speed on this body,
so nothing got worse. And M10-R20's own repro really is fixed — 15 MiB in one field is a 1.3–1.6 s
refusal, and I reproduced that. What is wrong is the **conclusion**, now standing in ARCHITECTURE as
*"the work is **bounded** instead"* and in DEVLOG as *"the request is blocked, never forwarded with a
partially scanned field"*. Both are true of a field. Neither is true of a request, and the number
that made M10-R20 a BLOCKER is a property of a request.

**Fix.** The unit has to become the one the attacker cannot multiply:

1. **One budget per request, threaded through the walk.** `PrivacyStage::on_request` already owns a
   per-request `Vault`; it can own a per-request validation counter the same way, handed to
   `mask_all` and down to the detector. It needs a seam on `PiiDetector` (a `try_detect_within`
   taking the shared `Cell`, defaulting to today's behaviour) — the smallest change that makes the
   bound mean what its name says.
2. **Or bound it where the body is already known:** cap the *cumulative* validated bytes per request
   in the stage itself, before the walk reaches the detector. Cruder, no trait change, and it
   composes with (1) rather than competing.
3. Whatever is chosen, **the threshold is a product decision, not a review one** — it trades a legal
   body size against a per-request CPU ceiling, and [M10-R29](#m10-r29) has to be settled first or
   the number will be measured against the wrong work.

**Test that would have caught it, and the one to add.** **DOS-07**, beside DOS-06 and at the same
total byte count: the identical content, split across *N* fields, driven through
`PrivacyStage::on_request` (or the real binary) rather than `try_detect` — asserting the **request**
is bounded or refused. Run against today's code it passes neither half, which is the guard telling
you its own scope.

**The rule to promote once fixed** ([TESTING](../TESTING.md) → *Algorithmic-complexity guards*, next
to the axis table, and [ARCHITECTURE](../ARCHITECTURE.md) → *Domestic phone coverage*, next to the
budget paragraph): *a budget scoped to a unit the client can multiply is not a bound — it is a rate.
When a guard's unit (one string) is smaller than the attack's unit (one body), the guard cannot see
the attack however many axes it varies inside its own unit.*

**Closed (2026-07-30), fix (1) — one `Budget` per request, threaded through.** `PrivacyStage::
on_request` creates it beside the `Vault` it already owns, and it travels via `mask_all_within` →
`try_detect_within` / `redetect_within`. `MAX_PHONE_VALIDATIONS_PER_FIELD` is now
`MAX_PHONE_VALIDATIONS_PER_REQUEST`, and the name is checkable rather than aspirational.

Measured on the same bodies, release, same box:

| body | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| 15.6 MiB across 78 × 200 KB fields | `200` in **57.0 / 59.2 s** | **refused in 0.19 s** |
| 4.0 MiB across 20 × 200 KB fields | `200` in 11.5 s | **refused in 1.46 s** |
| 1.0 MiB across 5 × 200 KB fields | `200` in 2.0 s | `200` in 0.70 s |
| one 200 KB field | `200` | `200` |

**Three wrappers had to be made to forward it, and that was the sharp part.** The trait default for
`try_detect_within` delegates to plain `try_detect` — correct for a detector whose cost is not what
is being bounded, and **silently wrong for a wrapper**: `CompositeDetector`, `CachingDetector` and
`FailOpen` would each have routed the call to `try_detect` and handed the detector underneath a
*fresh* allowance, restoring exactly the behaviour this finding is about, on the shipped path, with
every test green. Each now overrides both methods explicitly and says why in a doc comment.

> **`FailOpen` came out of this differently than it went in, and it is the part worth keeping.** Its
> whole purpose is to swallow a detector error, so it would have swallowed a budget refusal too —
> `Ok(vec![])`, i.e. *"no PII here"*, for a body nobody finished scanning. Round 4's fail-open hunt
> came back empty **only because nothing wraps the structured recognizers in `FailOpen` today**: a
> property of the wiring, not of the code. So the wrapper now distinguishes the two: *"this detector
> is unavailable"* is a property of the **detector** and is swallowed as before; *"the allowance for
> scanning this request ran out"* is a property of the **request** and propagates. Promoted to
> ARCHITECTURE. A finding hunted for a fail-open path, found none, and the *reason* none existed was
> itself the defect.

A **cache hit spends nothing**, which is correct rather than a loophole: the budget bounds work
actually done, a hit did none, and the module's soundness argument is that a hit returns what a fresh
scan would. The M10-R28 body cannot exploit it — its fields are distinct by construction. An error is
still never inserted, so a refusal cannot be replayed from the cache to a request with its own full
allowance.

**The guard is DOS-07**, and it is the axis table's fifth row: 20 fields that each pass **on their
own**, refused together, driven through `PrivacyStage::on_request`. It asserts the single-field pass
first — without that it could be green because one field already exceeds the allowance, which would
say nothing about the request being the unit.

Fix (2) — the cumulative-bytes cap in the stage — was **not** taken. It composes with (1) rather than
competing, and with (1) in place it would bound bytes rather than work: a large digit-poor body would
be refused like a dense one, for no gain. Worth revisiting only if a *second* validator tier ever
becomes expensive.

<a id="m10-r29"></a>
### M10-R29 — `MAX_PHONE_VALIDATIONS_PER_FIELD` is spent by **every** validating recognizer, so the always-on national-ID tier refuses legal requests in 45 ms with the phone tier switched off · **correctness / availability**

**Repro** (release, real `.exe`, one `messages[0].content` field):

```text
PII_LOCALES=            (the domestic-phone tier is NOT LOADED AT ALL — CFG-01's "none")
body: 800 KB of distinct bare 9-digit tokens, `id=123456789,` repeated with a fresh number each time
→ status 400 in 45 ms
   "a 800007 byte field exceeded the domestic-phone validation budget of 50000 checks …
    for a tool result, add a LIMIT to the query or return fewer rows per call"
```

| body (one field) | pre-fix `9751847` | HEAD, shipped default | HEAD, `PII_LOCALES=` |
|---|---|---|---|
| 600 KB distinct 9-digit tokens | 200 in 104 ms | 200 in 89 ms | 200 in 103 ms |
| **800 KB distinct 9-digit tokens** | **200 in 150 ms** | **400 (35 ms)** | **400 (45 ms)** |
| **800 KB distinct 11-digit tokens** | **200 in 131 ms** | **400 (46 ms)** | **400 (43 ms)** |

The pre-fix rows are what makes this more than a naming quibble: **a legal request that took 150 ms
and was masked and forwarded is now a 400.** The upstream byte counts confirm masking really was
happening (834 KB out for 800 KB in — the M4-R6 mod-11 over-masks), so the refusal costs real
coverage, not just a status code.

**Mechanism.** The counter lives in `push_candidates` (`recognizers.rs:764`), which decrements it on
every **cache-missing** call to `self.validate` — for *any* recognizer that has one. That is nine
more than the phone families:

| recognizer | `recognizers.rs` | validator | on by default? |
|---|---|---|---|
| CreditCard | `:230` | Luhn | always |
| IT Codice Fiscale | `:285` | checksum letter | always (M4-R1) |
| UK NINO | `:298` | prefix rules | always |
| ES DNI/NIE | `:307` | mod-23 | always |
| FR NIR | `:319` | mod-97 | always |
| NL BSN / PT NIF | `:334` | mod-11 | always |
| DE Steuer-ID / LV code | `:346` | Mod 11,10 | always |
| LV dashed code | `:354` | mod-11 | always |
| CN resident ID | `:363` | MOD 11-2 | always |

All nine are pure arithmetic on ≤ 18 bytes — nanoseconds, not the ~6.5 µs per region that the whole
budget exists to bound. Fifty thousand of them is **five milliseconds of work refused as if it were
half a second**. And because national IDs are always-on *by design* (M4-R1), there is no
configuration that opts out: the operator who set `PII_LOCALES=` in response to the `phonenumber`
supply-chain advisory that ARCHITECTURE documents gets the phone tier's refusals without the phone
tier.

**Three consequences, in the order they bite.**
1. **A false refusal.** Legal traffic is blocked where there is no cost to bound.
2. **A misdirected message.** [M10-R27](#m10-r27) made the refusal actionable and it is now
   *confidently wrong*: it names the domestic-phone budget and prescribes a SQL `LIMIT` for a field
   whose cost came from nine-digit tokens. An agent that follows it changes the wrong thing. This is
   M10-R27's own rule — *a fail-closed threshold is only as good as the failure it produces* —
   landing on M10-R27's own fix.
3. **The real protection is less than advertised.** At the shipped default the cheap validators eat
   the same budget the expensive ones need, so the phone tier's actual allowance is 50,000 *minus*
   whatever the always-on tier spent first — and `for rec in &self.recognizers` (`:938`) runs the
   universal and national-ID recognizers **before** the phone families, so on a mixed body they
   spend it first.

**Fix.** Count only what the budget exists to bound. The narrowest version is to decrement inside the
phone validator closure (`recognizers.rs:611-641`) rather than in `push_candidates`, which also makes
the count *per `parse()` call* rather than per candidate and so finally matches the ~6.5 µs the
number was derived from. If a global cap on all validators is wanted too, it should be its own
constant, its own number, and its own sentence in the message — not this one wearing a phone label.

**Test that would have caught it.** A case with `PII_LOCALES=` asserting that a digit-dense field is
**masked and forwarded**, not refused — the natural sibling of CFG-01, which already pins that the
empty value means "none". Today it fails immediately, which is the point.

**Closed (2026-07-30), and both halves of the fix landed.** The budget is no longer decremented in
`push_candidates`. `Validator` became `Box<dyn Fn(&str, &Budget) -> bool + …>`, so each validator
charges what it actually costs: the phone families spend **one unit per `phonenumber::parse()`**, and
the nine always-on checksums spend nothing. They go through a named `free(…)` adapter rather than nine
hand-written `|s, _|` closures — so *not charging* is one visible decision instead of nine
independent ones a tenth recognizer might silently join.

Measured, release, one field: `PII_LOCALES=` on the 800 KB body that was a `400` in 45 ms now spends
**0 units** and returns `200` in **3 ms** — against 132 ms with the nine regions on. The tier's share
of the cost is now the tier's alone.

Per-`parse()` rather than per-candidate matters more than it looks: one candidate is validated against
up to nine regions, so a "unit" used to mean anywhere from 1 to 9 parses, and **no number of units
could encode a CPU ceiling**. It can now — one unit is ~2.7 µs measured — which is what let
[M10-R30](#m10-r30)'s figure be re-derived instead of re-quoted, and what made choosing
[M10-R28](#m10-r28)'s threshold a measurement rather than a guess.

> **The tightening this exposed, and it changed the shipped threshold.** Charging per parse made the
> effective allowance ~9× smaller at the same constant. At 50,000 units a **367 KB database tool
> result with one phone column** — five thousand rows, an entirely ordinary thing for an agent to
> fetch — came back a `400`. Raised to **500,000** (~1.4 s CPU, ≈50,000 numbers per request) on the
> maintainer's call, measured against that legal payload rather than an adversarial one. *A
> fail-closed threshold whose refusal is a routine event is the wrong threshold* — every refusal costs
> the agent a turn, and a bound that fires on legal traffic teaches its operator to raise it rather
> than to trust it. Which is also why it is not an environment variable.

**The guard is the CFG-01 sibling** the finding asked for, in `complexity.rs`: with no phone region
enabled, a digit-dense field must be **masked and forwarded**. It also asserts the vault is non-empty
— the always-on national-ID tier really does match here, and an unrefused but *unscanned* field would
be the same leak wearing a `200`.

And the misdirected message is fixed with it: the refusal now names the **request** as the unit and
says that splitting the same content across more fields will not help.

<a id="m10-r30"></a>
### M10-R30 — the published bound is per *pass*, not per field: `mask_all` runs detection up to five times per field and each gets a fresh budget · **measurement / docs**

`ARCHITECTURE.md:203-209` and `DEVLOG.md:66-67` both state the bound as a per-field cost:

> `MAX_PHONE_VALIDATIONS_PER_FIELD` (50,000 ≈ 0.5 s in the shipped build) … reaching the budget takes
> on the order of half a megabyte of nothing but phone-shaped digit groups in a **single field**.

Three things in that sentence do not hold.

1. **"Per field" is per call.** `Vault::mask_all` (`anonymizer.rs:124-147`) invokes detection up to
   **five** times on the same field — `try_detect` on pass 0, `redetect` on passes 1–3, and one final
   confirming `redetect`. `StructuredRecognizers` does not override `redetect`, so each of those is
   `try_detect`, and `try_detect` allocates a **fresh** `Cell` (`recognizers.rs:936`). One field may
   therefore legitimately spend **250,000** cache-missing validator calls without ever "exceeding the
   budget". The constant's own name says `_PER_FIELD`.
2. **0.5 s is not what a sub-budget field costs.** Measured end to end on the real binary, steady
   state: a single 300 KB field that is *never* refused takes **1.32 s**, and 200 KB takes
   0.88–1.85 s across runs. Subtracting the tier-off baseline for the same bytes (~5 ms) leaves it
   all in the detector. That is 2–4× the published figure, and the multiplier in (1) is the obvious
   candidate.
3. **"Phone-shaped" is false** — [M10-R29](#m10-r29): 800 KB of bare 9-digit tokens reaches the
   budget with the phone tier not loaded.

**Why it matters beyond tidiness.** This is the sentence a reader consults to answer *"can a real
payload hit this?"* — the exact question [M10-R27](#m10-r27) was raised to ask. It answers with a
number that is 2–4× optimistic, for a unit that is 5× smaller than stated, about a shape that is not
the only shape that qualifies. M10-R27 priced the reachability of the refusal against it and
concluded ~2–5 MB in one `tool_result`; on the corrected arithmetic the answer is smaller in every
direction.

**Fix.** State the bound in the unit it actually has (*per detection pass*), give the per-field
figure as `passes × budget`, re-measure the wall clock on the shipped build, and drop "phone-shaped"
once [M10-R29](#m10-r29) is settled. If the per-field figure is meant to be a real bound, the budget
has to be threaded through `mask_all` rather than re-minted per pass — which is the same seam
[M10-R28](#m10-r28) needs, so the two fixes are one.

**Test that would have caught it.** DOS-06 asserts the refusal but never asserts the *cost* of the
largest field that is **not** refused. A wall-clock case at that boundary — sized from the release
profile, as the constant already is — would have made the 0.5 s claim checkable instead of quoted.

**Closed (2026-07-30) — and the fix was to stop quoting the number and start deriving it.** All three
defects are gone, but only (1) was a code change:

1. **The unit.** [M10-R28](#m10-r28)'s fix is the same seam this needed, exactly as predicted: one
   budget per request, threaded through `mask_all_within`, so the five fixpoint passes share one
   allowance instead of minting five. The constant is `MAX_PHONE_VALIDATIONS_PER_REQUEST` and the
   name is now checkable.
2. **The wall clock.** Re-measured on `--release` rather than restated: one unit is **~2.7 µs**, not
   the ~6.5 µs the library's docs suggest, and 500,000 units is **~1.4 s**. The largest field that
   is *not* refused — 2 MiB of nothing but phone-shaped groups, 499,380 units — measures **1.32 s**,
   which is the boundary cost the finding said nobody had measured.
3. **"Phone-shaped".** Dropped, because [M10-R29](#m10-r29) made it true instead: the phone validator
   is the only thing that charges the budget now, so the shapes that can reach it really are the
   phone families'.

**The measurement is DOS-BUD**, `#[ignore]`d and run with `--release`: the refusal line per field
size, per *field count*, over a realistic SQL tool result, and with the tier off. It is in the suite
rather than in a paragraph precisely because of this finding — *a number a reader must trust is a
number that goes stale; a number they can re-run is a fact.* Its realistic-payload row is also what
set the shipped threshold ([M10-R29](#m10-r29)), so the harness that answers *"can real traffic hit
this?"* is now the same one that chose the answer.

**Writing it reproduced the milestone's own trap a fourth time.** The first version of DOS-BUD's SQL
generator built the phone column with `(r * 7) % 9000`, which repeats after its period — so 20,000
rows measured the *same* 30,781 units as 10,000, and that reads as sub-linear cost rather than as a
generator artefact. Caught only because the two rows were adjacent in the output. It is an odometer
now, with the reason beside it, in the same file where DOS-06 already carries that warning.

<a id="m10-r31"></a>
### M10-R31 — M10-R26's closure corrected ARCHITECTURE and left the sentence it disproved in the source, at the line the finding named · **guard / docs**

[M10-R26](#m10-r26)'s fix item 1 read: *"**Correct the invariant to what actually holds**, in
ARCHITECTURE **and at `recognizers.rs:782`**"*. ARCHITECTURE was corrected, and well —
`ARCHITECTURE.md:227-233` now says the anchor *constrains* rather than *forbids*, names `0958` inside
`020 7946 0958` as the counter-example, and explains that the consequence is an over-mask rather than
a truncation. The source was not:

```text
src/pii/recognizers.rs:819   A trunk-anchored candidate can only *begin where a number begins*, so a
                             greedy over-match that swallowed the next number's trunk was rejected…
src/pii/recognizers.rs:644   A trunk-anchored candidate already begins where a number begins, so a
                             rejected over-match shadows rather than truncates…
```

`:819` is the line the finding cited (the budget parameters added in `a6c2046` moved it from `:782`).
`:644` is a second instance in `national_phone_recognizers`, and it is the one that matters most: it
is the justification written **next to `shrink_on_reject`**, i.e. next to the decision the false
sentence is used to defend. A reader of the source — which is where the next person will be when they
widen the trunk regex or enable a plan with shorter valid numbers — gets the disproved version, and
ARCHITECTURE's correction is a file away.

**This is [M10-R23](#m10-r23) one round later, in the same milestone.** That finding named one
warning at one line; its closure fixed a different link and reported success. This one named two
places; its closure fixed one and reported success. *A closure is checked against the finding's own
locations, or it is checked against nothing.*

**Fix.** Replace both with the sentence ARCHITECTURE already carries: *the trunk anchor pins a
candidate to a `0` on an ASCII word boundary — it excludes starts inside a group but not starts at a
later group; what makes that safe is that the shifted window is **accepted**, so it overlaps and the
resolver unions it (an over-mask), never truncates.* Then `grep -n "begin where a number begins" src/`
is empty. (`DEVLOG.md:137` also carries it, and that one is **correct as history** — it is round 1's
entry recording what was believed then, and `a6c2046`'s entry records the correction.)

**Test that would have caught it.** The grep in the finding, run over `src/` as well as `docs/`. More
durably: a closure that names two `file:line`s should be verified at both, and the record should say
so — which is what this round's verdict table is for.

**Closed (2026-07-30).** Both instances replaced with the version ARCHITECTURE carries: the anchor
*constrains* a candidate's start without forbidding a mid-value one (`0958` inside `020 7946 0958`),
and what it buys is the **consequence** — the shifted span is accepted, so it overlaps and the
resolver unions it, an over-mask rather than a truncation. `grep -rn "begin where a number begins"
src/` is now empty; `DEVLOG.md:137` keeps it, correctly, as the record of what round 1 believed.
The `:644` instance mattered more than the doc-comment one and it is the one that now reads right:
it sits next to `shrink_on_reject`, which is the decision the false sentence was used to defend.

<a id="m10-r32"></a>
### M10-R32 — M10-R27 changed client-visible behaviour with no test, and DOS-06's two assertions pass unchanged on the message it replaced · **guard**

`143c680` rewrote the refusal so an agent can act on it. It works — I verified the whole chain on the
real binary: 400, the message verbatim in `error.message`, nothing forwarded, no input-derived text.
Nothing in the suite checks any of that.

DOS-06's assertions (`tests/complexity.rs:326`, `:331`) are:

```rust
assert!(err.message.contains("budget"), …);
assert!(!err.message.contains("row "), …);
```

Both pass on the **pre-M10-R27** message, byte for byte — *"a N byte field exceeded the
domestic-phone validation budget of 50000 checks; blocking rather than forwarding a partially scanned
field"* contains `budget` and no `row `. So the entire actionable half — *"retrying it unchanged will
fail identically"*, the `LIMIT`, "drop the oversized turn" — can be deleted, truncated or reverted and
the suite stays green. **An assertion that cannot distinguish the fix from what it replaced is not a
regression guard for that fix.**

And the property the finding is *about* — that the message **reaches the client** — is asserted
nowhere at all. DOS-06 reads `err.message` from the detector directly; it never goes near
`privacy.rs`'s `ctx.block(err.to_string())` or `server.rs:709`'s `error_response`. The record itself
defers it: *"What round 4 should check: that the message survives end-to-end to the 400 body"*. A
property verified by hand once, at the moment it was written, is a property that will be wrong the
first time `DetectError`'s `Display` or the error-response shape changes — and both have moved before
in this project.

**Fix.** One `binary_smoke`/`proxy_e2e` case: drive a budget-exhausting field through the real path
and assert the 400 body's `error.message` (a) names the budget, (b) carries the actionable clause,
(c) contains no substring of the input. (c) is the one that must not be dropped — it is the
never-log-raw-PII bar on the one string this codebase deliberately builds from a request.

**Test that would have caught it.** Itself. Failing that, the closure discipline: a fix whose whole
content is a *string a client reads* has to be pinned where the client reads it, not where it is
constructed.

**Closed (2026-07-30).** **E2E-05** added to `tests/proxy_e2e.rs`
(`e2e05_budget_refusal_reaches_the_client_intact_and_carries_no_input_bytes`), catalogued in
[TESTING](../TESTING.md): a 1 MiB field built by DOS-06's odometer, driven through the real router,
asserting `400` + `error.type = "blocked"` and then the three properties on `error.message` — (a)
names the budget, (b) carries *"Retrying it unchanged will fail identically"* **and** the `LIMIT`,
(c) contains no digit run drawn from the body. (b) is what DOS-06 could not distinguish; (c) is
enforced structurally rather than by one `contains` — every digit run in the message is checked
against the body, so the only ones that may appear are `input.len()` and the constant. A **counting
mock upstream** pins "not forwarded" as a fact rather than an inference from the status code.
10 s in the debug profile; 1 MiB was enough to exhaust the budget, so the case costs a quarter of
DOS-06's 4 MiB.

> **Why the input is phone-shaped and not the cheap thing.** Bare 9-digit tokens reach the budget in
> 45 ms ([M10-R29](#m10-r29)) and would have made this test 200× faster — by riding the defect R29
> describes. A guard whose speed depends on a bug being present breaks the moment the bug is fixed,
> and would have argued *for* keeping it. This one is deliberately slow and R29-independent.

<a id="m10-r33"></a>
### M10-R33 — both READMEs enumerate the causes of a 400 and omit the one this milestone added; the "linear under load" bullet next to it is now contradicted by measurement · **docs**

`README.md:196-198` / `README.it.md:198-201`, in *The bar it holds itself to*:

> **Fail closed.** An unreadable request shape, an unknown content-block type, a required detector
> that errors, or masking that can't reach a stable fixpoint all **block the request (400)** …

That is an exhaustive-sounding list, and the **validation-budget refusal is not in it** — the one new
client-visible 400 M10 adds, and the only one an operator can trigger with a body that is otherwise
completely legal. Someone debugging a 400 in production reads this list first.

Three lines down, in the same block:

> **Linear under load.** The masking path is provably linear in both field *size* and entity *count*,
> and CPU-bound work runs off the async executor — **a large body can't stall the proxy for
> everyone.** *A proxy that is down protects nothing.*

Linear is still true. The conclusion drawn from it is not: [M10-R28](#m10-r28) measures a legal
15.6 MiB body holding a blocking-pool thread for **57 s** at the shipped default, 210× the same body
with the phone tier off. *Linear is a shape, not a budget* — and this bullet is where the project
tells its readers otherwise.

**Fix.** Add the budget refusal to the fail-closed list in both READMEs (one clause each, with what
to do about it, mirroring the message). Re-word the linearity bullet to claim what is measured —
linear in size and count, with the per-request CPU ceiling named once [M10-R28](#m10-r28) sets one.
Do not correct the second half before the first is fixed: a README that describes a bound the code
does not have is the failure this finding is about.

**Test that would have caught it.** None automated, and none is worth building. The check is the
milestone-done criterion already in `CLAUDE.md` — *"the affected docs reflect reality"* — applied to
the two files a user reads before any of the others.

**Fully closed (2026-07-30).** With [M10-R28](#m10-r28) landed there is a per-request CPU ceiling to
name, so the linearity bullet now claims what is measured in both READMEs: *linear is a shape, not a
budget* — the 15.6 MiB body that held a worker for **57 s** is refused in **0.19 s**, and the worst
case a single request can cost is **~1.4 s**. The order was the finding's own instruction and it was
worth following: written a round earlier, the sentence would have had to either restate the false
conclusion or invent a number.

**Half-closed first (2026-07-30), and the box stayed `[ ]` until R28 landed.** The omission was fixed
in the same pass as R31/R32/R34: both READMEs' fail-closed
list now names the budget refusal, says it is the only 400 an otherwise legal body can trigger, and
carries the same remedy the message does (retrying unchanged fails identically — shrink the field, a
`LIMIT` behind an oversized tool result). The linearity bullet is **untouched on purpose**, exactly as
this finding instructed: it claims a per-request CPU bound, and until [M10-R28](#m10-r28) sets one,
any wording I could write would either restate the false conclusion or invent a number. *Rewriting it
first would have been the failure this finding is about* — a README describing a bound the code does
not have. It reopens with R28 and closes with it.

<a id="m10-r34"></a>
### M10-R34 — PHONE-NAT-10's country-code aim names five regions and carries four · **low**

`src/pii/recognizers.rs:2292-2294`:

```rust
/// Calling codes of the regions that declare the `Groups` family (ES · IT · LV · PT · CN), as a
/// leading token the family's regex accepts (1–3 digits, non-zero first).
const GROUPS_OWNER_CALLING_CODES: [&str; 4] = ["34", "39", "86", "371"];
```

Five regions named, four codes: ES `34`, IT `39`, CN `86`, LV `371` — **PT `351` is missing.** The
band this array exists to reach is the one [M10-R21](#m10-r21) was written about, and its floor
(`FLOORS[3] = 10`, `recognizers.rs:2439`) is met with a measured ~17, so a fifth of the aim being
absent is not currently the difference between green and red. It is worth one line anyway, for the
reason M10-R21 is worth eight: this is the guard whose *power* the milestone has already had to
measure once, and its own doc overstates where it points.

**Fix.** `["34", "39", "86", "351", "371"]` and `[&str; 5]`, or drop PT from the comment. Re-run and
record the new per-shape count — if it moves, the floor's margin moves with it.

**Test that would have caught it.** The floor already reports the count on failure; nothing checks
the comment against the array. The cheap version is to derive the codes from `PHONE_REGIONS` rather
than restating them — the same "one home" discipline the rest of this milestone applied to numbers.

**Closed (2026-07-30) — and the array was the smaller half of it.** `["34", "39", "86", "351",
"371"]`, `[&str; 5]`. But adding PT changed **nothing**: the count stayed at exactly 17. The reason is
one line down at `recognizers.rs:2372`, which indexed the array `% 4` — **a restated literal, so the
fifth element was unreachable by construction**. It now indexes
`% GROUPS_OWNER_CALLING_CODES.len()`, and *that* is what moved the measurement.

Re-measured, deterministic and recorded next to the floors: `[355, 356, 330, **15**, 28, 234]` of
2400. Slot 3 went **17 → 15** — putting PT into the aim re-drew the whole seeded sequence — so the
margin over `FLOORS[3] = 10` narrowed from 7 to 5. Still clears; recorded because a margin that moved
once can move again, and the next person should see the real number rather than the round one.

> **The rule this leaves, and it is why a `low` was worth the eight lines.** *A collection and the
> modulus that indexes it are one fact; writing the length twice is how the second copy goes stale
> silently.* The finding caught the doc comment disagreeing with the array — the array **also**
> disagreed with its own index, and the only reason it was findable at all is that
> [M10-R21](#m10-r21) had forced this guard to report its power **per shape**. An aggregate count
> would have absorbed a whole missing country without a flicker, which is the same argument M10-R21
> made, arriving to collect.

<a id="review-5"></a>
## Round 5 — closure verification (2026-07-30)

**Three wrappers were made to forward the budget, and the detector that *mints* it was not.** Round
4's fix put one `Budget` on the request and threaded it through a new `_within` pair, and its own doc
comment names the hazard exactly: *"inheriting this default would route the call to plain `try_detect`
and hand the detector underneath a fresh allowance."* `CompositeDetector`, `CachingDetector` and
`FailOpen` each override **both** methods. `StructuredRecognizers` overrides `try_detect_within` and
**not** `redetect_within` — so every fixpoint pass after pass 0 goes trait-default →
`redetect` → `try_detect` → `Budget::new(500_000)`. One line, and it is the only detector in the tree
whose cost the budget exists to bound:

```text
try_detect_within(200 KB of distinct groups, Budget::new(1))  ->  Err          spent = 1
redetect_within (200 KB of distinct groups, Budget::new(1))  ->  Ok, 9,743 spans   spent = 0
```

That is [M10-R35](#m10-r35), and on the real binary at the completely shipped default it is a legal
**15.63 MiB body answering `200` in 17.2 s** — the same body with the phone tier off answers in
**0.36 s**. The published per-request ceiling is ~1.4 s. So [M10-R28](#m10-r28)'s closure and
[M10-R30](#m10-r30)'s do not hold: the allowance bounds **pass 0 of a request**, not a request.

**Round 4's own table looked at this line and asked the wrong question of it.** Its fail-open walk
(above, *The budget, attacked*) records: *"`redetect` on later fixpoint passes — `StructuredRecognizers`
does **not** override it, and the trait default is `self.try_detect(input)` … it propagates."* True,
and the property under test was error propagation. The same sentence is the whole mechanism of this
round's blocker, because `try_detect` does one other thing besides propagate: **it mints.** *A fact
established for one property is not established for another* — the note that says "does not override
it" is the note that should have said "and therefore gets a fresh allowance".

**Four of the seven round-4 closures hold**, each checked against the finding's own `file:line`s and
two of them against a **mutated** tree rather than a reading. Three new docs/measurement findings ride
along, all of them numbers the round-4 commits published: the headline **0.19 s refusal does not
reproduce** and contradicts the row above it in its own table ([M10-R36](#m10-r36)), both READMEs'
fail-closed bullet still describes the pre-R28 **field** unit ([M10-R37](#m10-r37)), and the
constant's own SQL table names a row DOS-BUD refutes ([M10-R38](#m10-r38)).

### Verdict per round-4 finding

| ID | Closure | How I checked |
|---|---|---|
| [M10-R28](#m10-r28) | **does NOT hold** | The `_within` seam exists and pass 0 really is charged once per request — I reproduced the fixed repro (78 × 200 KB distinct → REFUSED). But `StructuredRecognizers` never overrides `redetect_within`, so every later pass mints a fresh 500,000 — up to four of them per field (passes 1–3 plus the confirming `redetect`): measured `redetect_within(…, Budget::new(1))` → `Ok` with **spent = 0** and 9,743 spans, and a legal 15.63 MiB body → **`200` in 17.2 s** through the real `.exe`. [M10-R35](#m10-r35). |
| [M10-R29](#m10-r29) | **holds** | Only the phone validator charges. `free(…)` wraps all nine national-ID checksums and the card's Luhn (`recognizers.rs:67`), `budget.spend()` appears exactly once in the tree (`:668`, inside the `.any()`), so it is one unit per `parse()` and `.any()` short-circuits on accept. With `PII_LOCALES=` a 200 KB digit-dense field spends **0** units (DOS-BUD's own tier-off row) and the CFG-01 sibling is green. The memo charges a repeated candidate **once** and a distinct one **always** — 200 KB of odometer groups spends 48,820, 20 KB spends 4,880, linear. The *published rate* is what does not reproduce: [M10-R38](#m10-r38). |
| [M10-R30](#m10-r30) | **does NOT hold** | Its fix item 1 is *"the five fixpoint passes share one allowance instead of minting five"*. They do not. On a 100 KB field with one entity: pass 0 charged 48,775, pass 1 charged **0** and cost **48,775** (103.7 ms), and `mask_all_within` reports `spent = 48,775` for **two** full scans. Item 2's boundary figure (2 MiB / 499,380 units) reproduces; item 3 is genuinely fixed. Same finding: [M10-R35](#m10-r35). |
| [M10-R31](#m10-r31) | **holds** | `grep -rn "begin where a number begins" src/` is **empty**. Both named sites carry the version ARCHITECTURE carries — `recognizers.rs:673-679` (next to `shrink_on_reject`, the decision the false sentence defended) and `:856-862` (`shrink_to_a_valid_prefix`'s doc). `DEVLOG.md:228` keeps it, correctly, as history. |
| [M10-R32](#m10-r32) | **holds, and it is non-vacuous — proved by mutation** | In a throwaway worktree I replaced *"Retrying it unchanged will fail identically"* → *"Please try again later"* and *"add a LIMIT to"* → *"add a cap to"*. **E2E-05 fails** at `proxy_e2e.rs:920`, and `cargo test --test complexity` stays **green (8 passed)** — precisely the gap the finding was filed for. The record overstates the digit-run half, though: [M10-R40](#m10-r40). |
| [M10-R33](#m10-r33) | **does NOT hold** | Both halves landed, and both are now wrong. The linearity bullet publishes *"refused in 0.19 s"* and *"the worst case any single request can cost is ~1.4 s"* — the first does not reproduce ([M10-R36](#m10-r36)), the second is contradicted at 17.2 s ([M10-R35](#m10-r35)). And the fail-closed bullet it wrote a pass **earlier** still says *"a single field so digit-dense it exhausts the budget"* and prescribes *"shrink the field"*, which is the unit R28 then changed ([M10-R37](#m10-r37)). |
| [M10-R34](#m10-r34) | **holds, and I reproduced both sides of it** | `GROUPS_OWNER_CALLING_CODES: [&str; 5]` with `% GROUPS_OWNER_CALLING_CODES.len()` (`recognizers.rs:2387`, `:2464`). PHONE-NAT-10's per-shape counts, printed from a worktree build: **`[355, 356, 330, 15, 28, 234]` of 2400** — byte-for-byte the recorded numbers. Reverting the index alone to `% 4` gives **`[355, 356, 330, 17, 28, 234]`**, so the modulus really was what moved the measurement, exactly as the closure says. |

### The budget, attacked a second time

**1 · Is there a fourth wrapper, or a delegating detector, that inherits the default?** No wrapper —
`grep 'impl PiiDetector'` over `src/` gives six production impls: `StructuredRecognizers`,
`CompositeDetector`, `FailOpen`, `CachingDetector`, `OnnxNerDetector`, `GLiNerDetector`. The last two
hold no inner detector, and both **do** override `redetect` (`onnx.rs:752`, `gliner.rs:476`) to return
nothing, so their `redetect_within` default is correct *and* free. The wiring in `AppState::new`
(`server.rs:66-81`) composes `CachingDetector(CompositeDetector([structured, FailOpen(ner)?,
FailOpen(gliner)?]))` on `onnx` and `CachingDetector(CompositeDetector([structured]))` by default,
with the cache skipped entirely at `PII_CACHE_ENTRIES=0`. Every one of those four shapes forwards the
budget correctly down to `structured` — and then `structured` drops it on the `redetect` channel. **The
missed override is not a wrapper's; it is the leaf's, and the leaf is the one that mints.**

**2 · Does any request-side path still call the non-`_within` forms?** No. `mask_all` /
`try_detect` / `redetect` appear outside `#[cfg(test)]` only as the trait's own defaults and as
`StructuredRecognizers::try_detect`'s documented per-call entry point. `privacy.rs:761`'s `mask_all`
is inside `mod tests`. `Vault::mask_all` mints `Budget::new(MAX_PHONE_VALIDATIONS_PER_REQUEST)`, i.e.
a real ceiling rather than `unlimited` — good, and its neighbour's doc comment says the opposite
([M10-R39](#m10-r39)).

**3 · Can one budget cross two requests?** No, and the type system is what says so. `Budget` holds a
`std::cell::Cell`, so it is `!Sync`; `PrivacyStage::on_request` creates it as a **stack local**
(`privacy.rs:113`) and nothing stores it. The single-threaded assumption inside one request holds by
construction: `run_privacy_stages` (`server.rs:681-691`) runs the whole stage list inside **one**
`spawn_blocking`, and the field walk is a serial `for`. A shared budget would not compile.

**4 · `FailOpen`'s new asymmetry — the condition is right today, and it is right for a reason nobody
wrote down.** `Err(err) if budget.is_exhausted() => Err(err)` does not inspect the error; it reads a
*global side-condition*. It is nevertheless correct on every reachable path, because
`StructuredRecognizers::try_detect_within` returns `Err` **whenever** the budget is at zero
(`recognizers.rs:1023`, unconditional, after the scan) — even on PII-free text, which I checked —
and `CompositeDetector` `?`-propagates that before `FailOpen(ner)` is reached. So the invariant that
makes the guard sound is *no detector ever returns `Ok` with an exhausted budget*, and it is asserted
nowhere. Break it and the wrapper turns fail-**closed** on a genuine, unrelated ML failure: measured,
`FailOpen(AlwaysFails).try_detect_within("hello", &Budget::new(0))` → `Err("inference
unavailable")`. [M10-R41](#m10-r41), `low`, and the fix is mostly to write the invariant down.

**5 · The threshold and the test seam.** `Config::pii_max_phone_validations` is genuinely unreachable
from the environment: no `env::var` mentions it (`config.rs:188-190` says so in a comment and the code
agrees), and CLI-05 extracts keys by regex from a **tree walk** of `src/`, so it cannot drift for a
field that reads nothing. Lowering the allowance does not weaken any of the three guards that cross
it — DOS-07's solo field really does pass on its own (measured **4,880** units against its 20,000, a
4× margin) and its twenty together really are 4× over; E2E-05's 256 KB field exhausts 20,000 and the
assertion is about the message, not the number. What *does* rest on nothing is the shipped number's
own claim: DOS-BUD is the only thing that crosses 500,000 and it is `#[ignore]`d, so a regression in
the *size* of the ceiling is invisible to `cargo test` — which is how [M10-R35](#m10-r35) shipped.

### What I verified independently

- **Suite, both feature sets, no warnings.** `cargo test` → **217 passed / 0 failed / 5 ignored, zero
  warnings** (in a shell with `NO_COLOR` **unset**, so CLI-06 still asserts a property of the
  binary). `cargo test --features onnx --target-dir target/onnx` → **250 passed / 0 failed / 22
  ignored**. `cargo fmt --check` clean. `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean on **both**
  feature sets. `cargo doc --no-deps` → **15** warnings, every one pre-existing (M10 adds none).
- **DOS-BUD reproduced** (`cargo test --release --test complexity -- --ignored --nocapture
  budget_refusal_line`). Unit counts reproduce **exactly** — 31,210 / 124,890 / 249,720 / 499,380 per
  field size; 5,005 / 50,005 / 145,005 / **295,005** / 445,005 per SQL row count; 0 with the tier off.
  Wall clocks on this box run 1.5–2× ARCHITECTURE's (it was building `--features onnx` concurrently),
  which is disclosed variance rather than a finding — except the one row that is off by 16×
  ([M10-R36](#m10-r36)) and the one whose *units* disagree ([M10-R38](#m10-r38)).
- **The real `.exe`, end to end**, against a mock upstream that counts what it received, release
  profile, shipped default config, warmed. Every `200` was forwarded; on every `400` the upstream saw
  **zero** requests. The differential in [M10-R35](#m10-r35) was then re-run against a **patched**
  build of the same commit in a detached worktree — the same bodies, the same harness, the same box.
- **Two mutation experiments in a throwaway worktree** (`git worktree add … HEAD --detach`, removed
  afterwards; `git worktree list` shows only the checkout and `git status` is clean): stripping
  E2E-05's actionable clause (it fails; complexity stays green), and reverting M10-R34's modulus to
  `% 4` (slot 3 returns to 17). Both were run against **HEAD**, never the working tree.
- **The complexity wall clock, timed serially in debug** (`--test-threads=1 --nocapture`):
  splice 0.25 s · email 0.58 s · secret 0.92 s · arbitrary-groups 1.22 s · dense-real 1.40 s ·
  distinct-groups 1.41 s · **cards 1.84 s**. All below TESTING's published figures, so 30 s keeps a
  16× margin on the slowest linear case here and ≥9× on the box the note was written on, while the
  fastest quadratic on record (DOS-04's 52 s splice) is still 1.7× **past** it. **The widening holds
  and I could not construct a case it shelters.**
- **Things I tried and could not break.** No fourth wrapper, no delegating detector, and no
  request-side caller of the non-`_within` forms. The budget cannot cross a request (`!Sync`, stack
  local, one `spawn_blocking`). An error is still never cached. The `PII_CACHE_ENTRIES=0` and
  `--features onnx` wirings forward the budget identically. `PII_LOCALES=` still costs zero units.
  No refusal message carries input-derived text at any size I tried. `pii_max_phone_validations` is
  unreachable from the environment by any route. And the memo cannot double-charge or under-charge a
  candidate: a distinct candidate is charged once per region tried, a repeat is charged once in total,
  and the unit count is linear in field size across four sizes.
- **Environment.** Every port was OS-assigned (`bind(0)`) and asserted `!= 1234`; nothing bound,
  connected to, or otherwise touched **1234**. The only processes stopped were the proxy children
  this harness spawned, by handle.
