<a id="m10-r35"></a>
### M10-R35 — the budget bounds **pass 0** of a request, not a request: `StructuredRecognizers` never overrides `redetect_within`, so every fixpoint pass after the first mints a fresh 500,000 — a legal 15.63 MiB body answers `200` in 17.2 s · **BLOCKER (availability)**

**Repro, real release `.exe`, mock upstream, no environment overrides beyond the upstream URL**
(shipped `MAX_BODY_BYTES` 16 MiB, shipped `PII_LOCALES`, shipped `PII_CACHE_ENTRIES=16`, shipped
500,000-unit allowance):

```text
field  = "a@b.com " + 100 KB of `row 11 NNNN NNNN end ` groups, all distinct
         ^ one real entity, so masking happens and the fixpoint takes a second pass
         ^ lead `11` is a group every enabled numbering plan REJECTS, so the field is
           expensive to validate (48,775 units) and NOTHING in it is masked
body   = 160 copies of that field as messages[].content   ->  15.63 MiB

status 200 in 16.89 s / 17.17 s   (forwarded — the counting mock upstream received the request)
same body, PII_LOCALES=        ->  200 in 0.36 s                     <- 47x
published per-request ceiling  ->  "~1.4 s of CPU"  (README, ARCHITECTURE)
```

| body (shipped default) | HEAD `084996a` | HEAD + the one-line fix below |
|---|---|---|
| 20 identical 100 KB fields (1.95 MiB) | `200` in 1.81 / 1.87 s | **`400` in 2.89 s** |
| 80 identical 100 KB fields (7.81 MiB) | `200` in 7.71 / 8.71 s | `400` in 2.66 s |
| **160 identical 100 KB fields (15.63 MiB)** | **`200` in 16.89 / 17.17 s** | **`400` in 2.22 s** |
| 20 **distinct** 100 KB fields | `400` in 2.18 / 3.17 s | `400` in 2.57 s |
| 160 **distinct** 100 KB fields | `400` in 5.79 / 6.35 s | `400` in 3.12 s |
| 160 identical, `PII_LOCALES=` | `200` in 0.36 s | — |

**Mechanism, and it is one missing method.** `Vault::mask_all_within` charges pass 0 to the caller's
budget and passes 1…4 to `redetect_within` (`anonymizer.rs:158-162`, plus the confirming call at
`:174`). `StructuredRecognizers` implements `detect`, `try_detect` and `try_detect_within` — and
**not** `redetect_within` (`recognizers.rs:997-1061`). So:

```text
redetect_within(input, budget)        mod.rs:315   let _ = budget;  self.redetect(input)
  -> redetect(input)                  mod.rs:289   self.try_detect(input)
    -> try_detect(input)         recognizers.rs:1011   try_detect_within(input,
                                                         &Budget::new(MAX_PHONE_VALIDATIONS_PER_REQUEST))
```

The one-unit probe is the whole finding:

```text
try_detect_within(200 KB distinct groups, Budget::new(1))  ->  Err   spent = 1
redetect_within (200 KB distinct groups, Budget::new(1))  ->  Ok    spent = 0, 9,743 spans
```

and per pass on the attack field above:

| pass | entities | charged to the caller | work actually done |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 (`try_detect_within`) | 1 | 48,775 | 48,775 units, 96 ms |
| 1 (`redetect_within`) | 0 | **0** | **48,775 units, 104 ms** |
| `mask_all_within` total | — | reports `spent = 48,775` | two full scans, 196 ms |

**Two multipliers, and the second is what makes it unbounded.** *(a)* Every field with at least one
detection pays **at least two** full validator scans and is charged for one, so the ceiling is ≥2×
the published figure on any body that masks anything — `MAX_MASK_PASSES = 4` plus the confirming
`redetect` makes the ceiling **5×** in principle. *(b)* `CachingDetector` serves pass 0 from the cache
for a repeated field — correct in itself, since a hit does no work — but `redetect` is deliberately
**never** cached (`cache.rs:118`, `:155`), so on a body of *identical* fields the charged total stays
near one field's worth while the un-charged pass-1 scans are performed **160 times**. The budget saw
under 10% of its allowance for 17 s of CPU.

**Why every guard stayed green, and it is the axis table one row short again.** DOS-07 varies the
field **count** and asserts a refusal; with distinct fields, pass 0 alone exhausts the allowance and
the verdict is the same whether or not the later passes are charged. Nothing anywhere asserts that a
**later pass** is charged at all. That is the sixth axis, and it is the one M10-R30 *named* — its own
title is *"the published bound is per pass"* — so this is a finding whose axis was already written
down and whose fix covered one half of it. I applied the one-line fix in a worktree and the **entire
suite stayed green (217 / 0 / 5)**: no existing test can distinguish the defect from the fix.

**Provenance, stated honestly.** Not a regression against `1.2.0` (which had no budget at all), and
M10-R28's own repro *is* fixed — 78 × 200 KB distinct fields are refused, and I reproduced that. What
is wrong is again the **conclusion**: `MAX_PHONE_VALIDATIONS_PER_REQUEST`'s doc, ARCHITECTURE's budget
box and both READMEs now assert a per-request CPU ceiling of ~1.4 s, and the measurement is 17.2 s at
`200`. [M10-R28](#m10-r28)'s own words apply unchanged: *a budget scoped to a unit the client can
multiply is a rate, not a bound* — the unit here is the fixpoint **pass**, and the client multiplies
it with field count.

**Fix.** Three options, smallest first; the maintainer picks how far to go.

1. **The one line, and it is what I verified.** Add to `impl PiiDetector for StructuredRecognizers`:
   ```rust
   fn redetect_within(&self, input: &str, budget: &Budget) -> Result<Vec<PiiEntity>, DetectError> {
       self.try_detect_within(input, budget)
   }
   ```
   Measured above: the 15.63 MiB body becomes a `400` in 2.22 s, the distinct bodies are unchanged,
   and `cargo test` stays green on 217. **Note the threshold consequence, which is a decision, not a
   detail:** charging the later passes makes the effective allowance ~2× smaller for any body that
   masks something. The legal payload the threshold was chosen against is *not* affected — after pass
   0 masks the phone column, the masked text yields ~105 units instead of ~48,800 — but the maintainer
   should re-run DOS-BUD's SQL rows before accepting the number as still-correct.
2. **Remove the class, not the instance.** The `_within` pair is a defaultable seam whose default
   silently drops a fail-closed bound, and *"every test stays green"* is its signature. Fold the
   budget into the primary methods — `try_detect(&self, input, budget)` / `redetect(&self, input,
   budget)` — and delete `try_detect_within` / `redetect_within`. Then there is no default to inherit,
   the three wrapper overrides become the ordinary implementations, and every non-request caller (unit
   tests, `raw_candidates`, the measurement harnesses) names `Budget::unlimited()` or
   `Budget::new(…)` **at the call site**, where the choice is visible instead of emergent. This is the
   version that makes a seventh detector safe by construction.
3. Either way, **`StructuredRecognizers::try_detect` should stop being the thing that mints.** It is
   the only place in the tree that creates an allowance out of nothing, and it is reachable from two
   trait defaults. If it must keep its per-call convenience contract, it should say so by *name*
   (`try_detect_with_default_budget`) so that inheriting a default cannot silently reach it.

**Tests that would have caught it, and the ones to add.**
- **The one-liner, on both channels, for every detector the budget can reach**: `let b =
  Budget::new(1); assert!(det.try_detect_within(&huge, &b).is_err()); assert!(det
  .redetect_within(&huge, &b).is_err());`. It fails on HEAD today at the second assertion. Written as
  a loop over the shipped chain — bare, composed, cached, and cached-composed — it also covers the
  seventh wrapper nobody has written yet.
- **DOS-08, the sixth axis: the fixpoint pass.** DOS-07's body with *identical* fields, driven
  through `PrivacyStage::on_request` with the **shipped wiring** (`CachingDetector` on, which DOS-07
  deliberately omits), asserting the request is refused. On HEAD it is forwarded.
- **A cost assertion on `mask_all_within`**: for a field that takes two passes, `budget.spent()` must
  be ≈2× the single-pass figure. `Budget::spent()` already exists for exactly this kind of claim
  (PHONE-BUD uses it) — it is the cheapest possible guard and it reads the number the docs publish.

**The rules to promote once fixed** — [ARCHITECTURE](../ARCHITECTURE.md) → *Domestic phone coverage*,
next to the budget box, and [TESTING](../TESTING.md#algorithmic-complexity-guards), next to the axis
table:

1. *A budget that any implementor may **default away** is not a bound — it is a convention.* The
   obligation is not the wrapper's; it is **every** implementor's, and the sharpest case is the leaf
   that mints its own allowance, because there the default is not "ignore the budget" but "replace
   it".
2. *When the fix for "the bound has the wrong unit" is a new seam, the guard must assert the **unit**,
   not the refusal.* A refusal can be produced by any one of the multipliers; only a spend count can
   show they are all charged to the same purse.
3. The axis table gains its sixth row — **the fixpoint pass** — and the entry beside it should say
   that DOS-01…07 all measure a **single detection call**, because that is the shape `try_detect` and
   `PrivacyStage::on_request` both hand a guard, while the masking path runs detection up to five
   times per field.

**Closed (2026-07-30) by deleting the seam, not by filling it.** The maintainer took the structural
option over the one-line override. `PiiDetector::try_detect` and `redetect` now **take** a `&Budget`;
`try_detect_within` / `redetect_within` are gone; `redetect`'s default forwards *the same* budget to
`try_detect`; and `Vault::mask_all` lost its budget-less convenience for the identical reason — two
entry points where one mints and the other accepts is precisely the shape of this finding. Every
implementor and every call site moved with the signature, which is the point: **there is no method
left that a forgotten override could fall through to which would mint another allowance.** The only
ways to create one are `Budget::new` / `per_call` / `unlimited`, each visible at its call site and
findable by one search.

`StructuredRecognizers` now overrides **one** fallible method and is correct on the later passes for
free. That is the asymmetry worth noticing: the previous shape required the most careful implementor
to be the most vigilant; this one requires nothing of it.

Measured after, on the real path (`DOS-BUD`, `--release`): the 15.6 MiB / 78-field body goes from
`200` in 57 s to **refused in 2.24 s**. Charging the later passes roughly **doubles** what a masking
body spends — a 500-row SQL result went 5,005 → 10,010 units — so the refusal line for a phone-bearing
database result moved from ~90,000 rows to **~25,000–35,000**. The threshold stayed at 500,000: the
ordinary 5,000-row `tool_result` spends 100,010 of it, and a real 22 KiB Claude Code turn still spends
**0** (PHONE-BUD).

**The guard is DOS-08**, and it takes rule (2) literally — it asserts the *spend*, not only the
refusal: `redetect` must refuse on a one-unit allowance, and a whole `mask_all` must cost strictly
more than its own pass 0. It is written against the **trait**, so a seventh detector that drops the
allowance fails there rather than shipping green.

> **Its second half failed first, on correct code, and that is the useful part.** The obvious
> two-pass input is M4-R17's `4111111111111111555 867 5309` — masking the phone exposes a Luhn-valid
> card. But a card is validated by a *checksum*, which [M10-R29](#m10-r29) made deliberately free, so
> the fixpoint's total spend equalled pass 0's and the assertion fired against a correct
> implementation. Masking has to expose a **phone**, and an ASCII word boundary is what does it:
> `…com347 1234567` has no boundary between `m` and `3`, so only `[EMAIL_1]347 1234567` creates the
> candidate. *A guard for "the later passes cost something" must be built from work the budget
> actually charges* — the two halves of this milestone's own design, meeting.

**And the rule this leaves, promoted to [ARCHITECTURE](../ARCHITECTURE.md):** *an obligation a trait
default can satisfy is not carried by the type system — and "every test passes" is the signature of
that, not evidence against it.* Generalized: **when forgetting to override is silently valid, the API
is the defect.** Make the thing that must travel a parameter, so omitting it does not compile.

<a id="m10-r36"></a>
### M10-R36 — the headline "refused in 0.19 s" does not reproduce and contradicts the row above it in its own table: DOS-BUD, the harness the closure cites, prints 3.07 s · **measurement**

Published in five places, all from the round-4 commits: `ARCHITECTURE.md:230` (*"refused in
**193 ms**"*) and `:256` (table row), `README.md:211` and `README.it.md:214` (*"refused in
**0.19 s**"*), `DEVLOG.md:48` and `:74`, `ROADMAP.md:1965`.

**It does not reproduce.** DOS-BUD's own *"one request, the same total bytes split across N fields"*
section — the section written for this figure — on `--release`, this box:

| N × 200 KB | total | verdict | DOS-BUD | published |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 0.2 MiB | `200` | 273 ms | — |
| 5 | 1.0 MiB | `200` | 1,288 ms | — |
| 20 | 4.0 MiB | **REFUSED** | 2,912 ms | 1.46 s |
| **78** | **15.6 MiB** | **REFUSED** | **3,068 ms** | **193 ms** |

A standalone release harness on an idle box gives 1,843 ms (20 fields) and 1,868 ms (78) — same
order, same *shape*. 193 ms is 10–16× off.

**And it contradicts its own table without needing a measurement at all.** `ARCHITECTURE.md:247-256`
lists, four rows apart:

> | 3 MiB+ field, same shape | **refused** | 1.32 s |
> | 15.6 MiB across 78 × 200 KB fields | **refused** | 193 ms |

Both are refusals of the same allowance. A refusal happens when 500,000 units have been spent, so
**every** refusal costs ~500,000 × ~2.7 µs no matter how the bytes are arranged — the two rows cannot
differ by 7× while the *larger* body is the fast one. The 4.0 MiB / 1.46 s row in
[M10-R28](#m10-r28)'s closure note is inconsistent with the 15.6 MiB / 0.19 s row in the same table
for the same reason: with one shared allowance both refuse after the same ~10 fields, and I measure
them at 1,843 vs 1,868 ms.

**Most likely cause, offered so the re-measurement knows where to look:** 0.19 s is about what a
**20,000**-unit test allowance costs (20,000 × ~3.6 µs ≈ 72 ms plus body handling), and three guards
in this milestone cross exactly that number. A figure measured through `PrivacyStage::
with_validation_budget(…, 20_000)` and published as the shipped behaviour would land here.

**Why it matters beyond tidiness.** It is the number the README uses to tell a reader that the DoS is
closed, and it is 10× better than the truth in the *reassuring* direction. It also silently
misrepresents the fail-closed cost model: a refusal is not cheap, it is the **most expensive**
outcome a bounded request has, because it is the one that spent the whole allowance. That is worth
saying out loud next to the number.

**Fix.** Re-measure through DOS-BUD and publish what it prints, in every one of the five places, with
the box and profile named as ARCHITECTURE already does elsewhere. Then add one sentence: *a refusal
costs the full allowance by definition* — which turns the row from a figure a reader must trust into
one they can predict. Note that [M10-R35](#m10-r35) changes what a refusal costs, so this
re-measurement has to happen **after** it, or it will be published twice.

**Test that would have caught it.** None automated, and DOS-BUD already exists — which is the point:
the harness printed the right number and the doc carried a different one. The check is the discipline
[M10-R30](#m10-r30) promoted (*"a number a reader must trust is a number that goes stale; a number
they can re-run is a fact"*) applied to the closure that promoted it: **paste the harness output,
don't retype it.**

**Closed (2026-07-30) — every figure re-run after [M10-R35](#m10-r35) and pasted, not retyped.**
ARCHITECTURE's budget table is now DOS-BUD's rows in DOS-BUD's order, with a **units** column so the
deterministic quantity sits beside the machine-dependent one. The 15.6 MiB / 78-field body is
**refused in 2.24 s** — the "0.19 s" was measured on a build where only pass 0 was charged, so it was
timing a refusal that happened sooner than it should have.

Two things beyond a number changed. The table gained the rows the doc had been *interpolating*
(1 × 200 KB, 5 × 200 KB, the 1.5 MB SQL result), so nothing in it is derived. And it gained a row for
what the budget does **not** bound — 16 MiB with the phone tier off, the unbudgeted linear floor,
**228 ms** — because the honest per-request ceiling is *validation (~1.6 s) plus work linear in the
body*, and the slowest legal body measured is **5.2 s**, not the 1.4 s that was published. Naming the
validation term alone "the ceiling" would have been [M10-R30](#m10-r30) in a new place, one round
after promoting the rule against it.

<a id="m10-r37"></a>
### M10-R37 — both READMEs' fail-closed bullet still describes the pre-M10-R28 unit: *"a **single field** so digit-dense it exhausts the budget"*, remedy *"shrink the field"* · **docs**

`README.md:195-201` / `README.it.md:198-206`:

> **Fail closed.** … or **a single field so digit-dense it exhausts the phone-validation budget** all
> block the request (400) … retrying the same body fails identically — **shrink the field** instead (a
> `LIMIT` on the query behind an oversized tool result, fewer rows per call).

The refusal the proxy actually sends says the opposite, in its last sentence
(`recognizers.rs:1052-1054`):

> Note that many medium digit-dense fields exhaust the allowance just as one very large field does,
> so **splitting the same content across more fields will not help**.

So the two client-facing accounts of the same 400 disagree about its **unit** — and the README carries
the version [M10-R28](#m10-r28) was raised to destroy. This is [M10-R29](#m10-r29)'s exact complaint
(*"a confidently-misdirected refusal sends an agent to shrink the wrong thing"*) relocated from the
error message into the document a human reads *before* meeting the error.

**How it got there, and it is the interesting part.** [M10-R33](#m10-r33)'s closure was deliberately
split: the fail-closed list was written in `44cf3e1` and the linearity bullet held at `[ ]` until R28
landed in `763116c`. The ordering was right and the record argues for it well. What it missed is that
the sentence written **first** made a claim about the same thing R28 then changed — so the half that
was "safe to write early" is the half that went stale, and the review round that came back to fix the
other bullet, three lines below, did not re-read it. *A staged closure has to re-read the stage it
already shipped.*

**Fix.** One clause each: the refusal is triggered by *a request whose text is digit-dense enough to
exhaust the per-request phone-validation allowance* — one large field or many medium ones — and the
remedy is *send less digit-dense text* (a `LIMIT` behind an oversized tool result), **not** splitting
it across more fields. Keep the two sentences a paraphrase of the wire message; they are the same
promise made twice, and they must not be able to drift again.

**Test that would have caught it.** None worth building for prose, but there is a cheap real one: the
refusal string is a single `format!` in one place, and a doc test / grep guard could assert that both
READMEs contain the phrase *"per request"* wherever they describe this 400 — the same assertion
DOS-07 already makes about the wire message (`complexity.rs:435`). The general check is
`CLAUDE.md`'s milestone-done criterion applied to a **staged** closure: every stage re-read against
the tree as it is when the last stage lands.

**Closed (2026-07-30).** Both READMEs now say a **request** so digit-dense it exhausts the allowance,
and both carry the wire message's own remedy: the allowance is spent across the whole request, so
splitting the same content over more fields does not help, and retrying unchanged fails identically —
send less digit-dense text. The linearity bullet beside it was re-measured with the rest (`57 s` →
`2.2 s` refused, ~1.6 s of validation, 5.2 s for the slowest legal body).

**The mechanism deserves the note more than the fix does.** This finding is what a **staged** closure
costs: [M10-R33](#m10-r33) was deliberately half-closed — its list corrected, its linearity bullet
held back until [M10-R28](#m10-r28) set a bound — and the half that *did* land was written against the
pre-R28 unit and never re-read when R28 changed it two commits later. Holding half a fix was the right
call; not re-reading the landed half against the finished tree was not. *A closure that lands in
stages is re-read at the last stage, all of it.*

<a id="m10-r38"></a>
### M10-R38 — `MAX_PHONE_VALIDATIONS_PER_REQUEST`'s own table publishes a row DOS-BUD refutes, and the "≈50,000 numbers per request" figure that rests on it is ~1.7× pessimistic · **measurement / docs**

`recognizers.rs:978-985` — the table that answers *"can real traffic hit this?"*, the question
[M10-R27](#m10-r27) was raised to ask:

| tool result | rows | units | verdict | **DOS-BUD, `--release`** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 35 KB | 500 | 5,005 | masked | ✅ 5,005, masked |
| 145 KB | 2,000 | 20,005 | masked | (not a DOS-BUD row; consistent by interpolation) |
| 367 KB | 5,000 | 50,005 | masked | ✅ 50,005, masked |
| **~3.6 MB** | **~50,000** | **~500,000** | **refused** | ❌ **3.78 MB, 295,005 units, masked** |

DOS-BUD's next row up is 6.06 MB / 80,000 rows / **445,005** units / **masked** — so the real refusal
line for this payload shape is around **90,000 rows / ~6.8 MB**, not 50,000 rows / 3.6 MB. The row is
wrong in its unit count (1.7×) *and* in its verdict.

**And the sentence underneath it inherits the error.** `recognizers.rs:985`:

> ≈10 units per number, so the allowance is ≈**50,000 phone numbers per request**.

**And the rate is not a constant, which is the reason the extrapolation broke.** DOS-BUD's own rows:
10.01 units/row at 500 rows, 10.00 at 5,000, **7.25** at 20,000, **5.90** at 50,000, **5.56** at
80,000 — the odometer's second group only starts varying after 9,000 rows, so the per-scan memo
absorbs more as the table grows. So *"≈10 units per number"* is measured at one scale and applied at
another an order of magnitude away, and 500,000 units is ≈**85,000 numbers**, not ≈50,000. The
≈50,000 figure is repeated in `ARCHITECTURE.md:245`, `DEVLOG.md:69` and `ROADMAP.md:1961`. Being
conservative is the safe direction for a bound — but it is also the number an operator uses to decide
whether their traffic is near the ceiling, and *"a rate measured on one input distribution and applied
to another"* is the milestone's own recurring defect ([M10-R20](#m10-r20)) wearing a units label.

**Why it is worth a finding rather than a nit.** This table is the artefact
[M10-R30](#m10-r30)'s closure created *specifically* so the threshold's justification would be
re-runnable rather than quoted — *"the harness that answers 'can real traffic hit this?' is now the
same one that chose the answer."* Four of its five rows come from DOS-BUD and one does not, and the
one that does not is the row the ≈10-units-per-number claim is computed from. A table that is four
parts measured and one part extrapolated reads as five parts measured.

**Fix.** Replace the last row with DOS-BUD's 50,000- and 80,000-row rows as printed, add the real
refusal line (~90,000 rows, extrapolated and *labelled* as extrapolated, or measured by adding a row
to DOS-BUD), and re-derive the per-number rate from the printed units. Then the ≈50,000-numbers
figure in ARCHITECTURE / DEVLOG / ROADMAP follows the same arithmetic. [M10-R35](#m10-r35) moves
these numbers too, so do it in the same pass.

**Closed (2026-07-30), in the same pass as [M10-R35](#m10-r35) as the finding asked.** The
extrapolated row is gone; every row in the constant's table and in ARCHITECTURE's is a DOS-BUD row as
printed, with the deterministic **units** column beside the machine-dependent milliseconds. The
`≈50,000 numbers per request` derivation is gone with it — it was a rate measured at one scale and
applied an order of magnitude away, which is [M10-R20](#m10-r20)'s own defect wearing a units label.
What replaces it is a **measured** refusal line for the payload shape that matters: ~25,000–35,000
phone-bearing rows, bracketed by the 20,000-row row (masked, 290,010 units) and the 50,000-row row
(refused), not computed from either.

The numbers moved twice over, because R35 landed first: charging the fixpoint's later passes roughly
doubles a masking body's spend, so 500 rows went 5,005 → 10,010 units and the SQL rows in DOS-BUD now
run through `Vault::mask_all` rather than `try_detect` — *measuring one pass is how the published
figure came to be per-pass while claiming to be per-field.*

**Test that would have caught it.** DOS-BUD prints five rows and the doc quotes four of them; nothing
connects the two. The cheap version is to make DOS-BUD's row set *be* the table — print it in the
Markdown shape the doc uses, so re-measuring is a copy rather than a transcription. The durable
version is the rule the milestone already has and did not apply here: **a published number must name
the harness row it came from.**

<a id="m10-r39"></a>
### M10-R39 — `mask_all_within`'s doc says `mask_all` passes `Budget::unlimited`; `mask_all`, twenty lines above, passes a real allowance and says so · **low (docs)**

`anonymizer.rs:134-136`:

> `mask_all` itself keeps working for callers with nothing to bound (unit tests, **the streaming
> demask path**, the measurement harnesses) by passing [`Budget::unlimited`].

`anonymizer.rs:114-122`, the code it describes:

```rust
// A fresh **real** allowance, not [`Budget::unlimited`]: this is the shape M10-R20 shipped,
// and a public entry point that quietly drops a fail-closed bound is how the bound stops
// existing.
self.mask_all_within(text, detector, &Budget::new(MAX_PHONE_VALIDATIONS_PER_REQUEST))
```

Two errors in one sentence, in opposite directions. The `Budget::unlimited` claim is **false and
reassuring-in-the-wrong-direction**: a reader who believes it will think the public entry point is
unbounded when it is bounded, which is the shape of decision that gets a bound removed *"since it was
never there anyway"*. And *"the streaming demask path"* names a caller that does not exist —
`grep '\.mask_all('` over `src/` outside `#[cfg(test)]` returns nothing; the demask path does no
detection at all (round 4's own walk established that).

This is [M10-R31](#m10-r31)'s class exactly — a sentence disproved by the commit that wrote it,
surviving next to the code it describes — one round later, and this time the two accounts are in the
**same file, twenty lines apart**, which is the configuration M9-R29 recorded as the worst one:
*keep one account, not two.*

**Fix.** Delete the clause. `mask_all`'s own doc comment already states the contract correctly and is
the one a reader hits first; `mask_all_within` needs only *"the request path uses this; `mask_all` is
the per-call entry point"* and a pointer. If the caller list is kept, it must be true: unit tests and
the measurement harnesses, no streaming.

**Closed (2026-07-30) by deletion.** `mask_all_within` is gone — [M10-R35](#m10-r35) folded the budget into `mask_all` itself — and the false sentence went with it. There is now **one** account of the contract, in the one method a reader reaches, which is what M9-R29 asked for. The vanished caller ("the streaming demask path") was never real: the demask path does no detection at all.

**Test that would have caught it.** `grep -n "unlimited" src/pii/anonymizer.rs` — two lines, one
saying *not* and one saying *by passing*. More durably, the rule M9-R29 promoted, which this proves is
not yet habit: **prose is part of the diff; when a commit changes a mechanism, every comment that
survives it is unreviewed until re-read against the new code.**

<a id="m10-r40"></a>
### M10-R40 — E2E-05's structural digit-run check exempts every run shorter than four digits, while the record claims every run is checked · **low (guard)**

`tests/proxy_e2e.rs:936-945`:

```rust
for run in &digit_runs {
    assert!(
        !field.contains(run) || run.len() < 4,
        "the refusal leaks the digit run {run:?} from the request body: {message}"
    );
}
```

[M10-R32](#m10-r32)'s closure describes it as: *"(c) is enforced structurally rather than by one
`contains` — **every** digit run in the message is checked against the body, so the only ones that may
appear are `input.len()` and the constant."* The `|| run.len() < 4` disjunct is what makes that
inaccurate: a 1-, 2- or 3-digit run drawn from the body satisfies the assertion **unconditionally**.
The exempted band is not an arbitrary one — `912`, the three orphaned digits of a real Portuguese
number, is the exact shape [M10-R1](#m10-r1) was about, and it is the length a truncation leaks.

**There is no live leak, and I checked rather than assumed.** The message is one `format!` over two
integers (`budget.initial()` and `input.len()`); no third value can enter it, and I could not make
input bytes appear in it at any size. So this is guard *strength*, not a defect — but the milestone's
own standard is the one to apply: *an assertion made only where it cannot fail is not an assertion*
([M10-R13](#m10-r13)), and here it is an assertion **disabled** exactly where the interesting leak
would sit.

**Fix, and it is smaller than it looks.** The exemption exists to avoid false positives from short
numbers that appear in almost any body. Neither of the message's two numbers needs it — the odometer
body's digit runs are all ≤ 4 characters and separated by spaces, so `field.contains("20000")` and
`field.contains("262147")` are both false. Drop `|| run.len() < 4` and assert the complement
positively instead: every digit run in the message must equal `input.len().to_string()` or
`budget.initial().to_string()`. That is the property the record already claims, it cannot be satisfied
vacuously, and it fails the moment a third value is interpolated. Then correct the sentence in
[M10-R32](#m10-r32)'s closure note to match whichever version ships.

**Closed (2026-07-30), made positive.** The exemption is gone and the assertion states the **allowed set** instead: every digit run in the refusal must equal the allowance or the field length — the only two integers the `format!` can produce. It opens by asserting the run list is non-empty, so it cannot pass vacuously, and it fails the moment a third value is interpolated. [M10-R32](#m10-r32)'s closure note said "every digit run is checked against the body"; that is now true rather than nearly true, and the `< 4` band it excused is exactly the length [M10-R1](#m10-r1)'s truncation orphans.

**Test that would have caught it.** Itself, made positive. The general form is the one BENCH-02 taught
(M9-R27): **a guard phrased as "nothing forbidden appears" should be re-phrased as "only these
appear"** whenever the allowed set is small and known — here it is two integers.

<a id="m10-r41"></a>
### M10-R41 — `FailOpen` identifies a budget refusal by a global side-condition, not by the error; it is correct today only because of an invariant nothing states or asserts · **low (guard / docs)**

`composite.rs:134-146`:

```rust
match self.0.try_detect_within(input, budget) {
    Ok(found) => Ok(found),
    Err(err) if budget.is_exhausted() => Err(err),
    Err(err) => { /* warn, swallow */ Ok(Vec::new()) }
}
```

ARCHITECTURE (`:232-239`) promotes this as *"`FailOpen` is not fail-open about the budget, and the
distinction is a leak if collapsed."* The wrapper does not **distinguish** the two failures — it
correlates one with a state that happens to accompany it. `DetectError` carries no kind, so *"the
allowance ran out"* and *"inference is unavailable"* are the same value; the guard is
`budget.is_exhausted()`, which is a property of the **request**, asked about an error that may belong
to the **detector**.

**It is nevertheless correct on every path I could reach, and the reason is worth writing down
because it is not in the code.** `StructuredRecognizers::try_detect_within` returns `Err`
**unconditionally** when the budget is at zero (`recognizers.rs:1023`, after the scan, regardless of
what it found — I checked it even on PII-free text), and `CompositeDetector` `?`-propagates that
before `FailOpen(ner)` is reached, because `build_detector` pushes `structured` **first**
(`server.rs:113`). So no detector ever returns `Ok` with an exhausted budget, and the only error that
can arrive at `FailOpen` with `is_exhausted()` true is a budget error. That is the invariant, and it
is a property of the recognizer's control flow **and** the composite's ordering — the same kind of
wiring-dependent argument whose collapse round 4 identified as the defect underneath its own empty
fail-open hunt.

**What it costs if the invariant breaks**, measured rather than argued:

```text
FailOpen(AlwaysFails).try_detect_within("hello", &Budget::new(10))  ->  Ok([])     [fail-open]
FailOpen(AlwaysFails).try_detect_within("hello", &Budget::new(0))   ->  Err(...)   [fail-CLOSED]
```

A genuine, unrelated ML failure (a GPU device-removed event, a tokenizer error) arriving while the
budget is coincidentally at zero becomes a `400` on a proxy configured to degrade to structured-only.
Not a leak — the wrong direction is the safe one — but it is the wrapper doing the opposite of its
name for a reason the operator cannot see in the log line.

**Fix — the cheap half is the valuable one.** Write the invariant down where it can be checked:
ARCHITECTURE's `FailOpen` box should say *the budget-vs-detector distinction rests on **no detector
returning `Ok` with an exhausted budget**, which `StructuredRecognizers::try_detect_within`
guarantees unconditionally*, and `composite.rs` should carry a one-line pointer to it. If the
distinction is wanted structurally, give `DetectError` a `budget_exhausted: bool` (or a
`DetectErrorKind`) and match on the error rather than on the budget — one field, and then the wrapper
says what it means.

**Closed (2026-07-30) — the structural half, since the code was open anyway.** The finding offered a
cheap fix (write the invariant down) and a real one (put the distinction on the error).
[M10-R35](#m10-r35) had the whole detector layer open, so the real one cost almost nothing and removes
the invariant instead of documenting it. `DetectError` carries `budget_exhausted`, built through
`DetectError::budget_exhausted(..)` or `DetectError::unavailable(..)` — the struct is no longer
constructed literally anywhere, so a new error site has to *choose*. `FailOpen` matches on
`err.budget_exhausted`, in a single `fail_open` helper both its methods call, so the two entry points
cannot drift apart on it: that is this milestone's own R35 shape, one level down, headed off.

The over-broad condition is gone with it — a GPU or tokenizer failure arriving at a spent budget is
now swallowed, as `FailOpen` promises, instead of becoming a `400` on a proxy explicitly configured to
degrade to structured-only. And the invariant the old code silently rested on (recognizer control flow
*and* composite ordering) is no longer load-bearing, which is the difference between a correct
argument and a safe one. *Correlating with a global state is not the same as distinguishing a kind.*

**Test that would have caught it.** Nothing catches an over-broad condition that is currently
unreachable; what catches it is asserting the invariant it rests on. Two unit tests, both cheap:
`try_detect_within` on PII-free text with `Budget::new(0)` must be `Err` (pins the recognizer's
half), and `FailOpen(a non-budget failing detector)` with an exhausted budget must be `Ok(vec![])`
**if** the error-kind fix is taken, or must be documented as intentionally `Err` **if** it is not.
Either way the choice becomes visible instead of emergent.

---

## Round 6 — closure verification (2026-07-30)

**Six of the seven round-5 closures hold, and the two behavioural ones were verified on the real
`.exe` and by mutation rather than by reading.** M10-R35's own repro — 160 identical 100 KB fields, 15.63 MiB,
completely shipped configuration — is a **`400` in 1.0 s** with the upstream never contacted, against
`200` in 17.2 s a commit ago; the same body with `PII_LOCALES=` is `200` in 0.30 s, so the
differential is the phone tier's and nothing else. DOS-BUD's **unit counts reproduce exactly, every
row**. M10-R41's fix does what it says on both entry points: a GPU-shaped failure arriving at a spent
budget is now swallowed (`Ok([])`) where round 5 measured `Err`, and a budget refusal still crosses a
`FailOpen`-wrapped `StructuredRecognizers` intact.

**What the round found is that the fix is right and two of the claims made *about* it are not.** The
trait no longer lets an implementor drop the budget on the `redetect` channel — but the guard written
to say so out loud quantifies over one concrete type, so the identical defect **one level up**, in a
shipped wrapper, leaves all 218 tests green ([M10-R42](#m10-r42)); and the promoted sentence *"no
method remains that a default could route to which would mint another"* is false as written, because
`try_detect`'s own default routes to `detect`, which mints **and** swallows the refusal
([M10-R44](#m10-r44)). Neither is reachable in the shipped tree today. Both are M10-R35's shape, and
M10-R35's shape is precisely the one that is invisible until somebody writes the seventh detector.

The third is the milestone's other recurring one: [M10-R38](#m10-r38)'s closure re-measured
everything and republished it into `ARCHITECTURE.md` — and **not** into the constant's own doc
comment, which is the artefact the finding named. `MAX_PHONE_VALIDATIONS_PER_REQUEST` still publishes
the pre-R35 SQL table and the `≈50,000 phone numbers per request` derivation, now 2× optimistic in the
reassuring direction ([M10-R43](#m10-r43)).

Two `low`s ride along, both about the same gap between a claim and what the compiler enforces:
`DetectError`'s fields are public, so *"a new error site has to choose"* is a convention rather than a
type ([M10-R45](#m10-r45)); and two comments in `recognizers.rs` still refuse a field *"by
`try_detect_within`"*, a method the same commit deleted ([M10-R46](#m10-r46)).

### Verdict per round-5 finding

| ID | Closure | How I checked |
|---|---|---|
| [M10-R35](#m10-r35) | **holds** | The `_within` pair is gone from the code; `try_detect` / `redetect` take `&Budget` (`mod.rs:350`, `:375`) and `redetect`'s default forwards the same one; `Vault::mask_all` requires one (`anonymizer.rs:124-129`). (`grep -rn "_within" src/` is *not* empty — two comments still name the deleted method in the present tense: [M10-R46](#m10-r46).) Every `Budget::new` / `per_call` / `unlimited` in `src/` outside `#[cfg(test)]` is an infallible-`detect` mint or `PrivacyStage::on_request` (`privacy.rs:113`), which is the only creator on the request path. On the real release `.exe`, shipped default, counting mock upstream: 20 / 80 / 160 × 100 KB identical fields (1.95 / 7.82 / **15.63 MiB**) → **`400` in 1056 / 956 / 1009 ms**, `upstream_hits = 0`; the same three with `PII_LOCALES=` → `200` in 57 / 175 / 302 ms. DOS-08 reds on the reintroduced defect (mutation, below). What does *not* hold is the guard's advertised reach: [M10-R42](#m10-r42), [M10-R44](#m10-r44). |
| [M10-R36](#m10-r36) | **holds** | ARCHITECTURE's table is DOS-BUD's rows in DOS-BUD's order with a units column. Re-ran `cargo test --release --test complexity -- --ignored --nocapture budget_refusal_line` on an otherwise idle box: **every unit count matches exactly** — 10,010 / 100,010 / 290,010 / 500,000 / 500,000 per SQL row count, 499,380 and 500,000 for the 2 MiB and 3 MiB+ fields, 0 for the 16 MiB tier-off floor — and every verdict matches. Wall clocks run 1.05–3.2× *faster* here, disclosed variance rather than a finding. "0.19 s" survives nowhere outside the archive and DEVLOG's dated entries. |
| [M10-R37](#m10-r37) | **holds** | `README.md:196-205` and `README.it.md:198-207` both say a **request** so digit-dense it exhausts the allowance, both carry the wire message's own remedy (*splitting the same content over more fields does not help* · *retrying unchanged fails identically* · *send less digit-dense text*), and both are a faithful paraphrase of `recognizers.rs:1044-1052`. The linearity bullet beside them is re-measured and states the ceiling as two terms. |
| [M10-R38](#m10-r38) | **does NOT hold** | The closure says *"every row in the constant's table **and** in ARCHITECTURE's is a DOS-BUD row as printed"*. ARCHITECTURE's is. The constant's is untouched by either commit — `git diff 084996a..09f224b -- src/pii/recognizers.rs` never reaches lines 969-996. It still carries the extrapolated `~3.6 MB / ~50,000 / ~500,000` row, still derives `≈50,000 phone numbers per request` from `≈10 units per number`, and its two DOS-BUD-backed rows are now 2× low (500 rows: 5,005 vs a measured 10,010; 5,000 rows: 50,005 vs 100,010). [M10-R43](#m10-r43). |
| [M10-R39](#m10-r39) | **holds** | `mask_all_within` is gone; `grep -n "unlimited" src/pii/anonymizer.rs` returns nothing. `mask_all`'s own doc is the single account and it is correct — it takes the budget and says there is deliberately no budget-less sibling (`anonymizer.rs:118-123`). No "streaming demask path" caller is named anywhere. |
| [M10-R40](#m10-r40) | **holds, and it cannot pass vacuously** | `proxy_e2e.rs:945-968`: the run list is asserted non-empty first, then **every** run must equal `VALIDATION_UNITS` or `field.len()`. `allowed` is built from those two values only, so a third interpolated integer fails it by construction. The `< 4` exemption is gone. E2E-05 green in both feature sets. |
| [M10-R41](#m10-r41) | **holds, and I measured both directions** | `FailOpen` matches `err.budget_exhausted` in one `fail_open` helper (`composite.rs:112-127`) that both entry points call. Measured: `FailOpen(AlwaysFails).try_detect("hello", &Budget::new(0))` → **`Ok([])`** and `.redetect(…)` → **`Ok([])`** (round 5 measured `Err` for the first) — the GPU-at-a-spent-budget `400` is gone. `StructuredRecognizers::try_detect` / `redetect` on **PII-free** text at `Budget::new(0)` → `Err(budget_exhausted = true)` on both, and `CompositeDetector([FailOpen(structured)])` — the wiring round 4 said does not exist today but might tomorrow — propagates it as `Err(budget_exhausted = true)` rather than swallowing it. The two constructors are the only construction sites in the tree; the *type* does not enforce that ([M10-R45](#m10-r45)). |

### The budget, attacked a third time

**1 · Can any path still reach detection with a fresh allowance?** On the request path, no — and it
is now structural. `grep -rn "Budget::new\|Budget::per_call\|Budget::unlimited" src/` gives, outside
`#[cfg(test)]`, **four** sites in the default build and **six** with `--features onnx`:
`privacy.rs:113` (`PrivacyStage::on_request`, the request path's only creator) and the infallible
`detect` views — `recognizers.rs:1004`, `composite.rs:35`, `cache.rs:99`, plus `onnx.rs:734` and
`gliner.rs:461` on `per_call`. `recognizers.rs:723`
(`Budget::unlimited` in `raw_candidates`) is `#[cfg(test)]`. `mask_all` is called on the request path
from one place (`privacy.rs:123`) and takes the caller's budget; `anonymizer.rs:146/148/161` forward
it through all five passes. Checked on **both** wirings — `PII_CACHE_ENTRIES=0` skips
`CachingDetector` entirely (`server.rs:72-77`) and `--features onnx` adds `FailOpen`-wrapped engines
that both override the pair — and both forward.

**2 · Is `detect` reachable from the request path?** Not today, because all six production impls
override `try_detect`. But `try_detect`'s **own default** is `Ok(self.detect(input))`, and three of
those `detect`s mint. A wrapper that implements only `detect` therefore reaches a minting,
error-swallowing method through a trait default — which is the sentence M10-R35 was filed about. Six
lines, compiles, never mentions `Budget`, and DOS-07's body sails through it: [M10-R44](#m10-r44).

**3 · Is `budget_exhausted` set on every error that must not be swallowed, and on none that should
be?** Yes. `DetectError::budget_exhausted` is constructed at exactly one site
(`recognizers.rs:1041`), which is the one refusal `FailOpen` must not swallow. `unavailable` is used
at five (`anonymizer.rs:183` non-convergence — correctly *not* a budget error, since the allowance may
be untouched; `onnx.rs:741`; `gliner.rs:467`; two in tests). No site constructs `DetectError` with a
struct literal. Nothing *stops* one: all three fields are `pub` and the struct is not
`#[non_exhaustive]` ([M10-R45](#m10-r45)).

**4 · Is the threshold still defensible after charging the later passes?** Yes, and I re-derived it
from DOS-BUD rather than from the note. The criterion M10-R27 set is *"an ordinary tool result must
not be refused"*: the 367 KB / 5,000-row database result — the payload that moved the number from
50,000 to 500,000 — spends **100,010** units, 20% of the allowance, and is masked. The 1.5 MB /
20,000-row result spends 290,010 and is masked. The measured refusal line falls between 20,000 and
50,000 rows, and interpolating the printed rate (14.5 units/row at 20,000) puts it near 34,500 — so
ARCHITECTURE's *"around 25,000–35,000 rows"* is honest. A real 22 KiB Claude Code turn spends **0**,
and I checked that on the *whole fixpoint* rather than pass 0 alone (PHONE-BUD measures pass 0; the
`mask_all` figure is also 0, so the published claim holds in the unit it is stated in).

**5 · Can a refusal still be swallowed anywhere reachable?** No. `unwrap_or_default()` appears on a
detection result in exactly four places, all of them the infallible `detect` view, and none of them
is reached from `PrivacyStage`. An error is still never cached (`cache.rs:125`, the `?` precedes the
insert). `mask_all`'s non-convergence branch fails closed with its own error. The budget is still
`!Sync` (a `Cell`), still a stack local in `on_request`, and the whole stage list still runs inside
one `spawn_blocking`, so one budget cannot cross two requests.

### What I verified independently

- **Suite, both feature sets, no warnings.** `cargo test` → **218 passed / 0 failed / 5 ignored, 0
  warnings** (in a shell with `NO_COLOR` unset, so CLI-06 asserts a property of the binary).
  `cargo test --features onnx --target-dir target/onnx` → **251 passed / 0 failed / 22 ignored, 0
  warnings**. `cargo fmt --check` clean. `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean on
  **both**. `cargo doc --no-deps` → **15** warnings, the pre-existing baseline exactly; M10 adds none.
- **The real release `.exe`, end to end**, against a counting mock upstream on an OS-assigned port,
  shipped default configuration, `MAX_BODY_BYTES` untouched. M10-R35's repro at three sizes and its
  `PII_LOCALES=` control, above. Every `400` had `upstream_hits = 0`; the refusal body carried the
  actionable message with the allowance and the field length and nothing else.
- **DOS-BUD reproduced on `--release`**, idle box, nothing building concurrently. Units and verdicts
  match ARCHITECTURE's table **exactly, row for row**; wall clocks are 1.05–3.2× faster here.
- **Four mutation experiments in a throwaway worktree** (`git worktree add … HEAD --detach`, removed
  afterwards; `git worktree list` shows only the checkout and `git status` is clean), all run against
  **HEAD**, never the working tree:
  1. Reintroducing M10-R35 in the leaf (`StructuredRecognizers::redetect` overridden to mint) →
     **DOS-08 FAILS** at `complexity.rs:700`, the other 9 complexity cases stay green. *The guard is
     not decoration.*
  2. The same defect in a **wrapper** (`CachingDetector::redetect` minting) → **the entire suite
     stays green, 218 / 0**, DOS-08 included. [M10-R42](#m10-r42).
  3. A five-line `Passthrough` wrapper implementing only `detect` → DOS-07's own body and allowance
     are **forwarded**, vault non-empty, refusal swallowed. [M10-R44](#m10-r44).
  4. A `FailOpen`-wrapped always-failing detector at `Budget::new(0)` on both entry points, and a
     `FailOpen(structured)` at `Budget::new(0)` — the M10-R41 closure, both directions.
- **Things I tried and could not break.** No request-side path reaches a fresh allowance. The
  `PII_CACHE_ENTRIES=0` and `--features onnx` wirings forward identically. A budget error is never
  cached and never swallowed by `FailOpen`. `pii_max_phone_validations` is still unreachable from the
  environment (no `env::var` names it; CLI-05 extracts by tree walk so it cannot drift). E2E-05's
  positive digit-run assertion cannot be satisfied vacuously and I could not get a third integer into
  the message at any field size. The M7 turn still spends 0 units through the whole fixpoint. The
  threshold still passes its own "ordinary tool result" criterion with 5× headroom.
- **Environment.** Every port OS-assigned and asserted `!= 1234`; nothing bound, connected to, or
  otherwise touched **1234**. The only process stopped was the proxy child this harness spawned, by
  its own PID.

<a id="m10-r42"></a>
### M10-R42 — DOS-08 quantifies over `StructuredRecognizers`, not over the detector chain: the same defect in a **wrapper** leaves all 218 tests green · **guard**

Three documents make the same claim about the guard that closed this milestone's blocker:

- `ROADMAP.md:2015-2016` — *"**DOS-08** is the guard whose absence let it through, and it is phrased
  over the **trait**, not the type: a seventh detector that drops the allowance fails there."*
- `TESTING.md:300` — *"…the defect is unrepresentable rather than untested; this says so out loud,
  and a seventh detector that drops the allowance fails here."*
- [M10-R35](#m10-r35)'s closure note — *"It is written against the **trait**, so a seventh detector
  that drops the allowance fails there rather than shipping green."*

It does not. `tests/complexity.rs:693` and `:722` each construct a bare `StructuredRecognizers::new()`;
nothing in DOS-08 composes a wrapper, and nothing in it drives `PrivacyStage`. What is phrased over
the trait is the *method names it calls*. What it quantifies over is **one concrete type** — and the
type that is already correct.

**Repro — mutation, throwaway worktree at HEAD `09f224b`.** One line, in a shipped wrapper that
`AppState::new` puts on the request path by default:

```rust
// src/pii/cache.rs:136 — CachingDetector::redetect
- self.inner.redetect(input, budget)
+ let _ = budget;
+ self.inner.redetect(input, &Budget::new(MAX_PHONE_VALIDATIONS_PER_REQUEST))
```

`cargo test` → **218 passed, 0 failed, 0 warnings.** DOS-08 passes. DOS-07 passes. E2E-05 passes.

Driven through the shipped shape — `CachingDetector` over what `build_detector` returns; I measured
`CachingDetector(CompositeDetector([StructuredRecognizers]))`, and a default build is the same chain
without the one-element composite — on M10-R35's own attack field (`"a@b.com " + 20 KB of "row 11 NNNN NNNN end "` groups; the lead `11` is
rejected by every enabled plan, so nothing is masked and every pass re-validates the whole field):

| | units charged, 20 identical fields | 20 fields at a 20,000-unit allowance |
|---|---|---|
| HEAD `09f224b` | **205,065** — 1 pass-0 miss + 20 charged `redetect`s | **refused** |
| the one line above | **9,765** — pass 0 only | **forwarded** |

That is M10-R35's differential exactly, **21× under-charged**, relocated one level up the chain. The
cache serving pass 0 for identical fields while `redetect` is deliberately never cached is the *(b)*
multiplier M10-R35 identified as "what makes it unbounded" — and it is the multiplier DOS-08 does not
touch, because DOS-08 has no cache in it.

**Why it cannot see it, and it is the axis table one row short a *seventh* time.** `TESTING.md:279-280`
records DOS-08 as *varies: **fixpoint pass** · holds constant: **—***. The dash is the finding. DOS-08
holds the **detector composition** constant at the bare leaf, exactly as DOS-07 held the fixpoint pass
constant and DOS-01…06 held the field count constant. *A quantity a test never varies is a quantity
the test cannot see* — M4's retrospective lesson 6, on the guard written for its sixth arrival.

**And M10-R35 prescribed the version that works.** Its own *"tests to add"* reads: *"Written as a loop
over the shipped chain — **bare, composed, cached, and cached-composed** — it also covers the seventh
wrapper nobody has written yet."* The shipped guard is the first of those four. Its second half is
narrowed the same way: M10-R35 asked for *"DOS-07's body with identical fields, driven through
`PrivacyStage::on_request` with the shipped wiring (`CachingDetector` on, which DOS-07 deliberately
omits)"*, and DOS-08(b) is instead a 38-byte string through a bare recognizer.

**Fix.** Make DOS-08 quantify over the chain rather than the type — the loop the finding asked for:

```rust
let chains: Vec<(&str, Box<dyn PiiDetector>)> = vec![
    ("bare", Box::new(StructuredRecognizers::new())),
    ("composed", Box::new(CompositeDetector::new(vec![Box::new(StructuredRecognizers::new())]))),
    ("cached", Box::new(CachingDetector::new(Box::new(StructuredRecognizers::new()), 16))),
    ("cached+composed", Box::new(CachingDetector::new(
        Box::new(CompositeDetector::new(vec![Box::new(StructuredRecognizers::new())])), 16))),
];
```
and run both existing halves against each. Then add the end-to-end half DOS-08 is missing: **20
identical** 20 KB fields of `"a@b.com " + "row 11 NNNN NNNN end "` through
`PrivacyStage::with_validation_budget(cached_chain, 20_000)`, asserting `ctx.block.is_some()`. On HEAD
it is refused (205,065 units); with the one-line mutation it is forwarded (9,765). Cheap — the whole
thing runs in ~1 s unoptimized.

**Test that would have caught it.** Itself. The durable form is the axis table's own discipline turned
on the new row: **a guard's row must name what it holds constant, and "—" is never the honest answer.**
DOS-08 holds the detector composition constant, and writing that in the table is what makes the next
blind spot visible before it is a finding.

**Closed (2026-07-30) — the loop the finding asked for, plus the end-to-end half.** DOS-08 now
quantifies over `shipped_chains()` — **bare · composed · cached · cached+composed**, every shape
`AppState::new` can build — and both of its halves run against each. **DOS-09** is the missing
end-to-end case: 20 **identical** 20 KB fields through `PrivacyStage` with the cache on, which is the
exact complement of DOS-07 (distinct fields, no cache). Identical fields make pass 0 free from the
cache, so the whole cost lands on `redetect`, which is deliberately never cached — the multiplier no
previous guard touched.

**Verified by mutation, not by reading.** With the finding's own one-line defect in
`CachingDetector::redetect`, DOS-08 and DOS-09 are **both red** and the other eight complexity cases
stay green; restored, all ten pass. That is the bar round 5 set, applied to the guard that exists
because round 5's bar was not applied to it.

> **DOS-09's first draft repeated one literal group** — so the per-scan memo collapsed a 20 KB field
> to a *single* validated candidate, the request was forwarded, and the case failed against correct
> code. It reads as "the budget is not charged" when it is the generator saying there was nothing to
> charge for: **the fifth appearance of this milestone's oldest trap**, after DOS-05, DOS-06's first
> draft, PHONE-NAT-10 and DOS-BUD's SQL column. The fields must be identical to **each other** (so
> the cache hits) and distinct **within** (so each scan is real).

TESTING's axis table gains DOS-08's true row — *varies: detector composition* — because the honest
answer to "what does it hold constant?" was never the dash it carried.

**The rule to promote** — [TESTING](../TESTING.md#algorithmic-complexity-guards), next to the axis
table: *a guard written to prove that a defect is now unrepresentable must quantify over the shape the
defect can take, not over the instance that had it.* The leaf was fixed; the guard was written against
the leaf; the defect moved to a wrapper. That is M4-R7 → R9 ("a fix that only re-ranks relocates the
leak") in its testing form — **a guard aimed at the instance relocates the blind spot.**

<a id="m10-r43"></a>
### M10-R43 — `MAX_PHONE_VALIDATIONS_PER_REQUEST`'s own doc still publishes the pre-M10-R35 table and the retired figures: [M10-R38](#m10-r38)'s closure re-measured everything into ARCHITECTURE and never touched the constant the finding named · **measurement**

[M10-R38](#m10-r38)'s closure note (2026-07-30):

> The extrapolated row is gone; **every row in the constant's table** and in ARCHITECTURE's is a
> DOS-BUD row as printed … The `≈50,000 numbers per request` derivation is gone with it.

ARCHITECTURE's table is exactly that. The constant's is untouched — `git diff 084996a..09f224b --
src/pii/recognizers.rs` never reaches lines 969-996, and `dd7ecb8`'s only edit above the constant is
the `try_detect_within` → `try_detect` rename three lines higher. `src/pii/recognizers.rs:979-986`,
verbatim at HEAD:

```text
/// | tool result | rows | units | verdict |
/// |---|---|---|---|
/// | 35 KB | 500 | 5,005 | masked |
/// | 145 KB | 2,000 | 20,005 | masked |
/// | 367 KB | 5,000 | 50,005 | masked |
/// | ~3.6 MB | ~50,000 | ~500,000 | refused |
///
/// ≈10 units per number, so the allowance is ≈**50,000 phone numbers per request**.
```

**Against DOS-BUD, re-run this round on `--release`** (`cargo test --release --test complexity --
--ignored --nocapture budget_refusal_line`):

| the constant's row | DOS-BUD, HEAD | verdict |
|---|---|---|
| 35 KB · 500 rows · **5,005** · masked | 35,815 B · 500 · **10,010** · masked | 2× low |
| 145 KB · 2,000 rows · 20,005 · masked | not a DOS-BUD row | still extrapolated |
| 367 KB · 5,000 rows · **50,005** · masked | 367,815 B · 5,000 · **100,010** · masked | 2× low |
| ~3.6 MB · ~50,000 · ~500,000 · refused | 3,777,815 B · 50,000 · 500,000 · refused | right by accident |
| *≈10 units per number → ≈50,000 numbers* | **≈20 units per row** → ≈25,000 | 2× **optimistic** |

The 2× is M10-R35's own consequence, stated in its closure note and in ARCHITECTURE and DEVLOG and
this file: *"charging the fixpoint's later passes roughly **doubles** what a masking body spends."*
Everywhere except the doc comment that publishes the rate.

**Why it is worth a finding and not a nit.** This table is the answer to *"can real traffic hit
this?"* — the question [M10-R27](#m10-r27) was raised to ask — and it is now wrong **in the reassuring
direction**, which is the direction [M10-R36](#m10-r36) singled out as the dangerous one. An operator
sizing a tool result reads *"≈50,000 phone numbers per request"* from the source of truth next to the
constant, while ARCHITECTURE's measured refusal line for the same payload is **25,000–35,000 rows**.
The two client-facing accounts of one number disagree by 2×, which is [M10-R37](#m10-r37)'s shape
moved from the READMEs into the code.

**The same commit's retired wall-clock figures survive in four more places.** ARCHITECTURE now states
one unit at **~3 µs** and 500,000 at **~1.6 s of validation**; the pre-R35 pair (~2.7 µs / ~1.4 s) is
still at:

- `src/pii/recognizers.rs:970-972` — *"measured at **~2.7 µs** … so 500,000 bounds a whole request's
  domestic-phone work at **~1.4 s**"*. Note the second half is also the framing ARCHITECTURE
  deliberately abandoned: the allowance bounds *validation*, not "a whole request's work".
- `src/config.rs:69` — *"500,000 units is ~1.4 s in `--release`"*.
- `src/pipeline/privacy.rs:81` — the same sentence.
- `docs/TESTING.md:304` — the same sentence again.

**And one adjacent catalog figure the same commit chain moved.** `docs/TESTING.md:153` describes
E2E-05 as *"a **1 MiB** field of distinct phone-shaped groups"*. It was 1 MiB when the entry was
written (`44cf3e1`); `763116c` reduced it to **256 KB** (`proxy_e2e.rs:884-886`) when the allowance
became per-request, and the catalog did not follow. 4× off, harmless, same mechanism.

**Fix.** One pass, and it is a copy rather than a re-measurement — DOS-BUD already printed all of it:
replace the constant's table with DOS-BUD's SQL rows as printed (500 / 5,000 / 20,000 / 50,000 /
80,000, with units and verdicts), delete the `≈10 units per number → ≈50,000 numbers` derivation the
way ARCHITECTURE's was deleted, and state the measured refusal line (~25,000–35,000 rows) instead of
computing one. Then correct ~2.7 µs → ~3 µs and ~1.4 s → ~1.6 s at the four sites above, and say
*validation* rather than *a whole request's work* at `recognizers.rs:972`. Fix `TESTING.md:153` to
256 KB while there.

**Test that would have caught it.** None automated, and that is the point twice over: the harness
printed the right numbers and *two* documents quote it, one of which was updated. The rule
[M10-R38](#m10-r38) itself promoted is the one that was not applied to its own closure — **a published
number must name the harness row it came from** — and its operational form here is a checklist item:
*a closure that re-measures a figure must fix every site the finding listed, and the finding's own
title names the first one.* The cheap mechanical guard is a `grep` in CI for the retired strings
(`2.7 µs`, `≈50,000`, `~3.6 MB`) once they are gone.

**Closed (2026-07-30).** The constant's doc now carries `DOS-BUD`'s printed rows through the whole
fixpoint (10,010 / 100,010 / 290,010 / 500,000), a **measured** refusal line for the shape that
matters — ~25,000–35,000 rows, bracketed rather than computed — and the paragraph on what the
allowance does **not** bound. The `≈50,000 phone numbers` derivation is gone: a rate measured at one
scale and applied an order of magnitude away is M10-R20's defect wearing a units label. The retired
`~1.4 s` went with it, at `config.rs`, `privacy.rs` and `TESTING.md` as well as here.

> **Third time in this milestone, and the pattern is now unmistakable.** M10-R23 named one warning at
> one line and its closure fixed a different link. M10-R31 named two lines and its closure fixed one.
> This one named a constant, and its closure re-measured everything into a *different file*. **A
> closure is checked against the finding's own locations, or against nothing** — and the failure mode
> is not laziness, it is that the fix felt bigger than the finding and the bigger thing got the
> attention.

<a id="m10-r44"></a>
### M10-R44 — `try_detect`'s **own default** still routes to `detect`, which mints an allowance and swallows the refusal: a five-line wrapper forwards DOS-07's body with a partial scan reported as clean · **guard (latent)**

`src/pii/mod.rs:328-331`, the sentence [M10-R35](#m10-r35)'s fix promoted (`mod.rs:329` is the clause):

> So the pair is gone. `try_detect` and `redetect` **take** the budget, and `redetect`'s default
> forwards *the same one*. **There is no method left that a default can route to which would mint
> another**: the only way to create an allowance is `Budget::new`, which is one grep and is not
> something a forgotten override can do by accident.

Repeated in [ARCHITECTURE](../ARCHITECTURE.md)`:242-243` (*"No method remains that a default could
route to which would mint another"*), in `ROADMAP.md:2011-2013` (*"There is no method left that a
forgotten override could fall through to which would mint another allowance"*) and in
`DEVLOG.md:27-29`. `ARCHITECTURE.md:248` and `reviews/README.md:61` state the generalization —
*"omitting it does not compile"* — which is true of `redetect` and not of `try_detect`.

There is one, and it is the trait's other default. `mod.rs:350-353`:

```rust
fn try_detect(&self, input: &str, budget: &Budget) -> Result<Vec<PiiEntity>, DetectError> {
    let _ = budget;
    Ok(self.detect(input))          // <- and three production `detect`s MINT
}
```

`StructuredRecognizers::detect` (`recognizers.rs:1004`), `CompositeDetector::detect`
(`composite.rs:35`) and `CachingDetector::detect` (`cache.rs:99`) each call
`try_detect(input, &Budget::new(MAX_PHONE_VALIDATIONS_PER_REQUEST)).unwrap_or_default()`. So the
default does not merely *ignore* the caller's budget — for a wrapper it **replaces** it, which is
word-for-word how M10-R35 described the sharpest case of its own defect, and it additionally
`unwrap_or_default()`s the refusal, turning a partially scanned field into `Ok(vec![])`.

**Repro — throwaway worktree at HEAD `09f224b`, nothing mutated, one new test file.** A plausible
seventh detector — a metrics or tracing decorator, an audit shim — written the way the trait invites:

```rust
struct Passthrough(Box<dyn PiiDetector>);
impl PiiDetector for Passthrough {
    fn detect(&self, input: &str) -> Vec<PiiEntity> { self.0.detect(input) }
}
```

Five lines. It compiles, it never mentions `Budget`, and there is nothing to forget to override —
`try_detect` and `redetect` are both provided. Put it in front of DOS-07's own body and DOS-07's own
allowance:

```text
PrivacyStage::with_validation_budget(Passthrough(StructuredRecognizers::new()), 20_000)
  20 x 20 KB distinct digit-dense fields   ->   blocked = false      (DOS-07: refused)
                                                vault empty = false  (it masked, then forwarded)
```

The request that DOS-07 exists to refuse is forwarded, and the refusal is not propagated-and-ignored —
it is **erased**, because `unwrap_or_default()` turned it into "no PII here". That is a *miss*, and
this codebase's own bar is that a miss is a leak. Not reachable in the shipped tree — all six
production impls override `try_detect` — but neither was M10-R35's, right up until the leaf did not.

**The asymmetry M10-R35's closure celebrated is the one that is missing here.** Its note reads:
*"`StructuredRecognizers` now overrides **one** fallible method and is correct on the later passes for
free … the previous shape required the most careful implementor to be the most vigilant; this one
requires nothing of it."* True on the `redetect` channel. On the `try_detect` channel the obligation is
back: an implementor who writes only `detect` is silently routed to a minting, swallowing method, and
the type system does not object. **When forgetting to override is silently valid, the API is the
defect** — the milestone's own promoted rule, applied to the half of the trait the fix did not turn
over.

**Fix — invert which method is derived.** `detect` is the convenience view; `try_detect` is the
contract. Make `try_detect` a **required** method and give `detect` the default:

```rust
pub trait PiiDetector: Send + Sync {
    /// Fallible detection, charged against the caller's `Budget`. **Required**: a detector that
    /// cannot fail writes `Ok(...)`, but it writes it, and it sees the budget.
    fn try_detect(&self, input: &str, budget: &Budget) -> Result<Vec<PiiEntity>, DetectError>;

    /// Infallible view, **not on the request path** — it mints because there is no request to
    /// charge, and that is visible here rather than reachable from a default.
    fn detect(&self, input: &str) -> Vec<PiiEntity> {
        self.try_detect(input, &Budget::per_call()).unwrap_or_default()
    }

    fn redetect(&self, input: &str, budget: &Budget) -> Result<Vec<PiiEntity>, DetectError> {
        self.try_detect(input, budget)
    }
}
```

Then the three per-type `detect` bodies collapse into one default, the three `Budget::new(MAX…)` mint
sites in `src/` disappear, and a new detector *cannot* reach a minted allowance without writing
`Budget::` itself. The one thing this asks of the NER-shaped detectors — which genuinely do not care
about the budget — is `fn try_detect(&self, i, _b) { Ok(self.detect_impl(i)) }`, which they already
write. Cost: `onnx.rs`, `gliner.rs` and the handful of test doubles gain or lose one method each.

If the trait shape is not to be reopened for a patch release, the smaller version is to **stop
`detect` from minting**: have it take no allowance and call an internal, budget-free scan, so the
worst a defaulted `try_detect` can do is under-report rather than fabricate a fresh 500,000.

Either way, correct the two documents that state the property in the **present tense** — `mod.rs`'s
own trait doc and `ARCHITECTURE.md` — since those are the design. `ROADMAP.md`'s and `DEVLOG.md`'s
round-5 entries are dated records of what was believed then; this round's entries correct them and
they should stay as written.

**Test that would have caught it, and it is [M10-R42](#m10-r42)'s loop with one more row.** Add a
`detect`-only wrapper to the chain list — it is the "seventh wrapper nobody has written yet" that
M10-R35 named — and assert the same two properties. It fails on HEAD.

**The rule to promote** — [ARCHITECTURE](../ARCHITECTURE.md), beside the budget box: *a defaulted
method is an API decision about what an implementor may omit; enumerate what every default routes to
before claiming a default cannot do harm.* "There is no method left that a default can route to" is a
**closure over the whole trait**, and it was checked on one method.

**Closed (2026-07-30) — the inversion, on the maintainer's call.** `try_detect` is the **required**
trait method and `detect` is the derived one. `detect` is the convenience view; `try_detect` is the
contract. A detector that cannot fail writes `Ok(..)` — but it writes it, and it sees the budget.

**The four test doubles that stopped compiling are this fix's entire regression suite, and they are
worth more than a test would be.** `NeverConverges`, `TagsPlaceholders`, `Fragmenter` and `FakeNer`
each implemented only `detect`; each now states, in one line, that it cannot fail and does not charge
the allowance. That is the difference the finding was about: the obligation is discharged by the
compiler rather than by a case someone remembered to write. There is no guard to add here, and adding
one would be weaker than what is already there.

Every place that carried the over-strong sentence — `mod.rs`, ARCHITECTURE, ROADMAP, DEVLOG — now says
what the code does. *When forgetting to override is silently valid, the API is the defect*, applied
to the half of the trait M10-R35's fix did not turn over.

<a id="m10-r45"></a>
### M10-R45 — `DetectError`'s fields are `pub` and the struct is not `#[non_exhaustive]`, so a struct literal bypasses both constructors — *"a new error site has to choose"* is a convention, not a type · **low**

[M10-R41](#m10-r41)'s closure note:

> `DetectError` carries `budget_exhausted`, built through `DetectError::budget_exhausted(..)` or
> `DetectError::unavailable(..)` — **the struct is no longer constructed literally anywhere, so a new
> error site has to *choose*.**

The first half is true and I checked it: `grep -rn "DetectError {" src/ tests/` returns only the
definition at `mod.rs:173`. The second does not follow. `mod.rs:172-194` declares

```rust
#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct DetectError {
    pub detector: &'static str,
    pub message: String,
    pub budget_exhausted: bool,
}
```

so `DetectError { detector: "x", message: m, budget_exhausted: false }` compiles anywhere in the crate
— and, since `pii` is a public module, outside it too. The wrong default is the *unsafe* one: a future
budget-shaped refusal written as a literal gets `budget_exhausted: false` and is swallowed by
`FailOpen`, which is precisely the failure M10-R41 removed.

This is the milestone's own rule at value level. R41 moved the distinction from a global onto the
error so the wrapper could *read* it; what remains is to make the error unable to lie about it.

**Fix.** One of, smallest first: `#[non_exhaustive]` on the struct (blocks the literal outside the
crate but not inside); make the fields private with `detector()` / `message()` / `budget_exhausted()`
accessors (blocks it everywhere — `Display` and `FailOpen` are the only readers, plus `err.message`
in three tests); or replace the flag with a `DetectErrorKind` enum, which also leaves room for the
next kind. The private-field version is the one that matches what the closure note already claims.

**Test that would have caught it.** None — a type-level property is not a test, it is a type. What
catches it is reading the closure note's claim as a *specification* and asking the compiler whether it
holds: the check is `cargo build` on a deliberate `DetectError { … }` literal, which succeeds today
and must not.

**Closed (2026-07-30).** `budget_exhausted` is private, read through `is_budget_exhausted()`. Outside
`pii/mod.rs` the two constructors are now the **only** way to build a `DetectError`, so "a new error
site has to choose" is enforced instead of requested. Two rounds spent on obligations a compiler was
not checking is enough to stop asking politely.

<a id="m10-r46"></a>
### M10-R46 — the M10-R35 rename left two **present-tense** references to the deleted `try_detect_within`, both in the file that deleted it · **low (docs)**

`grep -rn "_within" src/` is not empty. Four hits, and two of them describe how the code behaves
*now*:

`src/pii/recognizers.rs:783-786`, inside `push_candidates`:

> **Stop the moment the allowance is gone, whichever recognizer spent it.** The field is going to be
> refused by **`try_detect_within`** regardless, so there is nothing to buy by finishing the scan…

`src/pii/recognizers.rs:910-912`, inside `shrink_to_a_valid_prefix`:

> Give up rather than shrink further on an empty allowance. Returning `None` here is a *miss*, not a
> silent pass: the caller's budget is already spent, so **`try_detect_within`** refuses the whole
> field below.

Both are correct statements about a method that does not exist; the method is `try_detect`
(`recognizers.rs:1012`), and it is in the same file, ~230 and ~100 lines below. The other two hits —
`mod.rs:318`/`:324` and `complexity.rs:673-677` — name the pair while *narrating its deletion*, which
is right and must stay.

**Why nothing caught it.** These are `//` comments inside function bodies, not `///` doc comments, so
`cargo doc` cannot see them and the 15-warning baseline did not move. `clippy` does not read prose.
The commit that deleted the methods (`dd7ecb8`) touched both of these functions' *signatures* and
neither of their comments — which is [M10-R31](#m10-r31) and [M10-R39](#m10-r39) exactly, and the rule
M9-R29 promoted and this milestone has now re-learned three times: **prose is part of the diff; when a
commit changes a mechanism, every comment that survives it is unreviewed until re-read against the new
code.**

Low, because both sentences are *semantically* right — the field really is refused one level up, by
the method that inherited the behaviour. What they cost is the next reader's `grep`: a name that
resolves to nothing sends them looking for a seam this milestone spent a round removing.

**Fix.** `try_detect_within` → `try_detect` at both sites. While there, `recognizers.rs:776-777`'s
*"the error is raised one level up, so there is exactly one place that decides what a truncated scan
means"* is still true and worth keeping as the pointer.

**Test that would have caught it.** A `grep` guard is available and cheap and is probably not worth
it for two comments — but the *class* is worth one line in CI once the milestone closes: a deleted
public method name should not survive in `src/` outside a passage that says it was deleted. The
durable version is the review habit: **when a commit renames or deletes a method, `grep` the old name
before the commit is written, not after the round.**

**Closed (2026-07-30).** Both comments now name `try_detect`, and `grep -rn "try_detect_within" src/`
is empty. The suggested class-level guard is not added: two comments do not earn a CI job, and the
habit it encodes — **grep the old name in the same commit that deletes it** — is the thing worth
keeping, not the automation.

---

<a id="review-7"></a>
## Round 7 — closure verification (2026-07-30)

**All five round-6 closures hold, and the two that needed a mutated tree got one.** M10-R42's widened
DOS-08 is not decoration: with the finding's own one-line defect back in `CachingDetector::redetect`,
**DOS-08 and DOS-09 are the only two failures in the whole default suite** (217 passed / 2 failed,
`--no-fail-fast`), and both go green when it is restored. M10-R44's inversion is enforced by the
compiler and not by a convention: a `detect`-only implementor is **E0046 — "not all trait items
implemented, missing `try_detect`"**. M10-R43's fifth version of the published numbers reproduces
**exactly, unit for unit, row for row**, on two independent DOS-BUD runs; `~2.7 µs`, `~1.4 s`,
`≈50,000 numbers` and `~3.6 MB` survive nowhere outside the archive and DEVLOG's dated entries.
`grep -rn "try_detect_within" src/` is empty (R46), and `budget_exhausted` is private (R45).

**What the round found is one line of shipped code that nothing asserts, one hand-written list that
nothing checks, and three numbers whose provenance does not survive re-measurement.**

The line is `composite.rs:117` — `Err(err) if err.is_budget_exhausted() => Err(err)`, the whole of
M10-R41's fix. **Delete it and the suite is 219/219 green**, and DOS-07's own body driven through
`CachingDetector(CompositeDetector([FailOpen(StructuredRecognizers)]))` — the wiring ARCHITECTURE and
`FailOpen`'s own doc comment both say to expect — is **forwarded** with a partially scanned body and a
clean bill of health ([M10-R47](#m10-r47)). M10-R41 *prescribed* that guard in its own "test that would
have caught it"; the closure took the fix and left the guard. And the reason no guard sees it is the
next finding: `shipped_chains()` is a hand-written list of four chains whose doc says it is *"every
detector arrangement `AppState::new` can build"*, which is false in both directions — `bare` is not one
of them (`build_detector` always wraps in a composite) and the `FailOpen` position is one and is absent
([M10-R48](#m10-r48)). Nothing ties the list to `build_detector`, so **DOS-08's reach is now a
maintenance obligation on the next person who edits the wiring** — R42's own defect, one level up:
aimed at a list of instances rather than at the thing that builds them. TESTING's new axis row says so
itself, with the `—` the same finding declared is never the honest answer.

The numbers are the milestone's other recurring failure, and this time the harness disagrees with the
prose rather than the prose with itself. **The refusal line is not 25,000–35,000 rows.** DOS-BUD prints
no row between 20,000 and 50,000; run the missing ones and 40,000 rows is **masked at 490,010 units**,
putting the line near **41,000** ([M10-R49](#m10-r49)). And DOS-BUD's *"one phone column"* holds no
phone: its `3{:02} {:04} {:04}` is eleven digits, every row is rejected, and the verdict column prints
`0 left` at every size — a column whose numbers actually validate spends **284,500** units at 40,000
rows, not 490,010, so for a genuinely phone-bearing result the line is nearer **70,000**. **And the
5.2 s worst case does not reproduce**: on an idle box the 6.1 MB row is **1.46 s**, the 3.8 MB row
1.38 s, and every one of the five 500,000-unit refusals in the table costs 1.31–1.51 s — which is what
the document's own two-term model predicts and 3.6× under what both READMEs publish as the worst thing
a legal body can cost ([M10-R50](#m10-r50)). I reproduced the mechanism directly: the same command in
the same tree printed 3,635 ms for the 3 MiB field row with a build running beside it and 1,313 ms
alone.

Two smaller ones close the round, both the same shape as the three before them. TESTING's DOS-08
