catalog entry still says *"phrased over the trait rather than the type"* and *"a seventh detector that
drops the allowance fails here"* — the two sentences M10-R42 was filed about, at the site the finding
named — and TESTING's E2E-05 entry still says *1 MiB* where M10-R43's fix listed it explicitly
([M10-R51](#m10-r51)). And M10-R45's *"outside `pii/mod.rs` the two constructors are the **only** way
to build a `DetectError`"* is false: a private field is visible in the defining module **and every
module below it**, so the literal still compiles in `pii::composite`, `pii::recognizers`,
`pii::onnx` — which is where all four error sites live ([M10-R52](#m10-r52)). *Two rounds spent on
obligations a compiler was not checking, and the compiler still is not checking this one where it
matters.*

Nothing here is a live leak or a live DoS, and nothing changes product behaviour.

### Verdict per round-6 finding

| ID | Closure | How I checked |
|---|---|---|
| [M10-R42](#m10-r42) | **holds**, by mutation | `CachingDetector::redetect` re-mutated to mint (`let _ = budget; self.inner.redetect(input, &Budget::new(MAX_PHONE_VALIDATIONS_PER_REQUEST))`) in a throwaway worktree at HEAD `5218cbd`: `cargo test --no-fail-fast` → **217 passed / 2 failed**, and the two are exactly `every_fixpoint_pass_is_charged_to_the_request_budget` and `identical_fields_through_the_cached_chain_are_charged_on_every_pass`. Restored → 219/219. Both halves of DOS-08 run against all four `shipped_chains()`, and I checked the two `CachingDetector` branches are both reached: (a)'s 4,000-byte field is cacheable, (b)'s 38-byte `two_pass` string takes the non-cacheable early return, so a mint in either branch reds. What the loop does **not** reach is the `FailOpen` position: [M10-R47](#m10-r47), [M10-R48](#m10-r48). |
| [M10-R43](#m10-r43) | **holds** | DOS-BUD re-run twice on `--release` (`cargo test --release --test complexity -- --ignored --nocapture budget_refusal_line`), the second time on an idle box. **Every unit count in the constant's table matches exactly** — 10,010 / 100,010 / 290,010 / 500,000 — as do ARCHITECTURE's 499,380, 500,000 and the 0-unit 16 MiB floor. `~3 µs` reproduces: the tier's own share is 130 ms − 3 ms over 48,820 units = **2.6 µs**. The 228 ms floor reproduces at **230 ms**. `grep`ed the four retired figures across `src/`, `docs/`, both READMEs and `.github/`: `~1.4 s` survives only at `mod.rs:340` and `ARCHITECTURE.md:237`, both *narrating* the ceiling R35 disproved, which is right. What does **not** survive re-measurement is the refusal line and the wall-clock column: [M10-R49](#m10-r49), [M10-R50](#m10-r50). |
| [M10-R44](#m10-r44) | **holds**, by the compiler | Wrote the implementor the finding describes — `impl PiiDetector for DetectOnly { fn detect(&self, _: &str) -> Vec<PiiEntity> { Vec::new() } }` — and it is **`error[E0046]`: not all trait items implemented, missing `try_detect`**, with rustc suggesting the signature. The other direction is still open by hand: `fn try_detect(&self, i, _budget) { Ok(self.0.detect(i)) }` compiles, and DOS-07's body through it is **forwarded** (`blocked=false`). That is not a regression — it is what the inversion left, and it now costs an explicitly discarded parameter beside the correct one-liner, which is the difference between an omission and a decision. Recorded, not filed. |
| [M10-R45](#m10-r45) | **fix holds, claim does not** | `budget_exhausted` is private and read through `is_budget_exhausted()` at the one place that reads it. But the closure's *"outside `pii/mod.rs` the two constructors are now the **only** way to build a `DetectError`"* is false inside `crate::pii::*` — compiled a literal in `src/pii/composite.rs` and it builds, with `budget_exhausted: false`, and `FailOpen::fail_open` swallows it. [M10-R52](#m10-r52). |
| [M10-R46](#m10-r46) | **holds** | `grep -rn "try_detect_within" src/` is empty. The four `_within` hits left in the tree are `mod.rs:332`/`:338` and `complexity.rs:699` narrating the deletion, plus two unrelated identifiers (`ner_perf.rs`, `perf.rs`). |

### The budget, attacked a fourth time

**1 · Can a shipped-plausible arrangement drop the allowance and still pass?** Yes — one, and it is
`FailOpen`. Not by dropping the *budget* (it forwards, and its inner NER would ignore one anyway) but by
dropping the *refusal*: `composite.rs:117` is the only thing that stops a budget-exhausted error being
converted to `Ok(vec![])`, and no test anywhere constructs one and passes it through the wrapper.
Deleted, the suite is **219/219** and `Caching(Composite([FailOpen(Structured)])))` — a shape one
`wrap_ml` call away — forwards DOS-07's body. [M10-R47](#m10-r47). The composition question the round
was commissioned with has the same answer from the other side: `shipped_chains()` cannot see it because
`FailOpen` is not in it. [M10-R48](#m10-r48).

**2 · Anything else that mints or swallows on the request path?** No. `grep -rn "Budget::new\|Budget::per_call\|Budget::unlimited" src/`
gives, outside `#[cfg(test)]`: `privacy.rs:113` (the request path's only creator), the four infallible
`detect` views (`recognizers.rs:1015`, `composite.rs:35`, `cache.rs:99`, `mod.rs:379`) and, with
`--features onnx`, `onnx.rs:734` / `gliner.rs:461`. Nothing on the request path calls `detect`. The
three per-type `detect` overrides still spell the mint `Budget::new(MAX_PHONE_VALIDATIONS_PER_REQUEST)`
where the new trait default spells it `Budget::per_call()` — identical by construction
(`per_call` *is* `new(MAX…)`), so this is redundancy rather than a defect, and it is the one part of
M10-R44's proposed fix that was not taken. Noted here so the next reader does not have to re-derive
that the two spellings agree.

**3 · Is a mint anywhere else still caught?** Yes, and I checked each by reasoning against the
guards rather than assuming: `CompositeDetector::redetect` → DOS-08(a) on the `composed` chain;
`Vault::mask_all` re-minting for passes 1..n → DOS-08(b)'s `spent()` comparison; `PrivacyStage`
minting per field → DOS-07; the cache's non-cacheable early return → DOS-08(b), whose 38-byte input
takes exactly that branch.

**4 · Is the threshold still defensible?** Yes, and more so than the docs claim. The criterion
M10-R27 set is *"an ordinary tool result must not be refused"*: the 367 KB / 5,000-row result spends
100,010 (20%), and the measured refusal line for DOS-BUD's own shape is **~41,000 rows**, not
25,000–35,000 — and for a column whose numbers actually validate, ~70,000. The published figure is
wrong in the **conservative** direction, which is why it is a `measurement` and not worse. The M7
22 KiB turn still spends **0** through the whole fixpoint.

### What I verified independently

- **Suite, both feature sets, no warnings.** `cargo test` → **219 passed / 0 failed / 5 ignored, 0
  warnings** (shell with `NO_COLOR` unset, so CLI-06 asserts a property of the binary).
  `cargo test --features onnx --target-dir target/onnx` → **252 passed / 0 failed / 22 ignored, 0
  warnings**. `cargo fmt --check` clean. `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean on
  **both**. `cargo doc --no-deps` → **15** warnings, the pre-existing baseline item for item; M10 adds
  none.
- **The real release `.exe`, end to end**, against a counting mock upstream on OS-assigned ports
  (53707/53708 and 55162/55163 — neither is 1234, nothing was bound to or connected to 1234), shipped
  default configuration:
  - *mask → inject → forward → restore, watched.* `upstream_received` (a key outside the demask path)
    carried `Mail [EMAIL_1] and call [PHONE_1] about IBAN [IBAN_1]` plus the injected augmentation
    system message; the client's `choices[0].message.content` came back with **the real values
    restored**.
  - *the refusal.* 160 identical 100 KB fields, each `"a@b.com " + "row 11 NNNN NNNN end "` groups
    (16,006,270 bytes) → **`400` in 1,364 ms**, upstream hits **1 → 1**, and the body is the actionable
    message naming the allowance, the unit (*"per request"*), the 100,012-byte field and nothing
    input-derived. The same body with `PII_LOCALES=` → **`200` in 438 ms**, upstream contacted: the
    differential is the phone tier's and nothing else.
  - *and one honest negative.* The same 160 identical fields **without** the leading email answer
    `200` in 473 ms spending ~5,000 units — correctly: nothing is masked, so the fixpoint stops at
    pass 0 and 159 of the 160 fields are cache hits that did no work. Recorded because it reads like a
    miss and is not, and because it is why M10-R35's repro needs a maskable prefix.
- **DOS-BUD reproduced twice on `--release`**, the second run with nothing else on the box. Units and
  verdicts match the constant's and ARCHITECTURE's tables **exactly**. Wall clocks are the finding.
- **Six experiments in a throwaway worktree** (`git worktree add … HEAD --detach`, removed afterwards;
  every restore followed by a `touch` so cargo could not serve a stale build), all against **HEAD
  `5218cbd`**, never the working tree: R42's mutation; the `detect`-only compile; the hand-written
  `Ok(self.detect(..))` wrapper; `FailOpen`'s guard deleted (twice — once for the suite, once driven
  through `PrivacyStage`); a `DetectError` literal inside `pii::composite`; and DOS-BUD's SQL table
  extended with the rows it does not print (25,000 / 30,000 / 32,000 / 34,000 / 35,000 / 40,000) plus
  a valid-mobile variant of its own generator.
- **Things I tried and could not break.** No request-side path reaches a fresh allowance. Both cache
  settings and both feature sets forward the budget identically. A budget error is still never cached
  (`cache.rs:125`, the `?` precedes the insert). `CompositeDetector`, `Vault::mask_all` and
  `PrivacyStage` are each covered by a guard that reds on a mint. The budget is still a `Cell`, still
  `!Sync`, still a stack local in `on_request`. `pii_max_phone_validations` is still unreachable from
  the environment. E2E-05 still cannot pass vacuously. The M7 turn still spends 0 through the whole
  fixpoint. And I could not find a second uncovered wrapper position: `FailOpen` is the only detector
  type in `src/` that `shipped_chains()` omits.
- **Environment.** Every port OS-assigned and checked `!= 1234`. The only processes stopped were the
  three proxy children this harness spawned, each by its own PID; nothing was killed by name.

---

<a id="m10-r47"></a>
### M10-R47 — `FailOpen`'s one rule — *never swallow a budget refusal* — is asserted by no test: delete the line and the suite is 219/219 green while a shipped-plausible chain forwards DOS-07's body · **guard**

`src/pii/composite.rs:112-127` is the whole of [M10-R41](#m10-r41)'s fix, and one line of it carries
the rule:

```rust
match outcome {
    Ok(found) => Ok(found),
    Err(err) if err.is_budget_exhausted() => Err(err),   // <- this line
    Err(err) => { tracing::warn!(...); Ok(Vec::new()) }
}
```

**Repro — mutation, throwaway worktree at HEAD `5218cbd`.** Delete that one line:

```text
cargo test --no-fail-fast   ->   219 passed, 0 failed, 0 warnings
```

Every guard in the project is green with the rule gone. Then drive DOS-07's own body and DOS-07's own
allowance (20 × 20 KB distinct digit-dense fields, `with_validation_budget(.., 20_000)`) through three
chains:

| chain | `ctx.block` |
|---|---|
| bare `StructuredRecognizers` (DOS-07) | **refused** |
| `Caching(Composite([Structured]))` — the shipped default (DOS-09) | **refused** |
| `Caching(Composite([FailOpen(Structured)])))` | **forwarded**, vault non-empty |

The third is a partially scanned body forwarded with a clean bill of health — *the* failure
M10-R41 was filed to remove, reachable again by one deleted line that nothing in the suite notices.

**Why this is not "a test for a wiring that does not exist".** The codebase has already
*rejected* that argument, in writing, twice. `FailOpen`'s own doc comment (`composite.rs:84-86`):
*"the answer was no only because nothing wraps the structured recognizers in `FailOpen` today. That is
a property of the wiring, not of the code, and wiring changes."* [ARCHITECTURE](../ARCHITECTURE.md)
repeats it. Round 4's fail-open hunt came back empty and **the reason it was empty was itself the
defect** — that is the sentence this milestone promoted. A rule the design says must hold in the code
rather than in the wiring is a rule the code has to assert.

**And M10-R41 asked for exactly this guard.** Its *"test that would have caught it"* reads:

> Two unit tests, both cheap: `try_detect_within` on PII-free text with `Budget::new(0)` must be `Err`
> (pins the recognizer's half), and `FailOpen(a non-budget failing detector)` with an exhausted budget
> must be `Ok(vec![])` **if** the error-kind fix is taken … Either way the choice becomes visible
> instead of emergent.

The error-kind fix *was* taken. Neither test was written: `grep -rn "Budget::new(0)" src/ tests/`
returns nothing, and `grep -rn "FailOpen" tests/` returns two **comments**. Round 6 verified the
behaviour by hand — `FailOpen(AlwaysFails).try_detect(.., &Budget::new(0)) → Ok([])` and a refusal
crossing intact — and a hand measurement is a fact about one afternoon, not a guard. *A closure that
takes a finding's fix and leaves its test converts a defect into a latency: the next edit re-opens it
silently.*

**Fix.** Two assertions, in `composite.rs`'s existing test module, using the doubles already there:

```rust
// The distinction FailOpen must not collapse — one line each way.
assert!(FailOpen(Box::new(FailingDetector))                       // DetectError::unavailable
    .try_detect("mail bob@test.com", &Budget::per_call()).is_ok());
assert!(FailOpen(Box::new(StructuredRecognizers::new()))          // DetectError::budget_exhausted
    .try_detect(&digit_dense, &Budget::new(1)).is_err());
```
and the same pair on `redetect`, which is the entry point that has no test at all. The larger version —
and the one that also closes [M10-R48](#m10-r48) — is to add `("failopen", FailOpen(structured))` and
`("cached+composed+failopen", …)` to `shipped_chains()`, which makes DOS-08 assert it on both channels
for free.

**Test that would have caught it.** Itself — and the durable form is the rule the milestone keeps
paying for in a new place: **when a finding names the guard that would have caught it, the closure is
not done until that guard exists.** R41 wrote its own guard down and the fix shipped without it; R47 is
what that costs. Promote to [TESTING](../TESTING.md#algorithmic-complexity-guards), beside the budget
guards: *the fail-closed distinction a wrapper must not collapse is a behaviour, and behaviours are
pinned by tests — not by the fact that today's wiring never produces the input.*

**Closed (2026-07-30) — FAILOPEN-BUD, in `pii::composite`, asserting both sides.** (a) an unavailable
detector is swallowed; (b) an exhausted allowance propagates on **both** entry points, with the error
flagged `budget_exhausted` and the budget actually spent. Either half alone is satisfied by a wrapper
that has stopped distinguishing them — swallow everything and (a) passes, propagate everything and (b)
passes — so *a guard for a distinction has to exercise both sides of it*.

Verified by removing the guard clause: the case reds, and nothing else in the suite does. That is the
whole finding — for two rounds the line had no witness, and M10-R41's own *"test that would have caught
it"* had prescribed exactly this case. **A closure that takes the fix and leaves the prescribed test
has closed half the finding**, and this is the second time in this milestone (M10-R32 was the first).

<a id="m10-r48"></a>
### M10-R48 — `shipped_chains()` says it is *"every detector arrangement `AppState::new` can build"* and is a hand-written list of four that is wrong in both directions, with nothing tying it to `build_detector` · **guard**

`tests/complexity.rs:671-692`:

```rust
/// Every detector arrangement `AppState::new` can build, so a guard about the **chain** is not
/// secretly a guard about one type (M10-R42).
///
/// The wiring is `CachingDetector` (optional) over `CompositeDetector` (one element in the default
/// build) over `StructuredRecognizers`. All four combinations are shipped shapes.
fn shipped_chains() -> Vec<(&'static str, Box<dyn PiiDetector>)> { … }   // bare · composed · cached · cached+composed
```

Against `src/server.rs`:

- **`bare` is not a shape `AppState::new` can build.** `build_detector` returns
  `CompositeDetector::new(vec![structured])` on the default build (`server.rs:179`) and
  `CompositeDetector::new(detectors)` on the `onnx` one (`:171`). There is no path that hands
  `PrivacyStage` a naked `StructuredRecognizers`. Harmless — extra coverage — but it is the half of
  the claim that is easy to check, and it is wrong.
- **The `FailOpen` position *is* a shape `AppState::new` builds, and is absent.** `wrap_ml`
  (`server.rs:419-424`) wraps each ML engine in `FailOpen` whenever `NER_REQUIRED` is off, so the
  shipped `--features onnx` chain is `Caching(Composite([Structured, FailOpen(Ner), FailOpen(GLiNER)]))`.
  Neither `FailOpen` nor a **multi-element** composite appears in the list. That is the position
  [M10-R47](#m10-r47) walks straight through.

**Nothing connects the list to the wiring.** `build_detector` is a private `async fn` reading
`env`; `shipped_chains()` is a hand-written literal in a test file. Add a seventh detector to the
first and the second stays green, silently — which is [M10-R42](#m10-r42)'s own defect one level up:
*the guard was aimed at the type, so it was widened to a list of instances, and the list is the new
instance.* The generalization R42 promoted — *quantify over the shape the defect can take, not the
instance that had it* — is satisfied for the four shapes somebody remembered and for no other.

**TESTING's axis table says it out loud, and did not notice.** `docs/TESTING.md:281`:

| guard | varies | holds constant |
|---|---|---|
| **DOS-08 + DOS-09 (M10-R42)** | **detector composition** | — |

with, eleven lines below it, *"**The dash was the finding**, which is the discipline to carry forward:
a guard's row must name what it holds constant, and `—` is never the honest answer."* The new row
carries a dash. The honest answer is **the wrapper inventory**: the four chains hold constant that
every wrapper in them is one somebody typed into a `Vec` in 2026-07-30, and that no `FailOpen` is
among them. **Sixth arrival, and the row written to record the fifth is where it landed.**

Two more documents carry the widened claim in the present tense and inherit the gap:
`docs/reviews/README.md:80-81` — *"The guard now quantifies over **every chain the wiring can
build**"* — and `ROADMAP.md`'s round-6 block.

**Fix.** Cheapest first, and the first two are worth doing regardless:

1. Add the missing positions to the list: `("failopen", FailOpen(structured))` and
   `("cached+composed+failopen", Caching(Composite([FailOpen(structured)])))`. `FailOpen` is in the
   default build (`pii::composite` is not feature-gated) and `complexity.rs` compiles on both feature
   sets, so this costs two lines and no `cfg`. It closes [M10-R47](#m10-r47)'s `try_detect`/`redetect`
   halves at the same time.
2. Correct the doc comment to what the list is — *"every wrapper position `build_detector` can
   produce, enumerated by hand"* — and give TESTING's row its honest `holds constant`.
3. The structural version, if it is judged worth it: make `build_detector`'s **composition** step a
   pure function of `(structured, mls, cache_entries)` that both `AppState::new` and the test call,
   so a new wrapper cannot enter the wiring without entering the guard. That is the only version that
   stops this being a maintenance obligation, and it is the one the finding's own lesson asks for.

**Test that would have caught it.** A test cannot check its own list. What catches it is the reading
R42 prescribed and stopped one step short of: **take the guard's quantifier to the code it claims to
quantify over, and diff them.** Four minutes, `server.rs` beside `complexity.rs`.

**Closed (2026-07-30), and the gap is named rather than papered over.** `shipped_chains()` gains the
three `FailOpen` positions — `fail-open`, `composed(fail-open)`, `cached+composed(fail-open)` — so
DOS-08 covers the wrapper it omitted. It is still a **hand-written list**, and its doc comment now says
so in the same breath: `build_detector` takes a `Config` and returns an opaque `Box<dyn PiiDetector>`,
so nothing can enumerate what it built. *A list of instances is weaker than a projection of the
wiring*, and writing that down is the honest mitigation — the alternative is a sentence that implies a
completeness the code cannot deliver, which is the class this whole round is about.

TESTING's axis row for DOS-08 now names what it varies (**detector composition**) instead of carrying
the `—` that M10-R42 had just declared is never the honest answer.

<a id="m10-r49"></a>
### M10-R49 — the refusal line is not 25,000–35,000 rows: measured, DOS-BUD's own shape masks at **40,000 rows / 490,010 units**, and its *"phone column"* contains no phone at all · **measurement**

Two documents publish a number DOS-BUD does not print, and describe it as if it did.

`src/pii/recognizers.rs:985-988`:

> So the refusal line for this shape sits at **~25,000–35,000 rows**, **bracketed by measurement rather
> than computed from a per-number rate** — the rate is not constant (the memo absorbs more as a table
> grows), and applying one measured at one scale to another an order of magnitude away is M10-R20's
> defect wearing a units label (M10-R38).

`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:309-310`: *"The refusal line for a phone-bearing database result sits around
**25,000–35,000 rows**, measured."*

**DOS-BUD prints 500 / 5,000 / 20,000 / 50,000 / 80,000 rows.** Measurement therefore brackets the line
in `(20,000, 50,000]` and says nothing narrower. 25,000–35,000 is an interpolation from the 14.5
units/row *average* at 20,000 rows — which is exactly the move the sentence beside it disclaims, and
[M10-R43](#m10-r43)'s closure deleted the `≈10 units per number` derivation for being that move.

**Repro — DOS-BUD's own generator, the rows it does not print** (throwaway worktree, `--release`,
idle box):

| rows | bytes | verdict | units |
|---|---|---|---|
| 20,000 | 1,497,815 | masked | 290,010 |
| 25,000 | 1,877,815 | masked | 340,010 |
| 30,000 | 2,257,815 | masked | 390,010 |
| 35,000 | 2,637,815 | masked | 440,010 |
| **40,000** | 3,017,815 | **masked** | **490,010** |
| 50,000 | 3,777,815 | **refused** | 500,000 |

The marginal rate is a flat **10 units/row** across the whole range (340,010 → 390,010 → 440,010 →
490,010), so the line is at **≈41,000 rows**. Every row the published interval calls refused is masked,
with 60,000 units to spare at its top end.

**And the payload is not phone-bearing.** DOS-BUD builds its phone column as
`3{:02} {:04} {:04}` (`complexity.rs:586`) — **eleven** digits, where an Italian mobile is ten. Not one
row validates, which is why the verdict column reads `0 left` at 500, 5,000 and 20,000 rows: **zero
phones are masked in the payload the threshold was chosen against.** What it measures is the cost of
*rejection*, which is the expensive verdict and therefore the conservative choice — but it is not what
three documents say it is, and the difference is a factor of nearly two:

| column | 500 rows | 5,000 | 20,000 | 40,000 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| DOS-BUD's `3xx nnnn nnnn` (11 digits, never valid) | 10,010 · **0 masked** | 100,010 · 0 | 290,010 · 0 | 490,010 · 0 |
| `3xx nnn nnnn` (10 digits, a real IT mobile) | 6,000 · **500 masked** | 39,500 · 5,000 | 144,500 · 20,000 | 284,500 · 40,000 |

So for a result whose phone column actually *is* a phone column, the line is near **70,000 rows**. The
published figure is wrong in the conservative direction — which is why this is `measurement` and not
worse — but it is the answer to *"can real traffic hit this?"*, the question [M10-R27](#m10-r27) exists
to ask, and it is off by 20–100%.

**Why nothing caught it.** The harness's own verdict column is mislabelled: it prints
`format!("{} left", masked.matches("[PHONE_").count())` — the count of placeholders **inserted**,
labelled *left*. A reader scanning `0 left` reads "nothing left over" and moves on; it means "nothing
was ever masked". *A column whose label contradicts its value is how a payload gets described as
something it is not for three rounds.*

**Fix.** Three lines of harness and two of prose. Add 30,000 and 40,000 to DOS-BUD's `for rows in […]`
and publish the line as the row it comes from (*"40,000 rows masks at 490,010 units; 50,000 is
refused"*) instead of an interval. Rename the verdict to `phones masked`. And either fix the generator
to emit a valid national number — in which case say so and re-publish the units, which are **lower** —
or rename the payload everywhere to *"a phone-**shaped** column"*, which is what it is and what makes
the measurement conservative on purpose.

**Test that would have caught it.** DOS-BUD is the test; it was asked the wrong grid. The rule to
promote, next to the budget guards in [TESTING](../TESTING.md#algorithmic-complexity-guards): **a
threshold's published crossing point must be a row the harness prints, not a point between two of
them** — the interval between a passing row and a failing row is the *only* honest claim a coarse grid
supports, and if a narrower one is wanted the grid gets narrower, not the prose. Its companion is the
[M10-R43](#m10-r43) rule this milestone already learned twice: *a rate measured at one scale does not
transfer to another.*

**Closed (2026-07-30), and the corrected answer is stronger than the one it replaces.** DOS-BUD's
column is a real ten-digit Italian mobile (`347 XXXXXXX`), its verdict reports **`N masked`** instead
of `N left`, and a new row measures the same shape at `MAX_BODY_BYTES`.

With a column that actually validates, the arithmetic inverts. `national_phone_valid` is `.any()` over
the enabled regions and **`.any()` short-circuits on accept**, so a *real* number costs ~1 unit while a
candidate every plan rejects pays for all nine:

| tool result | rows | units | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| 357 KB | 5,000 | 5,000 | masked |
| 3.7 MB | 50,000 | 50,000 | masked |
| **16 MiB** (`MAX_BODY_BYTES`) | **221,941** | **221,941** | masked, 2.56 s |

So **a legal phone-bearing body cannot reach the allowance at all** — the largest such request the
proxy will accept spends 44% of it, and `MAX_BODY_BYTES` binds first. What reaches the budget is
digit-dense text that *fails* validation: the adversarial shape, by construction, which is the right
thing for a fail-closed bound to be reachable by. Every published "refusal line in rows" is gone,
because for this payload shape there isn't one.

> **The instrument, not the product, and that is the lesson.** An eleven-digit column is not a phone
> column; the harness whose entire job is answering *"can real traffic reach this?"* was measuring the
> cost of **rejecting non-numbers** for two rounds. And it could not have told anyone: `0 left` is what
> a correctly-masked column prints too. *A measurement harness needs its own non-vacuity assertion —
> the same bar M4-R13 set for corpora, applied to the thing doing the measuring.* Reporting `N masked`
> is the whole fix, and it would have caught this on the first run.

<a id="m10-r50"></a>
### M10-R50 — the **5.2 s** worst case in both READMEs does not reproduce (1.46 s on an idle box), contradicts the two-term model published beside it, and is attributed to masking work its row never did · **measurement**

The figure, in four live documents:

- `README.md:213-215` / `README.it.md:216-218` — *"the slowest legal body measured is **5.2 s** for a
  6 MB database result, and `MAX_BODY_BYTES` bounds that."*
- `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:295` — the table cell `| 6.1 MB SQL result, 80,000 rows | refused | 500,000 | 5.23 s |`
  and `:316-317` — *"on a body that actually **masks tens of thousands of values** it dominates the
  tail: the worst legal body measured here costs **5.2 s**, not 1.6 s."*
- `src/pii/recognizers.rs:999-1000` — *"the slowest legal body measured costs **5.2 s**."*

**Three things are wrong with it, and the first is the one a reader can check.**

**1 · It does not reproduce.** Two DOS-BUD runs here with the documented invocation, the second with
nothing else on the box:

| DOS-BUD row | published | run 1 (a build running beside it) | run 2 (idle) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2 MiB field, 499,380 units | 1.57 s | 3.03 s | **1.32 s** |
| 3 MiB field, 500,000 units | 1.60 s | 3.64 s | **1.31 s** |
| 3.8 MB SQL, 500,000 units | 3.82 s | 1.63 s | **1.38 s** |
| 6.1 MB SQL, 500,000 units | **5.23 s** | 1.62 s | **1.46 s** |
| 16 MiB, tier off | 228 ms | 236 ms | **230 ms** |

Every unit count matches exactly and the tier-off floor matches to 1%, so this is not a slower or
faster machine: run 1 shows what contention does to the *field* rows (2.8×) and run 2 shows the
uncontended value of everything. Round 6's reviewer independently measured the same column *"1.05–3.2×
faster here"* and set it aside as disclosed variance — it is disclosed nowhere, and the row it lands
hardest on is the one two READMEs quote.

**2 · It contradicts the model on the same page.** ARCHITECTURE publishes the ceiling as two terms:
`units × ~3 µs` capped at ~1.6 s, **plus** work linear in the body, floor 228 ms for 16 MiB. A 6.1 MB
body refused at 500,000 units is therefore ~1.5 s + ~90 ms ≈ **1.6 s** — which is what run 2 measures
(1.46 s), and what the *other four* 500,000-unit refusals in the same table cost here (1.31–1.51 s).
There is no term in the published model that produces 5.2 s from 6.1 MB, and the table's own 3 MiB
field row — same 500,000 units, half the bytes — is printed at 1.60 s. **A number that its own document
cannot derive and its own harness does not print is a number to re-measure, not to publish twice in a
README.**

**3 · The row masks nothing.** The sentence attaches 5.2 s to *"a body that actually masks tens of
thousands of values"*. The 6.1 MB row is **refused** — `mask_all` returns `Err`, nothing is rewritten
— and DOS-BUD's SQL payload masks **zero** phones at every size anyway ([M10-R49](#m10-r49)). The
largest body in the table that actually masks is 1.5 MB / 20,000 rows at 756 ms here. The *claim* is
defensible — measured separately, a 40,000-row result of **valid** IT mobiles masks 40,000 values in
1.78 s while spending only 284,500 units, so the non-validation term really is ~0.9 s of that — but
none of the evidence for it is in the table, and the cell that was pressed into service does not show
it.

One adjacent overclaim, same cause: ARCHITECTURE:287-289 says *"**Every** row below is printed by
`DOS-BUD`"*, and the table's first row — the M7 22 KiB turn, 0 units — is printed by **PHONE-BUD**
(`tests/phone_overmask.rs:178`), which the prose eleven lines later correctly credits.

**Fix.** Re-run DOS-BUD alone and replace the wall-clock column wholesale — it is one command and the
units are already right. State the worst case the way the model does: *every refusal costs ~1.5 s of
validation plus work linear in the body; the slowest row measured is X s*, with X from the re-run.
Move the *"masking dominates the tail"* sentence onto a row that masks, or delete it and let the
tier-off floor plus the measured masked rows carry the point. Add the sentence the column needs —
**wall clocks are machine- and load-dependent; the units are not** — and credit the M7 row to
PHONE-BUD.

**Test that would have caught it.** None automated, and it should not be: a wall clock is not
assertable. What catches it is the rule this milestone already promoted, applied one column to the
right — *a published number must name the harness row it came from* — plus its missing half, which is
worth promoting to [TESTING](../TESTING.md#algorithmic-complexity-guards): **a measurement harness that
runs under `cargo test` must be run alone, and a figure that cannot be derived from the model published
beside it is a measurement error until shown otherwise.** This project already knows the mechanism —
TESTING's own note on the 30 s wall-clock budget records a 1.9 s case crossing 10 s under
`--features onnx` parallelism — and its published DoS numbers walked into it anyway.

**Closed (2026-07-30).** The **5.2 s** figure was wrong twice over — measured while the box was loaded,
*and* on the broken column of [M10-R49](#m10-r49). Re-measured idle, the slowest legal body is the
16 MiB phone-bearing result at **2.56 s**, and every refusal lands at **1.4–1.9 s**. Both READMEs,
ARCHITECTURE and the constant's doc now carry those, with the two-term model (`units × ~3 µs` capped
at ~1.5 s, plus linear work bounded by `MAX_BODY_BYTES`) that they are consistent with.

> **Third time a contended measurement reached a product doc in this milestone**, after M10-R2's first
> figures and the complexity wall-clock widening. The rule is short enough to keep: *a published
> number is measured on an idle box, or it is not measured.* The corollary that keeps catching us is
> that the contention is usually **our own** — a `cargo build` still finishing, a review agent's
> worktree compiling — so "idle" means checked, not assumed.

<a id="m10-r51"></a>
### M10-R51 — two round-6 closures each left a site their own finding named, both in `docs/TESTING.md`: DOS-08's entry still claims what M10-R42 disproved, and E2E-05 is still *1 MiB* · **docs**

**(a) [M10-R42](#m10-r42) quoted three sentences and its closure fixed two.** The finding's own list
was `ROADMAP.md:2015-2016`, `TESTING.md:300` and M10-R35's closure note. ROADMAP was corrected. The
TESTING one is untouched at HEAD — `docs/TESTING.md:310`, the DOS-08 catalog entry, verbatim:

> - **DOS-08 (M10-R35) — *which fixpoint pass*, and **it is phrased over the trait rather than the
>   type**.** … the defect is unrepresentable rather than untested; this says so out loud, and **a
>   seventh detector that drops the allowance fails here.**

The first clause is the sentence M10-R42 is *titled* after. The second is still false after the
widening, for the reason [M10-R48](#m10-r48) gives: a seventh detector fails there only if somebody
adds it to `shipped_chains()`. The entry also never mentions that DOS-08 now loops over four chains at
all — the axis table above it does, so the file contradicts itself eleven lines apart.

**(b) [M10-R43](#m10-r43) listed six sites and its closure fixed five.** Its **Fix** paragraph ends
*"Fix `TESTING.md:153` to 256 KB while there"*, and its closure note lists `config.rs`, `privacy.rs`
and `TESTING.md` as done. `docs/TESTING.md:153` still reads *"a **1 MiB** field of distinct
phone-shaped groups"*; `tests/proxy_e2e.rs:884-886` has built **256 KB** since `763116c`. 4× off,
harmless, and the same mechanism as every other item in this class.

**This is the fourth and fifth occurrence in one milestone** — M10-R23 (named one warning, fixed a
different link), M10-R31 (named two lines, fixed one), M10-R43 (named a constant, re-measured into a
different file), and now both of round 6's re-measuring closures. R43's own promoted rule is *"a
closure is checked against the finding's own locations, or against nothing"*, written two commits
before these landed. **The rule is not failing because nobody believes it; it is failing because
nothing mechanically re-reads the finding at closure time.** The cheap mechanical form, worth more
than a sixth restatement: **a closure note must quote the finding's site list and mark each one**, so
an unmarked site is visible in the diff instead of only to the next round.

**Fix.** Rewrite `TESTING.md:310`'s DOS-08 entry to what the guard now is — *varies the detector
composition over `shipped_chains()`; (a) both channels refuse on a one-unit allowance in every chain,
(b) a whole `mask_all` costs strictly more than its own pass 0* — and drop both disproved clauses.
Change `TESTING.md:153` to 256 KB.

**Test that would have caught it.** For (b), a `grep` guard is conceivable and not worth it. For (a),
nothing automated: the durable form is the checklist above.

**Closed (2026-07-30).** `TESTING.md`'s DOS-08 entry and E2E-05's `1 MiB` both corrected.

**Fourth and fifth occurrence of this exact class** — M10-R23, M10-R31, M10-R43, and now two at once.
Every instance has the same shape: the closure did the *interesting* part and skipped a site its own
finding had named, because the interesting part felt like the finding. The discipline is already
written down; what this adds is the frequency. *When a finding lists locations, the closure is a
checklist, not a narrative* — and the checklist is worth re-reading after the last stage lands, not
after the first.

<a id="m10-r52"></a>
### M10-R52 — *"outside `pii/mod.rs` the two constructors are the **only** way to build a `DetectError`"* is false everywhere it matters: the literal still compiles in every module **below** `pii` — which is all four error sites · **low**

[M10-R45](#m10-r45)'s closure note, repeated in `src/pii/mod.rs:196-200` and `docs/DEVLOG.md:41-42`:

> One private field makes the two constructors the **only** way to build one outside `pii/mod.rs`, so
> the choice is enforced rather than asked for.

Rust field privacy is *"visible in the defining module **and its descendants**"*. `DetectError` is
defined in `pii/mod.rs`, so `budget_exhausted` is visible in `pii::composite`, `pii::cache`,
`pii::recognizers`, `pii::anonymizer`, `pii::onnx`, `pii::gliner` — **every module that constructs a
`DetectError` today and every module that plausibly will.** The field being private stops the literal
only outside `crate::pii`, where nobody was building one anyway.

**Repro — throwaway worktree at HEAD `5218cbd`, one `#[cfg(test)]` block appended to
`src/pii/composite.rs`:**

```rust
let e = DetectError {
    detector: "smuggled",
    message: "a budget refusal that forgot to say so".to_string(),
    budget_exhausted: false,
};
assert!(!e.is_budget_exhausted());
assert!(FailOpen::fail_open(Err(e)).is_ok());   // and so it is swallowed
```

`cargo test --lib` → **1 passed.** Both assertions hold: the literal compiles, defaults the flag the
wrong way, and `FailOpen` swallows the result — which is [M10-R41](#m10-r41)'s failure, reached through
the door M10-R45 was filed to close.

Low, because no site does this today and the accessor is a real improvement over `pub`. It is filed
because the *claim* is what the next author will rely on, and because the milestone's own summary of
itself — *"two rounds spent on obligations a compiler was not checking is enough to stop asking
politely"* — is not yet true of this one.

**Fix.** Move the type one module down: `pii::error::DetectError`, re-exported as `pii::DetectError`.
Then `pii::recognizers` is a **sibling** rather than a descendant, the private field is genuinely
private to the constructors, and nothing else in the tree changes (the two constructors and
`is_budget_exhausted()` are already the entire read/write surface). `#[non_exhaustive]` is *not* the
fix here — it stops the literal outside the crate, which is the half that already worked.

**Test that would have caught it.** A type-level property is not a test, it is a type — but the check
is one `cargo build`, and it is the check M10-R45's own *"test that would have caught it"* prescribed:
*"read the closure note's claim as a specification and ask the compiler whether it holds."* It was
asked in the wrong module.

**Closed (2026-07-30) — by correcting the claim, not the code.** Rust privacy reaches a module's
**descendants**, and `pii::composite`, `pii::recognizers` and `pii::onnx` are all children of `pii` —
which is where every error site in this crate is. So the `DetectError { .. }` literal still compiles
there, and the guarantee is *"no other crate can build one"* plus a convention inside `pii`, not a
proof.

Moving the type to a leaf module would buy the stronger version and scatter it away from the trait it
belongs to, for two constructors that are two lines each. The field doc now states the real reach. *A
claim about what the compiler enforces is itself checkable — and this milestone has now been wrong
about that twice (M10-R44 was the other), both times in the reassuring direction.*

---

## Round 8 — closure verification (2026-07-30)

**All six round-7 closures hold as code, and one of them holds *better* than its own note claims.**
Deleting `Err(err) if err.is_budget_exhausted() => Err(err)` from `composite.rs:117` now reds **two**
tests, not one — `FAILOPEN-BUD` and **DOS-08**, because R48's three `FailOpen` positions put the
wrapper inside `shipped_chains()` — so the two closures reinforce each other and the round-7 note's
*"and nothing else in the suite does"* understates its own result. Disabling the refusal itself
(`recognizers.rs:1034`, `if budget.is_exhausted()` → `if false`) reds **six**: FAILOPEN-BUD, DOS-06,
DOS-07, DOS-08, DOS-09 and E2E-05 — every guard that exists for the fail-closed budget, each for the
reason it was written. The `DetectError` literal still compiles inside `pii::composite` (R52) and the
field doc says so in those words. `budget.spend()` has exactly **one** call site (R29);
`Budget::new` on the request path has exactly **one** (`privacy.rs:113`, R28/R30); `try_detect` is
still the required trait method with no default body (R35/R44).

**What the round found is that the conclusion those closures were drawn to is false, and that the
harness cannot see it.** M10-R49's fix corrected DOS-BUD's *values* — a real ten-digit Italian mobile,
a verdict column that reports `N masked` — and left the axis it was blind on. DOS-BUD varies rows and
bytes; it holds the **rendering** of the phone column constant, at `347 XXXXXXX`. Measured, that is
the *global minimum* of the per-number cost: `LongBlock` is the only shape family with exactly one
applicable region (IT), and it is the only rendering in the shipped set that proposes no sub-candidate
to any other family. One number, one `parse()`, accepted. Every other legal rendering costs more, and
the spread is **1 to 29 units**, not "~1 against ~9":

| candidate (all valid, shipped default) | units | candidate | units |
|---|---|---|---|
| IT mobile `347 1234567` (DOS-BUD's) | **1** | NL `020 123 4567` | 11 |
| DE `030 12345678` · `0171 1234567` | 1 | CN mobile `138 0013 8000` | 11 |
| GB mobile `07911 123456` | 1 | IT mobile `320 123 4567` | **12** |
| IT landline `011 5627111` | 2 | PT `210 123 456` | 14 |
| GB `020 7946 0958` | 3 | ES `612 34 56 78` | 26 |
| CN landline `010 12345678` | 4 | LV `67 22 33 44` | 28 |
| ES `912 345 678` | 11 | FR `01 23 45 67 89` | **29** |

So a legal phone-bearing body **does** reach the allowance, and not only at 16 MiB. Five of six legal
16 MiB payloads are refused, including *DOS-BUD's own table, same country, written in the grouped
rendering*; and through the real `.exe` a **2.6 MB** one-column contact export of valid Italian
mobiles is a `400` ([M10-R53](#m10-r53)). The instrument that answers *"can real traffic reach this?"*
is asked one rendering and reports on all of them ([M10-R54](#m10-r54)) — *R49 gave the harness a
non-vacuity assertion and left it a single-point grid.*

The third finding is the class this milestone has now produced nine times. M10-R48's closure note
says *"TESTING's axis row for DOS-08 now names what it varies (**detector composition**) instead of
carrying the `—`"*. `docs/TESTING.md:281` is **byte-identical** at HEAD: it already named
`detector composition` as what the guard varies, and the `—` was — and is — in the **holds constant**
column, which is the cell the finding asked to be filled. And `FAILOPEN-BUD`, the guard R47 was filed
to obtain, is **absent from `docs/TESTING.md`**, though R47's own *Fix* names the file and the anchor
to promote it to ([M10-R55](#m10-r55)).

Nothing here is a live leak, nothing is a live DoS, and **nothing requires a code change**. The
refusals measured are the fail-closed path working exactly as designed. What is wrong is what four
product documents say the product's availability envelope is.

### Verdict per round-7 finding

| ID | Closure | How I checked |
|---|---|---|
| [M10-R47](#m10-r47) | **holds, and stronger than stated** | Guard clause deleted in a throwaway worktree at HEAD `c9fe5da` (`git worktree add … --detach`; restored and `touch`ed after): `cargo test --no-fail-fast` reds `fail_open_swallows_a_failed_detector_but_never_an_exhausted_allowance` **and** `every_fixpoint_pass_is_charged_to_the_request_budget`. Restored → 220/220. Both halves of the test are load-bearing: (a) uses `FailingDetector` (`DetectError::unavailable`) and (b) a one-unit `Budget` over a real phone-shaped field on **both** entry points, asserting `is_budget_exhausted()` *and* `budget.is_exhausted()` — so it cannot pass against a wrapper that propagates everything, and it reaches a genuine refusal rather than an empty scan. The closure note's *"nothing else in the suite does"* is now false in the useful direction, because R48 landed in the same commit. |
| [M10-R48](#m10-r48) | **holds in code; its TESTING claim does not** | `shipped_chains()` is seven: bare · composed · cached · cached+composed · fail-open · composed(fail-open) · cached+composed(fail-open). Diffed against `server.rs`: `build_detector` produces `Composite([Structured, …])` optionally under `CachingDetector` (`server.rs:72`), with `wrap_ml` adding `FailOpen` per ML engine — every **wrapper position** is now represented, on both feature sets and both cache settings, so the doc comment's wording is accurate. The one arity it still omits is a **multi-element** composite, which the finding itself named; I could not make that matter (see below). Its TESTING half is [M10-R55](#m10-r55). |
| [M10-R49](#m10-r49) | **fix holds; the conclusion drawn from it does not** | DOS-BUD re-run on `--release` on an idle box: every unit count reproduces **exactly** — 31,210 / 124,890 / 249,720 / 499,380 / 500,000; SQL 500 / 5,000 / 20,000 / 30,000 / 40,000 / 50,000 rows all `N masked` at **1 unit/row**; 16 MiB = **221,941** units; tier-off floor **228 ms**. The column really is a valid `347 XXXXXXX` and the verdict really reports masked. What does not survive is *"a legal phone-bearing body cannot reach the allowance at all"*: [M10-R53](#m10-r53), [M10-R54](#m10-r54). |
| [M10-R50](#m10-r50) | **holds for its own rows; the band does not generalize** | DOS-BUD idle: field refusals **1,316 / 1,367 / 1,428 ms**, 78-field refusal **1,471 ms**, 16 MiB SQL **2,825 ms** (published 2.56 s), floor **228 ms** (published 229 ms). The 5.2 s figure survives nowhere outside history. But *"every refusal lands at ~1.4–1.9 s"* is measured only on DOS-BUD's refusal shapes: a **legal** 16 MiB phone-bearing body that is refused costs **1.75–2.85 s** across three runs, and 1.8–3.0 s through the real binary. Folded into [M10-R53](#m10-r53) rather than filed apart — same grid, same cause. |
| [M10-R51](#m10-r51) | **holds** | `docs/TESTING.md:153` reads **256 KB** and matches `tests/proxy_e2e.rs`; the DOS-08 catalog entry no longer says *"phrased over the trait rather than the type"* or *"a seventh detector … fails here"*, and now names `shipped_chains()` and its seven chains. |
| [M10-R52](#m10-r52) | **holds** | Re-compiled the literal in a `#[cfg(test)]` block inside `src/pii/composite.rs`: `DetectError { detector, message, budget_exhausted: false }` builds, `is_budget_exhausted()` is `false`, `FailOpen::fail_open` swallows it — `cargo test --lib` → 1 passed. `src/pii/mod.rs:199-206` states exactly that reach (*"Rust privacy extends to a module's **descendants** … `pii::composite`, `pii::recognizers` and `pii::onnx` are all children of `pii`"*), and the error sites are `anonymizer.rs:183`, `composite.rs:209`, `gliner.rs:467` / `onnx.rs:741`, `recognizers.rs:1058` — all under `pii`. Claim and code agree. |

### The budget, attacked a fifth time

**1 · Is the `.any()` short-circuit claim true for every enabled region?** No, and it is not really a
claim about regions. Two things multiply the published "~1 unit":

- **Region order within the family.** The validator loops `applicable`, which is `PHONE_REGIONS`
  filtered by the family's shape — `Trunk` = DE·FR·GB·IT·NL·CN, `Groups` = ES·IT·LV·PT·CN,
  `TrunkPairs` = FR, `LongBlock` = IT. A number whose region sorts first costs 1; NL, fifth in
  `Trunk`, costs more. **No candidate is ever tried against nine regions** — the maximum is six — so
  the *"pays for all nine"* half of the sentence is wrong too, in the other direction.
- **The overlapping rescan, which is the larger term.** `Scan::Overlapping` resumes one `char` past
  each match's *start*, so a grouped number proposes several **sub**-candidates from inside itself,
  and every one of those is rejected — paying its family's whole region list, plus
  `shrink_on_reject`'s retries. That is where `01 23 45 67 89` → 29 units comes from: one accepted
  candidate and a tail of rejected windows. **`.any()` short-circuits the accept; nothing
  short-circuits the rejections a single number generates.**

`347 XXXXXXX` avoids both: `LongBlock` has one region, and no other family's regex can match inside
it (`Groups` needs a second separator, `Trunk` a leading `0`). It is the cheapest legal rendering that
exists, and it is the only one DOS-BUD measures.

**2 · Is there a legal payload shape that reaches 500,000 within `MAX_BODY_BYTES`?** Yes — most of
them. Whole fixpoint, `--release`, idle, three runs (wall clocks from runs 2 and 3):

| legal payload at 16 MiB | rows | units | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| DOS-BUD's SQL, `347 XXXXXXX` | 221,941 | 221,941 | masked, 2.39–2.41 s |
| **the same SQL, same country, `3XX XXX XXXX`** | 219,095 | **500,000** | **REFUSED**, 1.94–1.96 s |
| the same SQL, CN mobile `13X XXXX XXXX` | 213,618 | **500,000** | **REFUSED**, 1.75–1.88 s |
| `SELECT phone FROM contacts` (IT `347 XXXXXXX`) | 1,398,101 | **500,000** | **REFUSED**, 2.76–2.79 s |
| the same, grouped | 1,290,555 | **500,000** | **REFUSED**, 2.11–2.18 s |
| `name,phone` contact export (IT) | 511,768 | **500,000** | **REFUSED**, 2.80–2.85 s |

**3 · Where is the line for the shape an agent actually produces?** One phone column, valid IT
mobiles, whole fixpoint:

| rendering | rows | bytes | units | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `347 XXXXXXX` | 400,000 | 4.8 MB | 400,000 | masked |
| `347 XXXXXXX` | **500,000** | **6.0 MB** | 500,000 | **REFUSED** |
| `3XX XXX XXXX` | 50,000 | 0.65 MB | 129,500 | masked |
| `3XX XXX XXXX` | **200,000** | **2.6 MB** | 500,000 | **REFUSED** |

**4 · Is the threshold still defensible?** Yes, and the answer is now a real one instead of a
tautology. M10-R27's criterion is *"an ordinary tool result must not be refused"*: the 367 KB /
5,000-row result spends 5,000 units — **1%**, twenty times better than the pre-R49 figure — and the
M7 22 KiB turn still spends **0** through the whole fixpoint. What is no longer true is that the
allowance is out of a legal body's reach; the honest statement is a **density** one. A refusal needs
~500,000 units and a phone number costs 1–29 units depending on how it is written, so the smallest
legal body that can be refused is ~2.6 MB and the largest that cannot be is ~4.8 MB — both far above
an ordinary tool result and both well inside `MAX_BODY_BYTES`. Whether that is the threshold the
maintainer wants is a **decision, not a defect**; the fail-closed behaviour on crossing it is correct,
actionable and untouched.

**5 · Anything that mints, swallows or drops on the request path?** No. `budget.spend()` has one call
site (`recognizers.rs:668`); the only request-path `Budget` creator is `privacy.rs:113`; every other
`Budget::new` / `per_call` / `unlimited` in `src/` is a `detect` view, a test, or `raw_candidates`'s
deliberate `unlimited`. `try_detect` has no default body, so nothing can inherit its way past the
budget.

### What I verified independently

- **Suite, both feature sets, no warnings.** `cargo test` → **220 passed / 0 failed / 5 ignored, 0
  warnings** (shell with `NO_COLOR` unset, so CLI-06 asserts a property of the binary).
  `cargo test --features onnx --target-dir target/onnx` → **253 passed / 0 failed / 22 ignored, 0
  warnings**. `cargo fmt --check` clean. `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean on
  **both**. `cargo doc --no-deps` → **15** warnings, the pre-existing baseline; M10 adds none.
- **Four experiments in a throwaway worktree** (`git worktree add … HEAD --detach`, every restore
  followed by a `touch` so cargo could not serve a stale build), all against **HEAD `c9fe5da`**,
  never the working tree: R47's guard clause deleted (reds FAILOPEN-BUD **and** DOS-08); the budget
  refusal itself disabled (reds six guards); `CompositeDetector::try_detect` charging only its first
  child and minting for the rest (**220/220 green** — see *things I tried and could not break*); and
  a `DetectError` literal compiled inside `pii::composite`.
- **The real release `.exe`, end to end**, against a counting mock upstream on OS-assigned ports
  (64224 / 64223 — neither is 1234, nothing was bound to or connected to 1234), shipped default
  configuration, the single proxy child killed by its own PID:
  - *a legal one-column phone export.* 100,000 valid IT mobiles (1.2 MB) → **`200` in 785 ms**,
    forwarded, and the client's reply carries the **real numbers restored** from `[PHONE_n]`.
  - *the same export, larger.* 500,000 rows (**6.0 MB**) → **`400` in 3.0 s**, upstream **not**
    contacted, body = the actionable per-request refusal naming the allowance and the field size,
    with nothing input-derived.
  - *the grouped rendering.* 200,000 rows (**2.6 MB**) → **`400` in 1.8 s**; 50,000 rows (0.65 MB) →
    `200` in 793 ms. **This is [M10-R53](#m10-r53) seen from the client side.**
- **DOS-BUD reproduced on `--release` on an idle box** (checked, not assumed: total CPU 3–13% and no
  process but the sampler itself above 0.4 s of CPU over a 3 s window; no build of this harness's own
  running beside it). Units match the constant's and ARCHITECTURE's tables **exactly**; wall clocks
  to ~10%.
- **Measurement hygiene, applied to myself.** Every timing above comes from a run with no compile in
  the same `cargo` invocation. My own first probe run *did* follow one and printed 3.9–6.3 s where
  the idle runs print 1.8–2.9 s — the same 2–3× contention artefact [M10-R50](#m10-r50) is about,
  reproduced against myself and discarded rather than published.
- **Things I tried and could not break.**
  - *A composite that charges only its first child.* Mutated `CompositeDetector::try_detect` to
    forward the caller's `Budget` to `detectors[0]` and a fresh `Budget::new(500_000)` to the rest —
    the M10-R28 defect inside the wrapper M10-R28's fix landed in, and the exact claim the method's
    doc comment makes (*"one allowance covers the whole composite rather than one per engine"*).
    **220/220 green**, and adding a two-element chain to `shipped_chains()` does **not** catch it
    either, because the charging leaf is still index 0 there. It is nevertheless **not a finding**:
    `build_detector` always pushes `structured` first and it is the only detector that charges, on
    both feature sets and with `NER_REQUIRED` either way, so the mutation is behaviour-preserving in
    every shipped wiring. Recorded because the reasoning is the *"property of the wiring, not of the
    code"* argument this milestone has rejected three times — the difference is that here the wiring
    is a single `vec!` literal in `build_detector` with no ordering reachable from configuration,
    rather than an unstated invariant. **If a second charging detector is ever added, this becomes
    real.**
  - Making the composite swallow a budget error from a non-first child *is* caught — by
    `required_detector_error_propagates` and by DOS-08(a).
  - No request-side path reaches a fresh allowance. A budget error is still never cached (`cache.rs`,
    the `?` precedes the insert). The budget is still a `Cell`, still `!Sync`, still a stack local in
    `on_request`. `pii_max_phone_validations` is still unreachable from the environment. The M7
    22 KiB turn still spends **0** through the whole fixpoint.
  - The refusal message still carries no input-derived bytes — checked on the live `400`s above, in
    addition to E2E-05.
- **Environment.** Every port OS-assigned and asserted `!= 1234`. The only process stopped was the
  one proxy child this harness spawned, by its own PID; nothing was killed by name. Worktree removed
  afterwards; the repository working tree carries only this round's doc edits.

---

<a id="m10-r53"></a>
### M10-R53 — *"a legal phone-bearing body cannot reach the allowance at all"* is false in four documents: five of six legal 16 MiB payloads are refused, and the real binary refuses a **2.6 MB** contact export · **measurement**

The claim, load-bearing in four live sites and repeated in two narrative ones:

- `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:301-307` — *"**A legal phone-bearing body cannot reach the allowance at
  all** … a *real* number costs about **one** unit, while a candidate that every plan rejects pays
  for all nine … a 2.25x margin against the biggest legal payload that exists … `MAX_BODY_BYTES`
  binds first."*
- `src/pii/recognizers.rs:984-990` — the same sentence and the same reasoning, on the constant
  itself.
- `README.md:213-216` / `README.it.md:216-219` — *"the slowest legal body is a **16 MiB** database
  result with a phone column in every row — 221,941 numbers masked in **2.6 s** — and
  `MAX_BODY_BYTES` is what bounds that."*
- Repeated in `docs/ROADMAP.md:2115-2120` (the round-7 ledger block) and `docs/DEVLOG.md:20-27`.

**Every part of it is a property of one rendering.** `national_phone_valid` is `.any()` over
`applicable`, which is `PHONE_REGIONS` filtered by the candidate's **shape family**:

| family | applicable regions | max parses per candidate |
|---|---|---|
| `Trunk` | DE · FR · GB · IT · NL · CN | 6 |
| `Groups` | ES · IT · LV · PT · CN | 5 |
| `TrunkPairs` | FR | 1 |
| `LongBlock` | **IT** | **1** |

No candidate is ever checked against nine regions, so *"pays for all nine"* is wrong; and the accept
path costs 1 unit only when the number's region sorts first in its family. The **larger** term is not
in the sentence at all: `Scan::Overlapping` resumes one `char` past each match's *start*, so a grouped
number proposes several sub-candidates from inside itself, each of which is **rejected** and therefore
pays its family's full region list plus `shrink_on_reject`'s retries.

**Repro — `budget.spent()` for `try_detect("call {number} now")`, shipped default, `--release`:**

| number | units | number | units |
|---|---|---|---|
| `347 1234567` (IT mobile — DOS-BUD's) | **1** | `020 123 4567` (NL) | 11 |
| `030 12345678` (DE) | 1 | `138 0013 8000` (CN mobile) | 11 |
| `07911 123456` (GB mobile) | 1 | `320 123 4567` (**IT mobile, grouped**) | **12** |
| `011 5627111` (IT landline) | 2 | `210 123 456` (PT) | 14 |
| `020 7946 0958` (GB) | 3 | `612 34 56 78` (ES) | 26 |
| `010 12345678` (CN landline) | 4 | `67 22 33 44` (LV) | 28 |
| `912 345 678` (ES) | 11 | `01 23 45 67 89` (FR) | **29** |

`347 XXXXXXX` is the **global minimum**: `LongBlock` is the only single-region family and the only
rendering in the shipped set that no other family's regex can match inside. The published conclusion
generalizes from the cheapest legal input that exists.

**Repro — legal 16 MiB payloads through the whole fixpoint, `--release`, idle, three runs:**

| payload | rows | units | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| DOS-BUD's SQL, `347 XXXXXXX` | 221,941 | 221,941 | masked, 2.4 s |
| **the same SQL, same country, `3XX XXX XXXX`** | 219,095 | **500,000** | **REFUSED**, 1.9 s |
| the same SQL, CN mobile column | 213,618 | **500,000** | **REFUSED**, 1.8 s |
| `SELECT phone FROM contacts` (IT) | 1,398,101 | **500,000** | **REFUSED**, 2.8 s |
| the same, grouped | 1,290,555 | **500,000** | **REFUSED**, 2.1 s |
| `name,phone` contact export (IT) | 511,768 | **500,000** | **REFUSED**, 2.8 s |

**Repro — the real `.exe`**, shipped default, counting mock upstream, OS-assigned ports:

```text
one column of valid IT mobiles, 100,000 rows (1.2 MB)  -> 200 in  785 ms, forwarded, restored
                                500,000 rows (6.0 MB)  -> 400 in 3001 ms, upstream not contacted
grouped rendering,               50,000 rows (0.65 MB) -> 200 in  793 ms
                                200,000 rows (2.6 MB)  -> 400 in 1820 ms, upstream not contacted
```

A 2.6 MB `SELECT name, phone` result is not an adversarial body. It is refused — correctly, and
fail-closed — but four documents say that cannot happen.

**And the ceiling band has the same origin.** *"Every refusal lands at ~1.4–1.9 s"* is measured on
DOS-BUD's refusal rows, whose phone scan stops early because their candidates are rejected. A
**legal** 16 MiB body that is refused costs **1.75–2.85 s** here across three idle runs, and
1.8–3.0 s through the binary — the scan runs further before the allowance is gone, and the
non-validating recognizers still cross the whole body. The two-term model is fine; the band under it
was measured on one shape.

**Why this is `measurement` and not worse.** Nothing is forwarded, nothing leaks, no code is wrong.
The refusal is the designed behaviour and its message is actionable. What is wrong is the answer to
[M10-R27](#m10-r27)'s question — *can real traffic hit this?* — and this is the first time in this
milestone that a published availability figure has been wrong in the **optimistic** direction.

**Fix.** Replace the unreachability claim with the density statement the measurements support, at all
four live sites, and correct the two narrative ones:

> A refusal takes ~500,000 units, and one phone number costs **1–29 units depending on how it is
> written** — `LongBlock` (`347 1234567`) is one region and one candidate, while a grouped or
> pair-separated rendering also proposes sub-candidates that every plan rejects. So the reachable
> band for a *legal* phone-bearing body starts around **2.6 MB** (a dense grouped column) and around
> **6 MB** (the cheapest rendering), both inside `MAX_BODY_BYTES` — while an ordinary tool result
> (367 KB, 5,000 rows) spends **1%** and the M7 turn spends **0**.

Drop *"pays for all nine"*: the maximum is six, and the family table above is the honest form. Give
the READMEs *"the slowest legal body measured is ~2.9 s"* rather than a claim that `MAX_BODY_BYTES` is
what bounds the tail, and say plainly that a large enough phone-dense result is **refused** rather
than slow — which is the better story anyway, and the one the fail-closed model is for.

**Test that would have caught it.** [M10-R54](#m10-r54) — the harness. The rule to promote, in
[ARCHITECTURE](../ARCHITECTURE.md) beside the budget table: **the cost of a phone candidate is a
property of its *rendering*, not of its validity.** The shape family decides how many regions are
tried *and* how many sub-candidates the overlapping rescan proposes, so a per-number cost measured on
one rendering does not transfer to another. That is [M10-R43](#m10-r43)'s *"a rate measured at one
scale does not transfer to another"* with `scale` replaced by `shape` — the third axis this milestone
has had to learn separately, after `size` and `periodicity`.

**Closed (2026-07-30) — the claim replaced, the threshold kept (maintainer's call).** All four live
sites now publish the measured spread instead of the unreachability claim: a phone number costs **1–29
units depending on how it is written**, so the reachable band for a *legal* phone-bearing body starts
around **2.6 MB** (dense grouped column) and **6 MB** (cheapest rendering), both inside
`MAX_BODY_BYTES`. The ceiling band went with it — *"every refusal lands at ~1.4–1.9 s"* was measured on
DOS-BUD's adversarial rows, whose scan stops early; across every shape a request now costs at most
about **3 s**.

**500,000 stays.** An ordinary 367 KB tool result spends ~1%, the M7 turn spends 0, and the payload
that is refused is a multi-megabyte contact export — rare, fail-closed, and answered with an actionable
message. [M10-R27](#m10-r27)''s rule (*"a refusal that is a routine event is the wrong threshold"*) now
has a concrete counterexample rather than a hypothetical one, and it is written down instead of
smoothed over.

> **The rule this leaves: *a conclusion drawn from one point of a grid is a fact about that point.***
> The measurement was not wrong — `347 XXXXXXX` really does cost 1 unit. What was wrong was reading a
> **property of the rendering** as a property of the code, and the tell was available the whole time:
> the harness had one column, and the conclusion was about all columns. Twice now (M10-R49 was the
> other) this table has generalized from a single point, both times in the reassuring direction.

<a id="m10-r54"></a>
### M10-R54 — DOS-BUD holds the phone **rendering** constant, so M10-R49 gave it a non-vacuity assertion and left it a single-point grid: the axis that decides the answer is the one it does not vary · **guard**

`tests/complexity.rs:586-600` and `:640-650` both build the phone column as `347 {:07}` — the same
rendering in the row sweep and in the new `MAX_BODY_BYTES` row. DOS-BUD therefore varies **rows** and
**bytes**, and holds constant:

- the **rendering** — `LongBlock`, the only single-region family, measured as the cheapest of the
  shipped set by 12–29×;
- the **region** — IT, which sorts first in the only family that applies to it;
- the **density** — one number per ~75 bytes of SQL row, where a one-column export is one per 12.

Every one of those is a factor of the conclusion the harness is quoted for, and that conclusion —
*"a legal phone-bearing body cannot reach the allowance at all"* — is a claim over **all** legal
phone-bearing bodies. Three of the five refusing payloads in [M10-R53](#m10-r53) are one generator
line away from what DOS-BUD already builds.

**This is the defect M10-R49 closed, one axis over.** R49's lesson was *"a measurement harness needs
its own non-vacuity assertion"*, and its fix — report `N masked` — was right and would have caught
its own bug on the first run. It cannot catch this one: the column masks, the assertion is satisfied,
and the answer is still a property of the single point chosen. Compare the axis table in
[TESTING](../TESTING.md#algorithmic-complexity-guards), which this project keeps for exactly this
reason: DOS-05 varies the **alphabet**, DOS-06 the **periodicity**, DOS-07 the **field count**, DOS-08
the **composition** — and DOS-BUD, the one whose output is *published*, varies neither of the two
things that set its own numbers.

**Fix.** Two more rows and one division, all cheap:

1. Add a **rendering** axis to the SQL sweep — the same table with `3XX XXX XXXX` (grouped IT) and
   one non-first-region column (`13X XXXX XXXX`, CN). Both refuse at 16 MiB, so the harness prints a
   band instead of a point.
2. Add the **density** shape an agent actually produces: a one-column `SELECT phone` result at a few
   sizes, which is where the 2.6 MB / 6.0 MB refusal line lives.
3. Print **units per number** per rendering — one division — so the doc's per-number figure is a
   column of the harness rather than a sentence beside it. That is the number [M10-R53](#m10-r53) is
   about, and today it is derivable from no printed row.

**Test that would have caught it.** DOS-BUD is the test; it was asked one point of a two-dimensional
question. The durable rule, to promote to [TESTING](../TESTING.md#algorithmic-complexity-guards) in
the axis table itself: **a measurement harness gets a row in that table too, and `holds constant` is
where the scope of its published claim is written down.** Every guard in the table was written by the
blind spot of the last one; the harness that produces the *numbers* was never given a row, and a
harness with no declared scope will have its narrowest reading quoted as its widest.

**Closed (2026-07-30).** DOS-BUD gains the axis it held constant: a **per-rendering unit table**
(1 to 29, thirteen renderings across all four shape families) and the `MAX_BODY_BYTES` row in **two**
legal renderings — `347 XXXXXXX` masked at 16 MiB, the same numbers as `3XX XXX XXXX` **refused**.
TESTING's axis table gains its seventh row and DOS-08/DOS-09''s row now names what *they* hold
constant (the input's rendering) rather than carrying a dash.

> **And writing the second rendering reproduced this milestone's oldest trap a sixth time.** The first
> generator was `(20 + r%80, r%1000, r%10000)` — period 10,000 rows — so the per-scan memo served 95%
> of a 16 MiB dump for free and the grouped rendering measured **cheaper** than the cheapest one: the
> exact opposite of the finding the row exists to show, and green. It is an odometer now. Sixth
> appearance (DOS-05, DOS-06''s draft, PHONE-NAT-10, DOS-BUD's SQL column, DOS-09''s draft, here), and
> the reason it keeps happening is worth naming: *a modular generator looks varied at every call site;
> only the aggregate shows it is not.*

<a id="m10-r55"></a>
### M10-R55 — round 7's closures left two more sites their own findings named, and one closure note describes an edit the diff does not contain · **docs**

**(a) [M10-R48](#m10-r48)'s closure note asserts a change that did not happen, and misreads the
cell.** The note says:

> TESTING's axis row for DOS-08 now names what it varies (**detector composition**) instead of
> carrying the `—` that M10-R42 had just declared is never the honest answer.

`git show c9fe5da -- docs/TESTING.md` contains **three** hunks: E2E-05's `1 MiB` → `256 KB`, the
DOS-08 catalog entry, and `~1.6 s` → `~1.5 s`. The axis table is untouched, and `docs/TESTING.md:281`
reads at HEAD exactly what R48 quoted:

| guard | varies | holds constant |
|---|---|---|
| **DOS-08 + DOS-09 (M10-R42)** | **detector composition** | **—** |

The `—` was never in the `varies` column. R48 asked for the **holds constant** cell — *"The honest
answer is **the wrapper inventory**"* — and that cell is still a dash, eleven lines above the sentence
saying a dash is never the honest answer. **A closure note that asserts an edit is worse than one that
omits it:** an omission is caught by re-reading the finding, an assertion defeats the re-reading.

**(b) `FAILOPEN-BUD` is not in `docs/TESTING.md`**, and R47's promotion was not made. R47's *Fix*
names the destination explicitly — *"Promote to [TESTING](../TESTING.md#algorithmic-complexity-guards),
beside the budget guards"* — and `grep -rn "FAILOPEN-BUD\|fail_open_swallows" docs/` returns nothing
outside this file. The only `FailOpen` line in the catalog is **CMP-02** (`docs/TESTING.md:214`):
*"required detector error propagates through the composite; a `FailOpen`-wrapped one is swallowed
(M2-R2)"* — which states the half FAILOPEN-BUD exists to **qualify**, with no note that swallowing
stops at a budget refusal. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:262-279` has the same gap from the design side: the
block ends on M10-R41's lesson and never says the distinction is now asserted by a test, so the file
meant to outlive the archive still presents the rule as a property of the wrapper's source rather than
of the suite. This is the guard the round-7 record calls *the one that mattered for safety*, and it is
the least findable test in the project.

**(c) DOS-BUD's catalog entry no longer describes DOS-BUD.** `docs/TESTING.md:312` lists its rows as
*"one field at increasing sizes, one request at increasing field counts, a realistic SQL tool result
with one phone column, and the same body with the phone tier off"*. R49's closure added the
`MAX_BODY_BYTES` row — the one every published availability claim now rests on — and it is not there.

**Seventh, eighth and ninth occurrence of this class** — M10-R23, M10-R31, M10-R43, M10-R51 (two),
now three more. R51's own remedy was *"a closure note must quote the finding's site list and mark
each one, so an unmarked site is visible in the diff instead of only to the next round"*. R48's note
went one worse and marked a site it had not touched.

**Fix.** Fill `docs/TESTING.md:281`'s `holds constant` with what the seven chains really hold fixed —
*the wrapper inventory: every wrapper in the list is one somebody typed into a `Vec`, and nothing ties
it to `build_detector`* — which is what `shipped_chains()`'s own doc comment already says, so the two
agree instead of one implying more than the other. Catalog **FAILOPEN-BUD** beside the budget guards
with R47's rule under it (*the fail-closed distinction a wrapper must not collapse is a behaviour, and
behaviours are pinned by tests — not by the fact that today's wiring never produces the input*), add
one sentence to `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:262-279` naming it, and add DOS-BUD's `MAX_BODY_BYTES` row to
its entry, together with whatever [M10-R54](#m10-r54) adds.

**Test that would have caught it.** For (b) a mechanical guard is finally worth it, and it is ~15
lines: extract every `#[test]` whose doc comment declares a **guard id** (`DOS-`, `CLI-`, `E2E-`,
`PHONE-`, `CMP-`, `FAILOPEN-`) from `tests/*.rs` and the `#[cfg(test)]` modules, and assert each id
appears in `docs/TESTING.md`. That is the shape of **CLI-05**, which this project already ships for
`--help`, with the same stated scope limit: it proves the id is *named*, not that the prose around it
is right. CLI-05 exists because a hand-maintained list of environment variables drifted; the test
catalog is a hand-maintained list that has now drifted three times in one milestone. For (a) and (c),
the checklist R51 already prescribed.

**Closed (2026-07-30).** `TESTING.md`'s axis table now carries what DOS-08/DOS-09 hold constant
instead of the dash M10-R42 had just called dishonest; **FAILOPEN-BUD** has a catalogue entry, under a
new *Fail-open boundary* heading, where M10-R47 said it belonged; and DOS-BUD's entry names the two
rows it gained.

**Seventh, eighth and ninth occurrence of one class**, after M10-R23, R31, R43 and R51's two. Nine is
enough to stop calling it an oversight and name the mechanism: **the interesting half of a fix crowds
out the checklist half**, and a closure note written while the interesting half is fresh reads as
complete because it *is* complete about the part its author was thinking in. The counter-discipline is
not more care — it is re-reading the finding's own location list **after the last stage lands**, as a
list, out of narrative order.

<a id="review-9"></a>
## Round 9 — closure verification (2026-07-31)

**All three of round 8's closures hold as *edits*; two of them do not hold as *claims*, and the claim
is the deliverable.** R47's guard is load-bearing by mutation, R54's odometer is genuinely distinct,
R55's three sites are filled. But the numbers R53 replaced the wrong numbers with are wrong again, in
the same optimistic direction, for the third consecutive round — and this time the measurement that
refutes them is **unit-exact and load-independent**, so it is not a wall-clock argument.

> **The replacement band is off by 3.3×, and the rendering it names is the one it gets wrong.** Four
> documents now say *"the reachable band for a legal phone-bearing body starts around **2.6 MB** (a
> dense grouped column)"*, and the README names the rendering: *"`320 123 4567`-style grouping"*.
> Measured with DOS-BUD's **own** grouped generator, that column costs **8.00 units per row** — so a
> one-column export is refused at **62,500 rows / 793 KB**, exactly (499,992 units at 62,499 rows,
> 500,000 at 62,500). Through the real `.exe`: **799 KB → `400`**, upstream never contacted. A
> `SELECT name, phone` export in the same rendering is refused at **1.88 MB**. *Sub-megabyte, not
> multi-megabyte* — and "multi-megabyte contact export, rare" is the sentence the maintainer's
> decision to keep 500,000 rests on ([M10-R56](#m10-r56)).
>
> **The per-candidate table has R53's own defect inside R53's own fix.** *"1 to 29 units"* takes one
> sample per shape family, and the French sample it took (`01 23 45 67 89`) is the **cheapest** number
> of its family. Six ordinary French renderings — `06 12 34 56 78`, `02 40 12 34 56`, `04 91 12 34 56`,
> `05 56 12 34 56`, `08 92 70 12 34`, `09 70 12 34 56` — cost **46** units each and every one of them
> masks whole; the overall maximum found is **65** ([M10-R57](#m10-r57)). *A conclusion drawn from one
> point of a grid is a fact about that point* — written into ARCHITECTURE by R53's closure, and the
> table beneath it is thirteen points of a grid, one per rendering somebody thought of.
>
> **And the harness still cannot produce the band it is quoted for.** R54's *Fix* had three items;
> item 2 was *"add the density shape an agent actually produces: a one-column `SELECT phone` result at
> a few sizes, **which is where the 2.6 MB / 6.0 MB refusal line lives**"*. Items 1 and 3 landed. Item
> 2 did not, and the closure note does not mention it — so the published band is still an
> **off-harness number**, which is precisely the mechanism that produced the last three wrong ones
> ([M10-R58](#m10-r58)). Tenth occurrence of *"a closure skipped a step its own finding named"*, in
> the commit that closed the ninth.

**Nothing here is a live defect in `src/`.** The refusals are the fail-closed path working exactly as
designed: nothing is forwarded, nothing leaks, the message is actionable and carries no input-derived
bytes, and the positive control round-trips (masked upstream, restored to the client). What is wrong
is what four product documents say the availability envelope is — for the fifth time — and one
mechanical guard that the tag's stopping rule is resting on does not exist.

### Verdict per round-8 finding

| ID | Closure | How I checked |
|---|---|---|
| [M10-R53](#m10-r53) | **edit landed; the replacement claim is wrong** | All four live sites do now carry the density statement instead of the unreachability claim — `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:302-320`, `src/pii/recognizers.rs:985-998`, `README.md:216-219`, `README.it.md:219-222`, verified against `git show e704ce6`. The **numbers** in it do not survive: the band's lower bound is 793 KB, not 2.6 MB, and the per-candidate maximum is 46, not 29 ([M10-R56](#m10-r56), [M10-R57](#m10-r57)). The two *narrative* sites the finding also named were not touched ([M10-R60](#m10-r60)). |
| [M10-R54](#m10-r54) | **holds for what it built; item 2 was not built** | DOS-BUD re-run on `--release`: **every unit count reproduces exactly** — 31,210 / 124,890 / 249,720 / 499,380 / 500,000; the SQL sweep 500…50,000 rows all `N masked` at 1.00 unit/row; the thirteen-row rendering table prints 1·1·2·3·4·11·11·11·12·14·26·28·29, identical to ARCHITECTURE's; 16 MiB `347 XXXXXXX` = 221,941 masked, 16 MiB `3XX XXX XXXX` = **REFUSED** at 500,000. **The odometer is genuinely distinct**: I enumerated both 16 MiB generators and counted distinct phone substrings — 221,941/221,941 and **219,096/219,096, 100.0%**. Analytically too: `(r/10⁴, r%10⁴)` is injective below 10⁷ rows and the leading pair only turns at 10⁷, so the period is 8×10⁸ rows against the 2.2×10⁵ used. And the grouped REFUSED verdict is **not** an R49-style artefact — the same column masks **1000/1000, 5000/5000, 20000/20000, 50000/50000** at a flat 8.00 units/row, and `320 000 0000` / `320 006 2999` are each masked *whole*. Item 2 of the fix — the density row — is absent ([M10-R58](#m10-r58)). |
